## Summary

Web Map & Transit is the MVP's world model and its crossing: one persisted block that records which hulls exist (the home-ship and two handmade derelicts), what the crew has seen and boarded of each and which are loaded, plus a small machine that carries the crew between the home-ship's pod console and a derelict's dock on a scripted, constant-velocity, no-input leg that costs fuel from the Graph's own tank, with one further verb, `berth`, that brings a boarded derelict alongside so a weld can be offered. Read it if you build or review anything that depends on what exists, what is loaded, where the pod is, the depth ring, or the hop's fuel, restore and attention rules; skip it for flight, steering, procedural generation, the chart's layout, boarding loops or any sound, which are owned elsewhere. It is a truth-channel system (no meter, no reveal, nothing the Perception Filter may wrap), every number in it is a [PLACEHOLDER] tuned at the kill/continue playtest, and its ask register is reconciled against the Designed neighbours (most asks accepted; the `pod_hop` lit exemption stays refused and is not needed, because the docked pod's own self-powered status glow lights its console, a user ruling; the berth-stimulus route is route 1, ruled by the user as D59, pushed by Interaction and not by this system) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*.

> **Quick reference** — Layer: `Feature` · Priority: `MVP` · Key deps: `Command & Simulation Layer` (designed), `Derelict Authoring (Handmade Kits)` (designed), `Save/Load & Versioned Format` (designed); soft: Life-Support Graph, Ship Body & Interior Physics, Ship Section & Layout Data Model, Interaction, Modular Ship Assembly, Attention, Suit & Equipment, Player Controller & Input, Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline & Camera (designed); Sanity & Hallucination Rules, Horror Entities (designed); downstream: Chart Table (designed), Boarding & Salvage (designed)

## Overview

Web Map & Transit is the MVP's **world model and its crossing**. As data it is one persisted simulation block (`web.state`, provisional name) that records which hulls exist in the web (the home-ship and, at MVP, two handmade derelicts), what the crew has seen and boarded of each, which of them are *loaded* into the simulation and which sleep as data, where the one podcraft is, and the state of a small four-state machine (`transit.step`, provisional name) that moves the crew between the home-ship's airlock and a derelict's dock. As an experience it is the **sortie's two doors joined by a dark**: the player stands at the pod console in a dock room, confirms a derelict, pays for the hop out of the same fuel tank that feeds the reactor, goes through a black hold into an exterior view of a small pod crossing the web on a scripted, constant-velocity leg (no steering, no input) with the target wreck resolving out of the dark and its one residual lamp as the only warm point, then cuts through black to a dock cell aboard the wreck, which is *exactly as the crew left it* (nothing regrew, nothing healed, nothing fast-forwarded). One further verb, `berth`, brings a **boarded** derelict hull-to-hull with the home-ship (the home-ship comes alongside; a dead hulk has no drive) so that Modular Ship Assembly may offer a weld. The system owns six facts and no others: the roster records, where the pod is (`pod_at`), the machine's state and epoch, the berth state, the leg and berth *data* (content files, not state), and the published ring reads. It owns no fuel store, no fuel meter, no spill, no map drawing, no loot, no sound and no steering; it calls published mutators of the Graph, Layout and Body inside one atomic apply and publishes one read view (`web.view`, provisional name) that the Chart Table (undesigned) only displays.

**How the player meets it.** Three beats, repeated about a handful of times in a 30-minute MVP session: (1) at the **chart table** the crew picks a target (a host-local UI choice that is never simulation state; the Chart Table owns the widget); (2) at the **pod console** in the dock room the press of the console is the commitment: the fuel leaves the tank, the screen goes to the black hold, and for a leg of `HOP_LEG_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] the player has no hands; (3) at the **dock** on the far side the player stands on an authored arrival cell, one tile clear of the docked-podcraft prop, and the work of boarding begins (Boarding & Salvage, designed, owns it). On the way home the same console carries the player back at no fuel cost (the round trip is prepaid). Once, after a derelict has been boarded, the player may `berth` it: the home-ship comes alongside, costing fuel again, and the weld becomes possible. There is no fuel gauge, ETA, progress ring or percentage anywhere in these beats (P1, P3): fuel is witnessed only by the Graph's own channels (the sight-glass, the drip, the puddle, the SCRAM), and the chart shows only what the crew lit.

**What the game loses without it.** Five neighbours are designed and waiting for an owner of three facts: *what exists*, *what is loaded* and *where the pod is*. The Layout `init_world` build order, Body's pod transfer (BM5) and velocity (BM6) mutators, Assembly's `docked` precondition for the weld offer, Attention's `ring_of` and Save/Load's world block each name this system as the provider and none can supply it themselves. Without it the home-ship has no door to a wreck, the derelicts have no unload rule (so every wreck would keep running, wearing and burning its lamp unseen), the weld has no way to be offered, the Attention baseline has no depth, and the sortie shape the whole game is built on (out, strip, home) has no middle. It is the cheapest system that connects the two ends of the sortie, and it is deliberately **not** a flight model, not procedural generation and not a depth-ring content system.

**Index wording, reconciled.** The index (IDX:33) describes the system as "the web world model (derelict list, seeds plus deltas, what is loaded) and the abstracted pod hop between home-ship and target (state 2.5), docking, fuel cost. Full flight is out of scope. Depth Rings extends it at VS; the Chart Table only displays it." This GDD keeps every clause and sharpens four: (a) "seeds plus deltas" are **schema reservations only**, inert at MVP (the roster is the handmade `mvp_world.json`; deltas are Vertical Slice, ADR-LSG-04); (b) "docking" has **two distinct meanings** that this GDD keeps apart: the pod docks at a derelict's `entry_room_key` room (an arrival cell and a presentation cut, no socket) and body-to-body `docked(a, b, frame_shift_px)` (the Assembly weld precondition, established by `berth`); (c) "fuel cost" is a *debit* of the Graph's FUEL store through M16 `withdraw_store`, never a Web Map & Transit store; (d) the concept's "fly the pod to a derelict" and "one handmade target derelict" are understated: in this GDD the player **takes the pod** or **hops** (nobody flies or steers; PCI:217 "no pod steering at MVP"), and the MVP contains two sorties plus one berth across three ships and a pod (CONFLICT-wording rows CD-1 and CD-3 in Interactions with Other Systems). "Repair a podcraft" (CON:19) is not MVP: the MVP pod is ready, and a reserved `POD_NOT_READY` reason leaves a clean slot for Onboarding.

**Vocabulary (fixed for this document).** *Web Map & Transit* or *the transit machine* names this system; *the `TRANSIT` behaviour state* names Horror Entities' sixth entity state (HOR:280), a different thing; `TRANSIT_HOLD` names the Pipeline's black plate. *Derelict* is never "spawn" (the roster names roles only); *restart from the last autosave* is the death stub (the older phrasing is banned, AC-WM-70); rings are **1-based** (outer ring = 1). Every name this GDD gives a block, hook, command, read or event is **provisional until the registry merge** and is marked so where it first appears.

**MVP slice.** In: the roster home + A + B + one persistent pod (`mvp_world.json`); star hops HOME<->A and HOME<->B (`pod_hop`, prepaid return); `berth` / `unberth` for B; weld reconcile (ABSORBED) and cut (ORPHAN, no recovery); dormancy-at-once with no fast-forward; ring 1 everywhere; fuel as a finite clock (no refuel). Later: FUEL_PORT refuel and fast-forward / deltas (VS-A); seeds and procedural derelicts, rings 2-3 (Depth Rings, VS); Codex knowledge (VS); multi-seat pod and stranded-crew recovery (VS-B); home exterior lamp (OQ-WM-12); pod repair gate `POD_NOT_READY` (Onboarding).

## Player Fantasy

*Source: creative-director proposal, reconciled with the game-designer and systems-designer proposals and the advisor rulings. Everything numeric here is a locked name cited verbatim or a Web Map & Transit `[PLACEHOLDER]`.*

### Pillars served (verbatim, then scope notes)

**Primary.**

> **Pillar 5: Deeper Is Richer, Loss Is Not Total.** "Risk and reward map to depth in the web. The outer ring is kept unsafe by depletion and wear (scrubbers degrade, salvage runs out), so there is no safe place to farm forever. **Failure contract**: losing the home-ship or a crewmate costs ship and body, never knowledge — decoded rules and lore persist, and the next crew wakes knowing more. *Design test*: safe farming vs. scarcity that pushes inward → scarcity." (CON:272-279)

> **Pillar 1: The World Never Lies.** "Pipes leak, frost forms, air hisses; the physical world is ground truth. Only instruments and perception can be wrong. [...] *Design test*: a hidden stat vs. a visibly leaking pipe → the pipe." (CON:208-221)

**Served.**

> **Pillar 2: Dread Before Chaos.** "Every breach is preceded by a tell a careful player could have perceived (sound, light, gauge drift, hull stress)." (CON:223-229) Web Map & Transit serves it as the **escape verb**: "Pod-hop departure from derelict A ends the breach" (AT:73, AT-22). It starts no breach and arrival never lands the player in an unwarned lethal state.

> **Pillar 3: The Web Watches.** "Depth sets the baseline; player action moves it from there." (CON:231-237) Web Map & Transit publishes the ring and pushes no stimulus at all; the one declared LOUD noise report of each berth apply is pushed by Interaction, as channel owner, through the `berth` verb's declaration, the route Attention accepts (OQ-AT-23; IA-33, IA-40; WM-26; OQ-WM-40, resolved: user ruling D59) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. The hop stays silent to the Web (it is the escape verb); the berth does not, because the home-ship's own drive burning fuel to come alongside is the largest power-and-noise act in the MVP and "restraint is a real strategy" needs it to cost something. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*

> **Pillar 4: Sanity Rewrites Reality.** "At least one channel of truth always remains." (CON:261-265) Web Map & Transit is one of those channels and owns no corruptible surface.

**Scope of P5 served (stated so the quote above does not imply a guarantee this system does not keep).** Web Map & Transit serves **P5's scarcity half**: fuel is a finite clock shared with the reactor, a left derelict is spent by visiting it and never regrows, depth sets the baseline, and the roster is never destroyed. It is **explicitly not a carrier of the failure contract's "never knowledge" half** ("decoded rules and lore persist, and the next crew wakes knowing more"): its seen and boarded flags are WORLD-scope and roll back with the world on death (WM-28), and MVP ships zero PROFILE blocks. The owner of that half is the Vertical Slice **Knowledge Codex & Logs** (PROFILE scope). Nothing in this GDD may be cited as evidence that knowledge survives a failure.

**Lexical and scope notes (carried under the quotes).** *"Fly."* The concept says "Fly the pod to a derelict" (CON:176). In every sentence this GDD writes, the player **takes the pod** or **hops**; nobody flies or steers. The exterior leg is the MVP's "abstracted pod hop" (CON:467-468): scripted, constant velocity, no input; it is a *view* of an abstraction, not a flight model. *"Repair a podcraft"* (CON:19) is not MVP. *"Flicker."* The residual lamp in the exterior is **lit or dark**, never "flickering" (Lighting bans the word for tells; the residual lamp is "steady, never modulated by Attention", DA:452). *System name.* Always "Web Map & Transit" or "the transit machine", never bare "transit" (Horror's `TRANSIT` behaviour state is a different thing). *Derelicts.* Never "spawn"; the roster names roles only.

### The emotion: the committed crossing

The core emotion is **the committed crossing**. The player chooses a wreck, pays for it out of the same tank that keeps the reactor running, and then has no hands for the length of a leg (`HOP_LEG_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER]) while the dark carries them across. The fantasy is not mastery of a vehicle; it is **the navigator's authority and the passenger's exposure in one beat**: "I chose this, I paid for this, and now I can only watch it come." The crew member the player *is* here is the one who decides where the ship's life goes next. The home-ship is a body the crew keeps breathing (Core Fantasy, CON:43-50); Web Map & Transit is the system that spends a piece of that breath to reach for more. Every hop is a small loan against the reactor, repaid only by what is carried home.

Five beats, in order across one hop:

| Beat | Feeling | Source in the world | Owner of the source |
|---|---|---|---|
| The pick (chart table, then pod console) | *Sober competence*: "the navigator planning a sortie" (ART 2.11) | the chart shows only what the crew has lit (ART 7.4); the console press is the commitment | Chart Table (display, undesigned), Web Map & Transit (the command) |
| The debit | *A price you can see later.* Not a number on a screen: the reactor's fuel tank is lighter and the timer it feeds is shorter | the Graph's FUEL loop via M16 `withdraw_store`; the witnesses are the Graph's (sight-glass, drip, puddle, SCRAM) | Life-Support Graph |
| The leg | *Smallness.* "Awe with a cold floor under it" (ART 2.5); the only time the web is seen as a thing | exterior chase view, the pod's running light the sole source, filaments only inside its pool | Web Map & Transit (presentation) |
| The resolve | *Somebody else's lantern.* The wreck resolving out of the dark with its one residual lamp: the only lit thing in the exterior the player did not pay to light, "which should feel slightly wrong because it is" (ART 2.5) | `visible_unknown`, lit iff the lamp node is intact (OK or DEGRADED) and its persisted battery stock is above zero (DA:452, narrowed by the chain predicate, CF-DA) | Derelict Authoring (data), Graph (stock), Web Map & Transit (draws) |
| The dock | *The held breath before boarding* (ART 2.6's register begins) | arrival on the authored `arrive_*` cell of the `entry_room_key` room; the wreck exactly as it was left | Web Map & Transit, Ship Body |

**What the player must never feel here:** mastery of piloting, a loading screen, a menu transition, a fast-travel convenience, or a reward for arriving. The hop is a decision and a crossing, not a feature.

### Framing choice (Category Gameplay: direct framing)

| | Framing | What it centres | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| **A** | **Direct: "The sortie's two ends."** *Pick, pay, cross, dock, return.* The fantasy is the commitment and its price; the leg is the mood of that commitment | the decision and the debit (P5), the truthful crossing (P1), the escape (P2) | **Chosen** |
| B | Navigator at the chart table: "plotting the web" | the map as an instrument, route planning | Rejected as primary: the map is the Chart Table's furniture, and framing this GDD around it would absorb a neighbour and overstate a star of three nodes as "navigation" |
| C | "The web seen whole": the leg as a set-piece of cosmic awe | Smallness as spectacle | Folded into A as the leg's mood, not the system's identity: a short crossing repeated several times a session becomes a cutscene, invites skip pressure and spectacle creep (a nebula was already rejected in ART 2.5), and the fantasy would be "look at this" rather than "I chose this" |

*(orchestrator ruling: framing A; the concept's MDA ranking puts Discovery first and Challenge second (CON:82-83): Discovery is delivered by what is at the end of the hop, Challenge by the cost of getting there, and A carries both without claiming either for the leg itself.)* It passes the "and also" test at system scale: it is an FTL jump, *and also* the fuel came out of the reactor that keeps you breathing, and the wreck you left is exactly as dangerous when you come back.

### Anchor moments

*Moment 1, "Somebody else's lantern" (first approach to derelict A; the Discovery payoff).* At the home-ship's pod console, lit and in reach, the player confirms derelict A. The press is the commitment: the departure applies at once, `HOP_FUEL_ML` [PLACEHOLDER] leaves the home FUEL_TANK node through M16, and the screen goes to the black hold (`TRANSIT_HOLD`). The camera cuts to the pod: a small sprite, one Tungsten running-light pool, nothing else. Filaments appear only where the pool touches them, iridescent there and nowhere else (ART 2.5). Near the end of the leg, one warm square resolves on A's north hull run (`exterior_anchor_px` (576, 84), Tungsten): `fx_a_res`, still running on battery `b_a1` (540000 dJ, 45 min of loaded time). It is lit because its lamp node is intact and its persisted stock is above zero, and for no other reason. Black, cut, the player is standing on `arrive_a_dock` (168, 336). On the chart afterwards A carries the single Tungsten tick, because the crew saw it from the pod (ART 7.4). *What the player learns:* the map grows only by looking; the lamp is a real thing on a real ship.

*Moment 2, "They escaped, they did not win" (the return during a breach; the P2 payoff).* This is Attention's Moment B (AT:73) seen from this side. The player carries the scrubber to A's dock room under a breach. The pod console is never refused for Attention reasons. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* It is never refused for light either: Interaction still refuses a `pod_hop` lit exemption (IA-6 stands), but the docked pod's dock-room prop carries a small self-powered status glow that counts as an ordinary lit fixture, so the console is lit at a dark dock with a spent cell, no glowstick and no power (WM-8b, user ruling OQ-WM-39); fuel never strands (E-03). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* The press departs: A's sections go dormant in the same apply and BREACH ends because the recorded occupant left a non-home locus body (AT-22): Attention's AT-22 departure test runs against the recorded `locus_body` before any dormancy fallback in the same run, so the breach ends and no fallback occurs (AT-22, E-27). The return leg costs nothing (the round trip was prepaid), so a return hop is never refused for fuel: a wreck can never strand the player for want of fuel (P5). The exterior is the same black and the same running light, and A's residual lamp is still lit behind them if its battery holds. *What the player learns:* the pod is a way out, not a weapon. The crossing home is the quiet after the tell, and its quiet is honest. Had the hop come during the TELL instead of the BREACH, the same departure would void the due-run breach (TELL to CALM, ABORTED, no STRESS: AT-22): the pod ends a derelict tell, and a tell centred on the home ship is the one a hop cannot end. *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*

*Moment 3, "The ship comes to the wreck" (berth; the weld's first beat).* Derelict B has been boarded. At the home pod console the player issues `berth`, paying `BERTH_FUEL_ML` [PLACEHOLDER]. In the fiction the home-ship, not the dead hulk, comes alongside (a hulk has no drive: P1). The player is not in the pod: they stand at the home console inside the hull, and the interior never shows the home-ship's own motion (`interior_invariance`), so the berth has no progress element. Its witnesses are physical and are sound and contact, never a picture: a sustained drive-hum and hull-strain bed for the whole of `BERTH_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] (the audible length of the closing is the berth's distance, and the same drive burn is reported to Attention as one LOUD noise push at the apply, made by Interaction from the `berth` declaration, not by this system, WM-26) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*, then the hull-contact cue when the two hulls meet (this system's `BERTHED` event, voiced by Adaptive Audio, AA-33 (g); Body's `BODY_DOCK_CONTACT` fires later, at the weld) and the weld prompt that appears at the airlock only once `docked` holds (MSA:47). This GDD promises no picture of the approach (Visual/Audio §2 and VA-R4 rule that no exterior is shown). *What the player learns:* the "mobile home-ship" is literal; growth costs fuel and is heard by the Web (one LOUD report at the apply), and at campaign scale the debit is small beside the reactor's own burn (D.4), so the felt price is the noise and the time, not the toll; the weld starts with a physical meeting, not a menu. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*

*Moment 4, "Exactly as we left it" (re-entry; the P5 payoff).* The player returns to A after a session at home. A is frozen at its last state: no fast-forward (LS-17, "resumes from its last state with no fast-forward"; HE-21). The pipe they left half-failed is still half-failed; the salvage they took is still gone; nothing regrew. If the residual lamp went dark while A was loaded, it is dark now, and "a lamp found dark on return is the honest reading" (DA:452). *What the player learns:* wrecks are spent by visiting them. Scarcity pushes inward.

**Moment-ordering guard (playtest tripwire).** Within the MVP 30-minute solo session (CON:483-485) a typical player should meet Moments 1, 2 or 4, and 3. If playtest notes describe the hop as "a loading screen" (Moment 1 unfelt) or "free" (the crossing costs nothing in time or risk), the *tuning* is wrong, not the fantasy. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* A tester who calls the *toll* small is not a failure: fuel is a campaign-scale timer (D.4: six sorties are at most 12 % of the tank; the felt cost of a sortie is time away and the reactor's own burn), and the P5 observables (`FUEL_LOW`, the reserve marks, a dark lamp on return) are exercised by scripted start-state fixtures, not by retuning (AC-WM-37b). The levers are `HOP_LEG_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER], `HOP_FUEL_ML` [PLACEHOLDER] and `BERTH_FUEL_ML` [PLACEHOLDER]. Making the hop free or skippable is not one of them.

### Design test (this system's, paired with P5 and P1)

> **"A crossing the player chose and paid for, to a wreck that stays as they left it, vs. a free trip to a place that resets → the crossing they paid for."**

| Half | Wording | Rejects | Checkable as |
|---|---|---|---|
| *Paid for* | Every hop out of the home body and every berth debits the Graph's fuel stock through M16, and the debit is witnessed only by the Graph's channels | zero cost; a Web Map & Transit fuel meter; an unpaid berth | fuel stock before and after equals the placeholder cost; review: no Web Map & Transit HUD element |
| *Stays as left* | A departed derelict is dormant at once and resumes with no fast-forward and no refill | a derelict that keeps running while away; any respawn or refill; a "fresh" derelict per visit | the Graph partition's `state_hash` for A is unchanged across a home stay |
| *Chose* (rider) | The crossing begins only at a lit pod console the player pressed. **For a hop** the trip is shown (the exterior leg). **For a berth** the trip is witnessed by sound and contact, not shown: a sustained drive-hum and hull-strain bed for the whole of BERTHING, then the contact Settle and the weld prompt; the interior never shows the home-ship's own motion (`interior_invariance`), so no picture is promised | a wreck pre-berthed from minute 0 (the closing would be a lie about the cost and the time of coming alongside); a hop with a still black screen and no leg; a berth with no drive bed (an asserted crossing) | review: the leg exterior exists for every hop (AC-WM-110); the berth bed spans BERTHING and ends at the contact beat (AC-WM-117); designed: the berth bed is Audio AA-33 (g), read from `web.view.transit_state == BERTHING` (state edge only), discharged by AC-AA-128 |

**Secondary test (P1 on the exterior):** "A wreck shown lit because the game wants you to go there vs. a wreck shown lit because its battery still holds → the battery." This rejects any authored lure, guidance glow or "point of interest" highlight on the map or the leg.

### Under hallucination: how the fantasy deepens, never breaks

Web Map & Transit owns **no corruptible channel**. The world block, `web.view`, `pod_at`, the leg exterior, `visible_unknown` and the residual-lamp point are truth channels the Perception Filter (undesigned) and Sanity never wrap (DA:1188; ART 7.4). So "deepens" can never mean "the map lies". It means three things.

1. **The crossing becomes a truth anchor the player learns to lean on.** At low sanity the derelict lies: a scrubber reads fixed (MVP hallucination class (b), CON:473), a gauge disagrees with the frost. The leg home is the one stretch where nothing in the world layer, and nothing Web Map & Transit draws, can be a hallucination. The HUD stays up through `TRANSIT_HOLD` and remains the suit layer, so it is still lie-capable (ART 2.9 Stage 1, "instruments disagree"). Players will learn to use the hop as a *perceptual reset of the world, not of the mind*: their sanity is unchanged by Web Map & Transit (Sanity owns the effect of `body_is_home`, SN:194), and only the world in front of them is guaranteed true. This is P4's "at least one channel of truth always remains", given a place and a duration.
2. **True channels deliver bad news a doubting player will want to disbelieve.** A residual lamp found dark on return is honest (DA:452). A player at low sanity will suspect it is a lie. The game must hold the line: the lamp's state is never wrapped, so the doubt is the player's to resolve, by boarding and finding the battery spent. This is "no lie is unfalsifiable" (CON:261) turned around: here there is no lie at all, and suspicion itself costs time.
3. **The corruptible instrument sits next to the truthful witness, never inside Web Map & Transit.** A fuel sight-glass on the home tank is a module-body reading and can lie (LSG:168, the corruptible module-body channel). Its witnesses (puddle, drip, SCRAM) are the Graph's consequence channel and cannot. The hop's `FUEL_LOW` refusal is a *rule*, not a reading. **Routed ask to the Perception Filter** *(provisional — Perception Filter undesigned; ASK-F1, CD-4)*: the pod console's refusal reasons (`FUEL_LOW`, `ARRIVAL_BLOCKED` and the rest) must not be Filter-wrappable. A hop the player wrongly believes is refused would trap them inside a breach by a lie, a lethal lie with no falsification check, which breaks P4's guard rail. Recommendation: the refusal reasons join the truth-only list.

| Hallucination class | May it touch Web Map & Transit? | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| (a) phantom entities | Not in the leg exterior, not on the chart. A phantom may appear in a dock room *after* arrival: that is the derelict's space and the Filter's business | the leg is a truth channel |
| (b) false system states | Not the pod console's refusal, not `pod_at`, not the dock prop | the refusal reasons are truth-only (ASK-F1); the dock prop renders iff `pod_at` equals that dock (WM-33) |
| (c) crewmates as horrors (VS-B) | The pod has one seat. In co-op the crewmate glyph on the chart is the beam-shape identity (ART 7.4/7.5), a human channel, never faked | P1 human channel *(provisional — Co-op Session undesigned)* |
| (d) phantom salvage | Web Map & Transit never counts loot; `carry_ref` travels unchanged through the hop (IA:290) | scope guard (Boarding & Salvage, designed, owns loot) |

**Art-bible scope conflict (CD-2, routed to the art-director).** ART 2.9 titles the low-sanity overlay "modifier on any state". Taken literally that includes 2.5 Pod transit and 2.11 Menus & web map. ART 7.4 already excludes the map ("never shows ... violet ... sanity"); the leg exterior has no such exclusion in the bible yet this GDD makes it a truth channel. Recommended fix (art-bible owner): amend 2.9's scope to exclude **the world content of 2.5 Pod transit** (the exterior, the pod sprite and pool, filaments, the wreck silhouettes and the residual lamp) and all of 2.11. The HUD shown during the hold stays in scope and lie-capable. Until amended, this GDD's Visual/Audio section states the exclusion and cites this paragraph.

### Reference techniques: adopt and reject

| Reference | Technique | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| *Beyond the Aquila Rift* (concept inspiration; ART 9.5) | the arrival as the moment the world turns out to be other than you thought | **Adopt the mood of arrival** (the held breath, the wrong-feeling residual light). **Reject arrival-as-deception**: the place you dock at is exactly what it was; the "kind lie" belongs to Sanity, never to the crossing |
| *Pandorum* (concept inspiration; the chest lamp) | a single carried light defining what exists | **Adopt** for the leg: the pod's running light is the only source and the filaments exist only inside it (ART 2.5, Principle 3) |
| *Sunless Sea* | fuel as a finite campaign clock; darkness as the default state of the sea | **Adopt** fuel-as-clock (no MVP refuel) and darkness as default. **Reject** its Terror meter and on-screen fuel and supply counters (P3: never a meter) and steering the hull (anti-pillar) |
| *FTL: Faster Than Light* | the jump as an abstracted, committed beat; fuel per jump | **Adopt** the commitment of a jump and one fuel debit per sortie. **Reject** jump-triggered random events (this system pushes no stimulus per hop and starts no harm), fuel-zero as an instant fail state (the floor `HOP_FUEL_FLOOR_ML` [PLACEHOLDER] refuses the hop before the reactor's margin is spent) and a beacon map you scout ahead (the chart never reveals) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| *Stellaris* (concept inspiration) | a galaxy map of nodes and lanes; survey reveals | **Adopt** the star of nodes as legible human geometry (rectilinear dashed routes, ART 7.4). **Reject** reveal-by-survey, any map knowledge the crew did not light, and resource or threat overlays |
| *Satisfactory* (concept inspiration) | infrastructure that moves you across distance | **Reject for MVP**: no route building, no vehicle progression, no pod upgrades (anti-pillar: not a freeform builder) |
| *Project Zomboid* (designer touchstone) | every trip out is a risk with a known way home; death is final | **Adopt** the sortie shape (out, loot, home) and the unchanged world on return. Death stays lethal at both ends of the hop and the pod is not a respawn. Saves are Save/Load's: "restart from the last autosave" (SL-7) |
| *Outer Wilds* (outside the concept list; recorded as a rejection) | free-flight navigation as the core verb | **Reject** entirely: "Full flight is out of scope" (index); no input during the leg |
| Joseph Wright of Derby (ART 9.1) | the source inside the frame | **Adopt** for the exterior: the pod's pool and the residual lamp are the only sources, both inside the frame |

### Anti-pillar guard rails (stated here, enforced in Detailed Design)

1. **Not a flight game.** The player never steers, throttles, aims or lands the pod. The leg is authored and constant-velocity. No input during it, no minigame, no docking QTE.
2. **Not a combat game.** The pod has no weapon, no ram, no flare launcher, no shield. The hop repels nothing; it removes the player from danger.
3. **Not a freeform builder.** There are no routes to build, no pod modules, no pod upgrades, no pod customisation. The pod is one persistent body, not an asset to improve.
4. **No authored story.** Hops carry no dialogue, radio chatter, log drops, captions or narrated arrivals. The world block holds no narrative field.
5. **No win state.** No "reached the centre" flag, no depth-progress meter, no percentage of the web charted, no completion count on the chart or in `SaveInfo` (the depth label is `RING <n>` with no progress semantics; ART 2.11 "the centre is a horizon").
6. **No intentional PvP.** In VS-B no crew action through Web Map & Transit can strand, abandon or harm another player by design. Leaving with the pod while a crewmate is aboard the derelict is a VS-B co-op question (the pod has one seat, DA-3), flagged, not designed here *(provisional — Co-op Session undesigned; CD-5)*.
7. **No meter, no reveal.** No fuel gauge, ETA, leg progress bar, danger rating, loot estimate or "recommended" marker. The chart records what the crew lit; it never reveals.
8. **No convenience that erases the price.** No free hop out of the home body, no skip, no instant return, no refill of a derelict, no fast-forward.

### Design Test summary (for validation)

We will know this Player Fantasy was right if, at the MVP kill/continue playtest (at least three outside testers, a 30-plus-minute solo session, CON:483-485): at least two of three testers mention the residual lamp or the exterior unprompted (Moment 1 felt); no tester describes the hop as a loading screen, and none asks for a skip after fewer than four hops (else retune `HOP_LEG_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] down within its range before considering a skip); at least one tester notices the fuel debit through the Graph's witnesses or the reactor timer, not through a Web Map & Transit element (in the session with the scripted low-tank fixture of AC-WM-37b, since a 30-minute session at campaign-scale defaults will not reach `FUEL_LOW` by play alone) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; at least one tester uses the pod to escape a tell or breach (Moment 2) and none reports the pod refusing them during one; and no tester reports a derelict "refilling" (Moment 4 holds); **and, for P3, no tester reports berthing or hopping as a way to dodge the Web's attention for free** (the observable that validates the berth-stimulus decision, OQ-WM-40, at the kill/continue playtest: AC-WM-81g), and no tester reports the fuel floor (`FUEL_LOW`) as arbitrary or hidden (the observable for the reserve band, ASK-G3: AC-WM-81h).

## Detailed Design

### Core Rules

*Conventions.* Rules are named `WM-n`; each states a **Rule**, the documents it **Cites**, the **Pillar** it serves, and what **Decided** it (advisor ruling `R-n`, locked GDD section, or an orchestrator ruling recorded here). Criteria are written in Acceptance Criteria as `AC-WM-nn`, edge cases as `E-nn`, formulas as `D.n` (section Formulas). Names of blocks, hooks, commands, reads, events and states introduced here are **provisional until the registry merge** and are marked *(provisional)* once per rule. A contract that a neighbour wrote as an *offer* is **confirmed or corrected** in the Interactions CONFLICT rows, never copied silently. Every `[PLACEHOLDER]` value is a placeholder at every mention; the Body, Lighting and Attention placeholders this system reuses (`WORLD_COORD_LIMIT` [PLACEHOLDER], `VEL_MAX_PX_PER_TICK` [PLACEHOLDER], `BODIES_LIVE_MAX` [PLACEHOLDER], `AMBIENT_RING_PM` [PLACEHOLDER], `B_table` [PLACEHOLDER], `R_BREATH` [PLACEHOLDER]) belong to their owners and are cited, never restated as settled.

**Orchestrator rulings recorded in this GDD** (where two specialist proposals or a ruling's wording disagreed; each is repeated in the rule it affects and forwarded in Open Questions):

| ID | Ruling | Why | Rule |
|---|---|---|---|
| OR-1 | **Arrival is two SYSTEM commands**: `pod_arrive` ends the leg and enters `ARRIVING`; `pod_settle`, a fixed `ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] later, makes the target live, re-checks the cell, transfers the avatar and returns to `IDLE`. **Accepted (ruled, checkpoint 2026-10-26):** R12 is amended to "the arrived one goes live in `pod_settle`" and R26 to "re-validated in `pod_settle`"; liveness and the re-check move to `pod_settle`, `ARRIVING` has a fixed simulation length, and the Pipeline (not the sim) holds the plate for any projector lag. | A `PROJECTOR` has no submit path (KRN conformance), so the simulation cannot wait for a scene-ready report; a frame-rate-dependent exit from `ARRIVING` would break replay and restore determinism; and it gives every state R2 names a job. Nothing live runs while the player is behind the black hold (P2 fairness). | WM-14 |
| OR-2 | `loaded` and `berthed` are **derived, not persisted**. `loaded(d)` reads the Graph through the pure read `section(section_id) -> {kind, dormant}` (ASK-G1, accepted: LS-19 publishes `section()`); the DIAGNOSTIC mirror of the first draft is retired (checkpoint 2026-10-26, C3) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. | Two writers of one fact is a recorded failure class; SL-15 forbids clamping on restore, so a persisted mirror could only reject the whole save. | WM-3 |
| OR-3 | Phase-boundary commands are **pose-carrying** (absolute end pose, derived from persisted parameters, plus the next velocity in one apply); a pose offset of exactly `v x (T_apply - (t0 + n))` (a restore drop, or a kernel queue delay of up to +8 ticks, KRN D.4) is *expected*, not a defect; `LEG_POSE_MISMATCH` is raised iff the observed offset differs from that value (checkpoint 2026-10-26, C8). | KRN drops every pending command on restore (`Dropped(RELOAD)`); geometry must not depend on tick counting. | WM-13 |
| OR-4 | The lamp predicate is the **chain predicate** (ruled, checkpoint 2026-10-26; amends R6): **lit iff `read_view.node(fx_res).state in {OK, DEGRADED}` and `read_view.node(battery).stock > 0`** (registry: `residual_lamp_predicate`) (LS-10 states, LS-19 `node()` fields). It equals `stock > 0` in default MVP play. | R6 amended: a salvaged or destroyed lamp (M6) with a charged battery would otherwise leave a phantom light, a P1 lie by commission; DA:452's "honest reading" stands. | WM-18, OQ-WM-3 |
| OR-5 | Items **not in the original rulings**, all accepted (ruled, checkpoint 2026-10-26): the `ORPHAN` status, the `unberth` verb, the `UNBERTHED` event, the `TARGET_ABSORBED` reason, persisting the active leg's parameters (not only its id) and the active berth's path parameters. | Each closes a hole the rulings leave open (a named cut piece, the berthed-but-never-welded soft lock, a distinct diagnostic, golden saves surviving a tuning pass). | WM-3, WM-9, WM-22, WM-23, WM-30 |
| OR-6 | The fuel payer is found by **section `kind` HOME**, not by "the home body" and not by name. | After a weld, B's tank sits in the home body but B's section stays `kind` DERELICT; section kind is unambiguous. A refinement of R10. | WM-20 |
| OR-7 *(proposed, not adopted; design review 2026-10-27)* | The fuel payer is every FUEL_TANK **connected to the home fuel loop**, not only HOME-kind tanks. | The economy-designer finding: after a weld the player sees fuel they can never use to leave, and welding a fueled derelict gains them nothing. Needs a Graph read of fuel-loop connectivity that the Graph does not publish, and reopens the single-tank reserve marks, so OR-6 stands. | E-06, OQ-WM-23 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| OR-8 *(design review 2026-10-27)* | **Boundary values are keyed to nominal ticks**: `arrive_T = t0 + n`, the settle tick, `first_boarded_T` and `t_phase += n_i`; a late apply sets the nominal trajectory at the apply tick. | A restore drops pending SYSTEM commands, so a boundary applies one tick late; keying persisted values to the apply tick made the restored and uninterrupted hashes diverge forever and the restore-equivalence AC unreachable. | WM-13, WM-14, WM-21, D.2, E-23, AC-WM-54 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |

#### A. Scope, ownership, the block and the hook

**WM-1 — Six facts, one writer each.** *Rule.* Web Map & Transit owns exactly six facts: (1) the roster records (what hulls exist and what the crew has seen of each); (2) `pod_at` (where the pod is); (3) the transit machine's state and `leg_epoch`; (4) the berth state; (5) the leg and berth *data* (content files `assets/data/world/legs.json` and `mvp_world.json`, not state); (6) the published ring reads. It never writes fuel, dormancy mechanics, pose arithmetic, geometry, air, light or stimuli (it makes no `push_stimulus` call at all; the berth's one declared LOUD noise report is Interaction's, from the `berth` declaration, IA-33, IA-40, WM-26) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling G1)*: it calls the mutators their owners publish (Graph M16 and M19 `set_section_dormant` (ASK-G1, accepted), Body BM5, BM6 and BM7 (ASK-B1, accepted), Interaction `sync_section`, Emitter E1 at build and Emitter E10 `set_dock_status` in the applies that write `pod_at` (WM-8b)) inside one atomic apply *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, and every other effect is a read. It does not define the weld offer, `q` or channels (Assembly), `room_at`, `build_section` geometry or sockets (Layout), the `docked` test (Body), fuel physics, puddles, compression or dormancy mechanics (Graph), the picker, route drawing, snap-to-node or the pause panel (Chart Table), loot or the aboard loop (Boarding & Salvage), or sounds (Adaptive Audio). It calls, cites and publishes. *Cites.* KRN CR-9; IDX:33; brief scope guard. *Pillar.* P1 (no hidden Web Map & Transit stat). *Decided.* R10, guard rail 3.

**WM-2 — One block, one hook.** *Rule.* One SIM block `web.state` *(provisional)*: `persist` true, `persist_scope` WORLD, `scope` SHARED, `repl_hint` SHARED_SUMMARY, `schema_version` 1, `supported_versions` [1], `restore_after: [layout.state, graph.state, body.state]` and **no other entry**. `controller.state` is deliberately excluded: the registry marks it as an open conflict (REG consumer note `controller.state (OQ-9 CONFLICT)`, OQ-9), and `IN_POD` is reconciled by a read in `transit.step` rather than at restore (WM-16). One step hook `transit.step` *(provisional)*, `divisor` 1, `offset` 0, `after: [body.integrate, graph.propagate.failure]`; both ids exist verbatim (`body.integrate` in REG `body_state_block`, `graph.propagate.failure` LSG:64), and an unknown id in `after` would fail boot (CR-3, no alias concept). The divisor is **not a knob**: leg arrival is exact only if the hook sees every tick (WM-11). Dirty keys are explicit: `WEB/<derelict_id>`, `WEB/T` (machine and berth fields), `WEB/POD` (pod fields); the 4.7 packed-array setter change means no write may rely on a setter to mark a key (SL-15 item 9). Per tick during a leg or a berth this block marks **zero** dirty keys (progress is derived from `T - t0`); Body dirties the pod's pose each tick, which is Body's cost. The block registers one `state_hash` row (records in `mvp_world.json` order, fixed field order, no dictionary iteration; KRN D.12). *Cites.* KRN CR-3, CR-8; SL-15; REG `layout_state_block`, `body_state_block` and LSG LS-1 `graph.state` (the three `restore_after` block ids). *Pillar.* P5. *Decided.* R1.

**WM-3 — The roster and the record.** *Rule.* The block holds one **record** per hop-addressable hull of content: HOME, A, B (the pod has no record; it is WM-5). The MVP roster is read from `assets/data/world/mvp_world.json` (`{template_id, kind, world_slot}`, DA-26, DA:140). A record is plain integers and enums; no `Vector2i`, no string, no narrative field, no "reached the centre" flag, no completion flag.

| Field | Type | Range | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `derelict_id` | i32 | stable content id | the string key is resolved to the id at load |
| `template_id` | i32 | index into the content table | `home_ship`, `derelict_a`, `derelict_b` |
| `kind` | u8 | HOME, DERELICT | fixed by the roster |
| `ring` | u8 | 1 and up | **1-based**; MVP: 1 for all (WM-27) |
| `world_slot` | u8 | 0..7 | distinct per record (lint) |
| `seed` | 8 bytes (u64 under S-1) | 0 at MVP | schema reservation; handmade = 0 (WM-29) |
| `status` | u8 | UNSEEN, SEEN, BOARDED, ABSORBED, ORPHAN *(ruled, checkpoint 2026-10-26)* | WM-28 (registry: `web_record_status`) |
| `unknown_seen` | u8 | 0 or 1 | the crew saw the residual lamp lit from the pod; **monotone**: set to 1 at a `pod_arrive`, never reset to 0 by a later dark lamp, a later visit, a weld or a restore (WM-28, AC-WM-123) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `first_boarded_T` | i64 | -1, or the tick | the NOMINAL tick of the first `pod_settle` at this record (`arrive_T + ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS`, WM-13), never the late apply tick; HOME = 0 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `section_id`, `body_id` | i32 | from Graph and Layout at `init_world` | stable ids (LY-6 e); validated at `post_restore`, never repaired |

Machine and pod fields (persisted): `transit_state` (u8: IDLE, LEG, ARRIVING, BERTHING), `leg_epoch` (u32, monotonic, never reused), `t0`, `arrive_T`, `t_phase` (i64 ticks; `arrive_T` and `t_phase` are NOMINAL ticks, `t0 + n`, `arrive_T + ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS` and `t_phase + n_i`, never the wall apply tick, WM-13) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*, `target_id` (i32), `leg_reversed` (u8, 0 or 1: set by the T5 recovery reversal, WM-15, cleared by the next `pod_hop`; *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*), `origin_id` (i32: the record the in-flight leg departed from, HOME on an OUT leg and the departure derelict on a BACK leg, NONE while IDLE or BERTHING; *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*), `leg_dir` (u8: OUT, BACK), the **active leg's parameters** `{n, vx, vy, p0x, p0y}` *(ruled, checkpoint 2026-10-26); see WM-30*, `berth_target_id` (i32 or NONE), `berth_epoch` (u32), `berth_phase` (u8), the **active berth's path** `{m, n_i, vx_i, vy_i, start offset}` *(ruled, checkpoint 2026-10-26), C13; validated in WM-30*, `pod_body_id`, `pod_section_id`, `pod_at` (a record id, or IN_FLIGHT = -1). **Derived, never persisted** (OR-2): `loaded(d)` is `not section(section_id(d)).dormant` through the Graph's pure read `section(section_id) -> {kind, dormant}` (ASK-G1, accepted: LS-19 publishes the read) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* (the Graph is the truth, and `graph.state` restores first); `berthed` is `berth_target_id != NONE` with the machine `IDLE`; leg progress is `T - t0`. Diagnostic counters (`transit_stale_cmds` and the like) are DIAGNOSTIC-class and are not part of the block. *(Amended 2026-10-29 — sync knock-on fix)* **The two Save/Load trigger counters `pod_arrived_seq` and `berthed_seq` (WM-31) are likewise host-local runtime counters, not block fields**: not persisted, not in the `state_hash` row, not in the byte budget (D.11) or the golden save, reset to 0 by `post_restore`, and read only through the separate `web.save_seq()`. The block is small by construction (about 700 canonical bytes with field tags; budget in D.11) and comfortably inside `repl_hint` SHARED_SUMMARY; one golden save per version covers the three records and the pod. *Cites.* DA-26; SL-15; KRN D.12. *Pillar.* P5, P1. *Decided.* R1, R19, R20; OR-2.

**WM-4 — Star topology.** *Rule.* The pod travels HOME to exactly one derelict, or from that derelict to HOME. From HOME the legal targets are derelicts whose `status` is not ABSORBED or ORPHAN and that are not berthed; from a derelict the only target is HOME. There is no derelict-to-derelict edge, no free coordinate, no steering, no route building. After B is welded and ABSORBED the home dock remains the `airlock` room and the post-weld sortie is HOME to A (loaded set S4). The MVP sentence "pick a target, hop, dock, return" is the whole topology. *Cites.* IDX:33; PCI:217 ("no pod steering at MVP"). *Pillar.* P5 (the star is the shortest honest model of "deeper"). *Decided.* R14.

**WM-5 — The pod is one persistent Layout body, never built per hop, never breathed.** *Rule.* The pod is built **once**, at `init_world`, from the `pod` template (DA-3: one room `pod_cabin`, 2 x 2 tiles, one seat cell `seat_pod_1` (72, 48) section-local px, no fixture, no module node). It is a Layout body with its own `body_id` (BDY-25) (registry: `pod_body`) whose section is **dormant always** and is parked where `pod_at` says; Web Map & Transit adds **no other body, ever, in MVP** (lint `BODY_COUNT_MVP`): zero per-hop Class A allocation, a trivial restore, and the 4 / 3 / 4 body count of MSA-41 (home + A + B + pod before the weld, 3 after it, 4 after the one cut). Under Lighting's own definition (LO-24, LO:277: a body is **live** when it is built and has at least one non-dormant section or hosts the avatar) at most **two** bodies are live in every reachable MVP state (home plus one loaded derelict, or home plus the pod while it hosts the avatar during a leg), against `BODIES_LIVE_MAX` [PLACEHOLDER] 4 (range 2-8): a **margin of 2**. The *built* count (4 before the weld, 3 after it, 4 after the one cut) would equal the cap only under a "built" reading that Lighting does not use. ASK-LT1 is ANSWERED (Lighting states the definition). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* `room_at` on the pod answers `pod_cabin`, **not** NONE (BDY-29 makes NONE on a Layout body a defect-only state that CI asserts never occurs in a clean build, AC-BDY-29a). Air during a leg is Suit's hold (WM-17), not the Graph's. *Cites.* BDY-25, BDY-29; MSA-41 (MSA:333); DA-3; REG `BODIES_LIVE_MAX` [PLACEHOLDER]. *Pillar.* P1. *Decided.* R3, R4, R25.

**WM-6 — Pose discipline (ASK-B1 = BM7).** *Rule.* Body has no world-pose setter: pose rows start at zero and `world += vel` each tick (BDY-26), so without a setter every loaded body would sit at world (0, 0), bodies could not be separated, and `docked` could not be established. Web Map & Transit therefore asks Body for **BM7 `set_body_pose(body_id, world_x, world_y)`**, Class A, whose `can_set_pose` rejects `|coord|` above `WORLD_COORD_LIMIT` [PLACEHOLDER] *(BM7 now published by Body, BDY-21; ASK-B1; fills the slot Body's own Open Question 23 invited, BDY:1242, and subsumes re-origin, so no separate `rebase_world` exists)*. Poses are set only inside these applies, each one atomic: `init_world` (each body in its own `world_slot`, so no two bodies ever overlay); the departure apply (re-origin of the departure body, and **every body outside the leg re-posed to `init_pose(its slot) - init_pose(the departure slot)`**, so a dormant derelict is never left at an old approach pose and slot separation holds at every departure, not only at `init_world`, D.14) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; `pod_arrive` (the pod forced to `p1`, WM-13); `pod_settle` (the pod parked at the target, or the T5 reversal); the `berth` and `unberth` applies; and every `berth_step` (pose-carrying, WM-13). The re-origins (`init_world`, departure, berth start) happen under black or outside the visible field; the boundary re-poses differ from the integrated pose only by the expected slip. A re-origin is a pure translation of every body with live content by one common vector, so no relative offset between bodies that share a view changes (lint `REORIGIN_IS_TRANSLATION`). Poses of dormant bodies are never read by the simulation; the PROJECTOR reads them for the exterior silhouettes at both ends of a leg (WM-18, CF-AD-3). Leg and berth coordinates stay far inside `WORLD_COORD_LIMIT` [PLACEHOLDER] by construction; float32 canvas-offset exactness is `[UNV]` pending spike S2 (REG `WORLD_COORD_LIMIT`), so a failure there is cosmetic (exterior only) and never a simulation fault. **Fallback if Body refuses BM7:** the `PROJECTOR` builds exterior content only for the avatar's body and bodies `docked` to it (a presentation rule, no simulation change); `berth` becomes impossible and the design falls back to the derelict being pre-berthed at its world slot (losing "the wreck resolving out of the dark" for B only and making the leg to B a lie about distance, which is why BM7 is the recommended path). *Cites.* BDY-26, BDY-27, BDY:339; REG `WORLD_COORD_LIMIT` [PLACEHOLDER]. *Pillar.* P1. *Decided.* R8. *Gcd note.* The two authored sockets differ by `frame_shift_px` (696, 384), whose gcd is 24; no equal-tick constant-velocity phase with a speed bound of `VEL_MAX_PX_PER_TICK` [PLACEHOLDER] can reach that offset from a separated start, which is what forces BM7 rather than a clever path (derivation in Formulas).

**WM-7 — `init_world(world_seed)`.** *Rule.* Web Map & Transit owns `init_world` (kernel amendment K-2, accepted in KRN CR-18; SL-18 names it, DA:681 and BDY:339 repeat it). It runs in `LOADING` under one `WriteScope`, in `restore_after` order, with this fixed order (lint `INIT_WORLD_ORDER`): (1) Layout LM1 `build_section(template_id, kind)` x4 in `mvp_world.json` order (`home_ship`, `derelict_a`, `derelict_b`, `pod`; Graph M1 `instantiate_section` runs inside LM1, LY-25); (2) Emitter E1 for each section; (3) Interaction `place_loose` then `sync_section(home)`; (4) Body BM1 `place_actor(avatar, home body, spawn_cryo_1)` with `spawn_cryo_1` (216, 168) section-local px; (5) BM7 poses (WM-6); (6) M19 dormancy for A, B and the pod (ASK-G1, accepted; each flip is gated by the WM-24 rules, and no avatar is in A, B or the pod at this point) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; (7) Emitter E10 `set_dock_status(home dock section, on)` (the home dock's glow ON, every other dock glow stays OFF as E1 created them; WM-8b) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, then the block's records (HOME `status` BOARDED with `first_boarded_T` 0 because the crew wakes in it; A and B UNSEEN; `pod_at` HOME; `transit_state` IDLE). Any rejected call fails the whole build with `SL_NEW_GAME_NO_BASELINE`. `world_seed` is passed through and unused at MVP. Nothing is ever built after `init_world` (WM-24). *Cites.* SL-18 (SL:255), DA-26, AC-DA-98, BDY:339, LY-25. *Pillar.* P5 (the roster is never destroyed). *Decided.* R21.

#### B. The hop

**WM-8 — The pick and the console.** *Rule.* The **pick** is host-local presentation state of the Chart Table (undesigned): never simulation, never persisted, never assuming pause (KRN CR-16, KRN Open Question 6, PCI:78; pause is single-player only). The only simulation write is the PLAYER-origin command **`pod_hop{target_id}`** *(command vocabulary already lists `pod_hop`, KRN:85)* submitted by pressing the **pod console**: one of two Interaction CONSOLE subjects (`kind_hint` CONSOLE) distinguished by Layout's `console_role` (ASK-CT-13, ANSWERED 2026-10-28): the **POD** console (`pod_hop`, carry-admitted) anchored on the docked-podcraft prop in every dock room where the pod is parked, and the **DOCK** console (`berth`, `unberth`) anchored on the home airlock's prop only (WM-21: `berth` starts only from HOME); Interaction's object classes are POD_CONSOLE and DOCK_CONSOLE (IA-40); both `tool_required` NONE, each subject's `eligible` = this system's pure `console_ready(role)` predicate (`pod_at` equals this dock's record, machine `IDLE`, console in reach; and lit, for all three verbs: the `pod_hop` exemption (ASK-I2) stays REFUSED by Interaction, IA-6 and Interaction Open Question 31, and is not needed, because the console is lit by the docked pod's self-powered status glow (WM-8b), which counts as an ordinary lit fixture; `NOT_LIT` is therefore a backstop reason that is unreachable by construction at a pod console and the BS-19 invariant holds; user ruling, OQ-WM-39) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. For the DOCK subject `console_ready` additionally requires the dock to be HOME's. Within the DOCK subject the rows are selected by one more pure read published here, `berthed_present()` (true iff a derelict is berthed, from the persisted berth fields): `unberth` (priority 1, eligible iff `berthed_present`) and `berth` (priority 2, eligible iff not `berthed_present`), so Interaction's `select_verb` (IA-5) stays a function of (class, tool, eligible) and the plate shows `unberth` while a record is berthed (CT-10 R5). A DOCK press away from HOME cannot occur (no DOCK subject exists at a derelict dock, CT-10 R1b); through a test double it returns `NOT_DOCKED_HERE`. A POD press at HOME with a derelict berthed and no valid pick is routed to this system and returns `BERTH_PENDING` (WM-9, CT-10 R6). From a derelict the press carries HOME implicitly. *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* The prompt belongs to Interaction; no Web Map & Transit HUD element exists (guard rail 1). The Chart Table's confirm gesture may issue a resume then the console press; that is the Chart Table's design call (Chart Table designed). **Gap carried to Authoring/Layout:** Derelict Authoring authors the docked-podcraft prop (DA-6, kit gap K-3) but no pod-console declaration; the console is this GDD's requirement on the prop's `subject_anchors` entry (ASK-L3 to Authoring and Layout, OPEN, OQ-DA-39; ASK-I1 to Interaction, accepted). *Cites.* IA:131 (`pod_hop`, "Web Map's"), IA-37; DA-6; KRN CR-16; CT-10 R1b, R6; ASK-CT-13, ASK-CT-14 (ANSWERED 2026-10-28: Interaction draws the text-free pick echo, IA-40; this system holds no pick). *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* *Pillar.* P1 (the press is the commitment; the chart is a human instrument). *Decided.* R15.

**WM-8b — The docked pod lights its own console (user ruling, OQ-WM-39; closes the BS-19 dark-dock strand).** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *Rule.* Interaction's IA-6 stands unchanged ("No fourth exception for the pod console"; the `pod_hop` lit exemption ASK-I2 stays refused, so the Controller's `in_reach` lit conjunct is untouched). Instead **the docked-podcraft prop in every dock room carries a small self-powered status glow**: a nodeless (`node_ref` 0) FIXTURE of a new self-powered fixture class (Emitter State has since amended EM-3 and EM-11 to admit it: FIXTURE subkind DOCK_STATUS, self-powered, `node_ref` 0, one per dock room, created OFF by E1 and switched only by the SYSTEM mutator E10 `set_dock_status`; `set_switch` of either origin is rejected `NOT_SWITCHABLE` for it) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling G1)* that counts as an **ordinary lit fixture** for the console subject's `lit(o)` test. It is therefore **not** a fourth `act_gate` exception and Interaction changes nothing. (1) **On iff the prop is present (WM-33)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling G1)*: each dock room's one DOCK_STATUS glow record is created OFF at `init_world` (Emitter E1) and this system switches it with Emitter's E10 `set_dock_status(section_id, on)` **inside the apply that writes `pod_at`**: ON for the home dock in `init_world` step 7, OFF for the origin dock in the `pod_hop` departure apply (WM-10), ON for the target dock in `pod_settle` (WM-14; this includes the settle that ends a T5 reversed leg, WM-15), and **no call at `berth` or `unberth`** (the pod does not move, `pod_at` is unwritten, and the verbs run only at HOME where the home glow is already ON). After every apply `switch_on(glow(d)) == (pod_at == record(d))`, and the glow is OFF at every dock while the pod is IN_FLIGHT, so wherever a console can be pressed the glow is ON. The Emitter call is infallible in a validated apply (an unknown or glow-less section is a lint-prevented defect, Emitter E10), so it adds no rejection path. It draws from no Graph node, no battery and no chest-lamp cell, so it is lit with the dock room unpowered, the cell spent and no glowstick in the pack or thrown (the BS-19 strand cell, AC-WM-128). (2) **Every dock room places it**, the home airlock included (Derelict Authoring DA-46 placement rule; lints `DOCK_GLOW_LIT_AT_CONSOLE` and `DOCK_GLOW_ARRIVAL_DARK`, AC-WM-127a). Every CONSOLE anchor in the room is lit by it: the POD anchor in every dock room and, at the home airlock, the DOCK anchor (WM-8); `pod_hop`, `berth` and `unberth` are therefore all lit-gated by the one glow. *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* (3) **It lights the console subject only:** Emitter State's falloff (`DOCK_STATUS_BASE_PM`, `DOCK_STATUS_REACH_PX`; Emitter owns the peak, reach and falloff, this GDD owns none) lights the console anchor with margin, and the lint G2 of D.15 (Authoring's `DOCK_GLOW_ARRIVAL_DARK`) keeps the authored arrival cell below `LIT_OFF_PM`, so a dark derelict stays dark (darkness-first holds; the pod is the crew's one thread home) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling G1)*. (4) `NOT_LIT` stays in the reason set as a backstop (a malformed prop or a test double) and is **unreachable by construction at a pod console**. (5) **BS-19 is owned jointly:** this system guarantees the switch-on calls (the E10 call sites above, AC-WM-127b) and Emitter State owns the glow record and its numbers (EM-11, D.1b, I-DOCK); Derelict Authoring lints its placement (DA-46: `DOCK_GLOW_LIT_AT_CONSOLE` = G1, `DOCK_GLOW_ARRIVAL_DARK` = G2); Boarding's AC-BS-92 strand cell passes through AC-WM-128. (6) **Presentation separation:** the art-director keeps the glow's luminance separate from the visible-unknown residual lamp's band and the glow is not Tungsten (steady Tungsten is the residual lamp's, WM-18); the glow never enters WM-18's predicate and never sets `unknown_seen`. (7) The glow is a real emitter that counts as light and is never a tell carrier (Attention AT-20 and its `g_dock` term, bounded by `DOCK_GLOW_TERM_MAX`; Emitter EM-11); this system pushes no stimulus for it (WM-26). Attention's and Emitter's text calls the pod 'berthed' where it means docked at its dock room: it has nothing to do with this system's `berth` verb (CF-EM-2). *Cites.* IA-6; EM-3, EM-11, EM-13, EM:660; BS-19, AC-BS-92; DA-6, DA-23. *Pillar.* P5 (the escape verb is never lost to the dark), P4 darkness-first. *Decided.* user ruling 2026-10-27 (design-review decision D58, cross-cutting ruling X2); it reverses this GDD's own answer to ASK-BS-W2 (no console self-light) and accepts the previously declined ASK-BS-W3 (OQ-WM-38 (a)).

**WM-9 — Commands and reasons (closed sets, provisional).** *Rule.* All commands this system registers, with their origin and binding (registry: `web_commands`):

| Command | Origin | Registration (origin / actor_binding / actor_source / coalesce / max_age_ticks) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix, G6)* | Payload | Applies | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `pod_hop` | PLAYER | PLAYER; `OWNED_BY_ISSUER`; actor_source `body.state` (the avatar actor at the console, kernel CR-7); coalesce `NONE`; max_age_ticks 120 (`MAX_AGE_TICKS_ACTION`, kernel constant, cited) | `target_id` | P2 | aborts any channel (`ABORT(BODY)`); `carry_ref` travels |
| `berth` | PLAYER | PLAYER; `OWNED_BY_ISSUER`; actor_source `body.state`; coalesce `NONE`; max_age_ticks 120 (`MAX_AGE_TICKS_ACTION`) | `derelict_id` | P2 | new verb (in the kernel's row 8 vocabulary, ASK-K accepted); WM-21; `noise_class` LOUD in Interaction's declaration (IA-33, IA-40); the one push at the apply is Interaction's, this system makes 0 calls (WM-26, OQ-WM-40) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling G1)* |
| `unberth` *(ruled, checkpoint 2026-10-26)* | PLAYER | PLAYER; `OWNED_BY_ISSUER`; actor_source `body.state`; coalesce `NONE`; max_age_ticks 120 (`MAX_AGE_TICKS_ACTION`) | none | P2 | the reverse of `berth`; WM-22; reasons `BUSY`, `IN_TRANSIT`, `OUT_OF_RANGE`, `NOT_LIT`, `NOT_DOCKED_HERE`, `NOT_BERTHED`, `SOCKET_NOT_FREE`; **never `FUEL_LOW`** (C14); `noise_class` QUIET at MVP (WM-26) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `pod_arrive` | SYSTEM | SYSTEM only; `actor_binding NONE`, no actor_source; coalesce `NONE`; max_age_ticks 120 (`MAX_AGE_TICKS_ACTION`, cited; the commands are idempotent and epoch-guarded, so a `STALE` reject is simply re-decided by the transit hook, CR-9 owner rule) [PLACEHOLDER] | `leg_epoch` | T+1 | `allowed_origins {SYSTEM}`, `actor_binding NONE` (the `attention_arm_tell` pattern, AT-21); a PLAYER attempt is `ORIGIN_NOT_ALLOWED` |
| `pod_settle` | SYSTEM | SYSTEM only; `actor_binding NONE`, no actor_source; coalesce `NONE`; max_age_ticks 120 (`MAX_AGE_TICKS_ACTION`, cited; re-decided by the transit hook on `STALE`) [PLACEHOLDER] | `leg_epoch` | T+1 | same pattern |
| `berth_step` | SYSTEM | SYSTEM only; `actor_binding NONE`, no actor_source; coalesce `NONE`; max_age_ticks 120 (`MAX_AGE_TICKS_ACTION`, cited; re-decided by the transit hook on `STALE`) [PLACEHOLDER] | `berth_epoch`, `phase` | T+1 | same pattern |
| `web_reconcile` | SYSTEM | SYSTEM only; `actor_binding NONE`, no actor_source; coalesce `NONE`; max_age_ticks 120 (`MAX_AGE_TICKS_ACTION`, cited; re-decided by the transit hook on `STALE`) [PLACEHOLDER] | `record_ids` | T+1 | same pattern; idempotent |

These are the declarations kernel CR-6 requires with no silent default: a PLAYER type with `OWNED_BY_ISSUER` and no actor_source, or a `NONE` type that declares one, fails boot with `ACTOR_SOURCE_INVALID` naming the type; an omitted coalesce or max_age_ticks fails boot. The actor of `pod_hop`, `berth` and `unberth` is the avatar's Body actor_id, so the P2 authority check is (`body.state`, actor) in `half_b[PlayerId(issuer_peer)]` (CR-7); Interaction declares the console rows (IA-40) but Web Map registers the types, so these fields are Web Map's. *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix, G6)*

`pod_hop` is validated in this reason order, **cheapest and most informative first** (the first failing reason is the answer; none changes state): `BUSY` (an ASSEMBLY weld channel is open on the home body, or the seat's Controller state cannot act: STAGGERED, DOWNED, DEAD or already IN_POD (the IN_POD conjunct is a read of the Controller's `loco_state` as committed at the end of the previous tick, i.e. as visible at the start of P2 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: a second press on the SAME tick as an applying hop sees the pre-hop state and is `IN_TRANSIT`, AC-WM-24; a press on any later tick by the lone traveller in the pod is `BUSY`, AC-WM-21); any other open channel is *aborted* by the hop, not refused. Carrying a module (`carry_ref` set) is never a `BUSY` cause for `pod_hop` *(Interaction IA-2 / IA-28 admit `pod_hop` on the pod CONSOLE while carrying, registry `admitted_while_carrying`; ASK-I1 and C-BS-1 closed; the matching Controller PC-8 carve-out is routed to the Controller owner)*), `IN_TRANSIT` (machine not `IDLE`), `OUT_OF_RANGE`, `NOT_LIT` (both owned by Interaction's eligibility and returned under this system's names as backstops, because no prompt exists when they hold; **`NOT_LIT` is returned for `pod_hop`, `berth` and `unberth` alike** when the console is unlit: Interaction refused the `pod_hop` exemption (ASK-I2, IA-6, Interaction Open Question 31), but the console is lit by the docked pod's self-powered status glow (WM-8b), so a press at a pod console is never refused `NOT_LIT` and the escape verb cannot be lost to the dark; the reason is a backstop, exercised through a test double; user ruling OQ-WM-39) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*, `NOT_DOCKED_HERE` (`pod_at` is not this dock), `BERTH_PENDING` **for a payload-less `pod_hop`** (`target_id` absent or NONE) **while an unwelded derelict is berthed** (necessarily with the pod at HOME, WM-22: no hop leaves HOME while berthed; Chart Table CT-10 row R6 relies on this so the plate shows the berth-pending words) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*, then `TARGET_UNKNOWN` (a payload-less `pod_hop` with nothing berthed still returns `TARGET_UNKNOWN`, a diagnostic path: the Chart Table opens the chart instead, CT-10 R3; a payload-less press from a derelict carries HOME implicitly (WM-8) and never reaches either rule), `TARGET_ABSORBED` *(ruled, checkpoint 2026-10-26; a welded-in or orphaned record; the Chart Table never offers it, so it is a diagnostic path, never shown)*, `TARGET_HERE`, `TARGET_BERTHED` (a hop to the hull-to-hull berthed derelict: it is alongside, and boarding it waits for the weld), `BERTH_PENDING` (second position: a payload naming any other derelict while an unwelded derelict is berthed, unchanged: this keeps the reachable loaded sets exactly S0 to S4; the restated full `pod_hop` order is BUSY, IN_TRANSIT, OUT_OF_RANGE, NOT_LIT, NOT_DOCKED_HERE, BERTH_PENDING (payload-less form), TARGET_UNKNOWN, TARGET_ABSORBED, TARGET_HERE, TARGET_BERTHED, BERTH_PENDING (payload form), FUEL_LOW, ARRIVAL_BLOCKED, CAPACITY; the `berth` and `unberth` orders are unchanged) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*, `FUEL_LOW` (WM-20; HOME-origin only), `ARRIVAL_BLOCKED` (the authored arrival cell fails Body's `can_transfer`, WM-15), `CAPACITY` (a hard runtime cap owned by another system would be exceeded by loading the target, via a pure preview; none is reachable at MVP, and the Interaction fixture budget is a lint on registered neighbourhoods, not a cap: S4's 145 subjects are an INFO upper bound with every registered neighbourhood at or under 120, so there is no overrun, D.9, E-44, OQ-WM-27) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*; the ordered-refusal tests AC-WM-10 and AC-WM-39 exercise it only through a capacity test double) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*, `POD_NOT_READY` (**reserved, never returned at MVP**: Onboarding's later repair gate). `berth` adds `NOT_BOARDED` (any target that is not a BOARDED DERELICT: the wrong kind, UNSEEN, SEEN, ABSORBED, ORPHAN or an unknown id), `NO_MATE_SOCKET`, `SOCKET_NOT_FREE`, `ALREADY_BERTHED`, validated in the total order `BUSY`, `IN_TRANSIT`, `OUT_OF_RANGE`, `NOT_LIT`, `NOT_DOCKED_HERE`, `NOT_BOARDED`, `NO_MATE_SOCKET`, `SOCKET_NOT_FREE`, `ALREADY_BERTHED`, `FUEL_LOW`, `CAPACITY` (`NO_MATE_SOCKET` before `SOCKET_NOT_FREE`, both from the one `can_attach` preview; OQ-WM-19 closed) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, and shares `BUSY`, `IN_TRANSIT`, `OUT_OF_RANGE`, `NOT_LIT`, `NOT_DOCKED_HERE`, `FUEL_LOW`, `CAPACITY`. `unberth` (ruled, checkpoint 2026-10-26) shares `BUSY`, `IN_TRANSIT`, `OUT_OF_RANGE`, `NOT_LIT`, `NOT_DOCKED_HERE` and adds `NOT_BERTHED` and `SOCKET_NOT_FREE`; it has **no `FUEL_LOW` path and no fuel floor** (never strand: a floor on `unberth` would bring back the soft lock it exists to remove), its status effects never regress B (B stays BOARDED) and `UNBERTHED` is emitted unconditionally. The restore reasons are `WEB_RESTORE_VERSION`, `WEB_RESTORE_UNKNOWN_RECORD`, `WEB_RESTORE_EPOCH`, `WEB_RESTORE_POD_BODY`, `WEB_RESTORE_STATE`, `WEB_RESTORE_LEG_INEXACT`, `WEB_RESTORE_BERTH_INEXACT`, `WEB_RESTORE_LOADED_SET` (a live derelict where the persisted machine allows none, E-29) (WM-30) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. The dormancy-guard defect reasons are `WEB_DORMANCY_HOME` and `WEB_DORMANCY_AVATAR` (WM-24). A SYSTEM command whose `leg_epoch` or `berth_epoch` is stale or whose state does not match is **not** a fault: it is rejected `STALE_EPOCH` / `NOT_IN_LEG` / `NOT_ARRIVING` and counted at DEBUG (`transit_stale_cmds`), never `FAULTED` (a resubmitted command whose twin already applied is expected). The refusal reasons are **truth-only** surfaces (ASK-F1, Player Fantasy). *Cites.* KRN CR-5, CR-7; IA:131. *Pillar.* P1, P4. *Decided.* R15; OR-5.

**WM-10 — The departure apply (`pod_hop`).** *Rule.* After validation, one atomic apply in the kernel's fixed order Graph, then Layout, then Body (KRN:197; no rollback, so everything fallible is in `validate` or is a Class B clamp):
1. **Graph.** `granted := M16 withdraw_store(fuel tank, HOP_FUEL_ML)` [PLACEHOLDER] iff the origin is HOME, else nothing (WM-20). If the origin is a derelict: M19 `set_section_dormant(origin, true)` (ASK-G1, accepted) sets the origin's section dormant, gated by the WM-24 validate rules (skip if already dormant, HOME-kind guard, avatar-present guard, `SEAM_ACTIVE`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **with the avatar-present guard evaluated over the origin body's occupancy excluding the issuing traveller, whom BM5 moves out in step 3 of this same apply (WM-24 (3)); a second living actor aboard still refuses the hop `WEB_DORMANCY_AVATAR`**, and Interaction `sync_section` unregisters its subjects (ASK-I1, accepted, IA-37) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. HOME is never made dormant (home keeps running while the crew is away; the Graph's clocks run whether or not the player acts).
2. **Layout.** Nothing is built, nothing is discarded (WM-24; this corrects Layout's offer, CONFLICT row CF-LY).
3. **Body.** BM5 `transfer_actor(avatar, pod body, seat_pod_1, cause POD)`; BM7 `set_body_pose` x3 plus one per remaining body (departure body re-origin, the pod at the leg's start, the target at the leg's authored approach pose, and every other body re-posed to its slot-relative pose, WM-6; at most four calls at MVP) (ASK-B1, accepted, BDY-21) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; BM6 `set_body_velocity(pod body, vx, vy)` with a whole-pixel velocity.
4. **Record.** `transit_state := LEG`, `leg_epoch += 1`, `t0 := T`, `target_id`, `origin_id := the record pod_at held` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, `leg_dir` (OUT from HOME, BACK to HOME), the active leg's parameters, `pod_at := IN_FLIGHT`; Emitter E10 `set_dock_status(the origin dock section, off)` in this same apply (the pod has left, so the origin dock's glow goes OFF; with `pod_at` IN_FLIGHT no dock glow is ON; WM-8b) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*.
5. **Notify.** `POD_DEPARTED` (WM-31).
The player is now `IN_POD`: locomotion intents are applied but ignored, the avatar is not drawn, and the pod is a leg's worth of transport, not a safe room: the statuses of the hold/run table (WM-17b) keep running, and a death in the pod is the Controller's DEAD (user ruling, OQ-WM-41) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. *Cites.* KRN:197, CR-9; BDY-25; PCI:173, :191. *Pillar.* P2 (the escape verb removes the player from danger and starts none). *Decided.* R2, R10, R12.

**WM-11 — The leg is a script.** *Rule.* From `t0`, Body's `body.integrate` adds the pod's whole-pixel velocity to its pose once per tick, `n` times (BDY-9 puts the pose step before actors; a BM6 applied at P2 of tick `t0` is integrated at P3 of the same tick). The pod leg is **exact**: after `n` integrations the pod is at `p1 = p0 + v x n` (formula `pod_leg`, three binding bounds: velocity at most `VEL_MAX_PX_PER_TICK` [PLACEHOLDER], exactness per axis, coordinates at most `WORLD_COORD_LIMIT` [PLACEHOLDER]; registered as formula `pod_leg`, D.1). `transit.step` (P3, after `body.integrate`) evaluates one **idempotent** rule every run: *if the state is LEG and `T >= t0 + n - 1` and no `pod_arrive` for this `leg_epoch` has applied, submit SYSTEM `pod_arrive{leg_epoch}`.* It is resubmitted until it applies, because a pending SYSTEM command is dropped on restore (WM-30). The hook writes **no state** and calls **no Class A mutator** (KRN CR-9: a hook reaches another block only through a Class B mutator or a T+1 SYSTEM command). There is no steering, no hazard, no encounter, no stimulus, no random event and no random delay: the leg takes the same time every time (the web's distances do not change, and a hop never produces an unwarned harm). The leg's data (`p0`, velocity, `n`, the approach pose) is **content** in `assets/data/world/legs.json`, linted on load; the return leg is the exact time reverse of the outbound leg, so it needs no extra authored data. *Cites.* BDY-9, BDY-26; KRN CR-9; REG `VEL_MAX_PX_PER_TICK` [PLACEHOLDER]. *Pillar.* P2, P1. *Decided.* R2, R7.

**WM-12 — Why arrival is a command, not a hook call.** *Rule (stated so the kernel reviewer need not ask).* BM5, BM6, BM7 and the dormancy mutator are Class A (validate-then-apply). A step hook may not call them. Arrival is therefore **two SYSTEM commands applied at P2 of tick T+1** (`pod_arrive`, `pod_settle`; WM-14), and a berth phase change is a third (`berth_step`; WM-21). They carry `allowed_origins {SYSTEM}` and no actor binding, so a PLAYER attempt is `ORIGIN_NOT_ALLOWED`. The hook's only outputs are those submissions; its only inputs are reads. *Cites.* KRN CR-9 (tail, KRN:104), KRN:200. *Pillar.* P1 (one writer per fact). *Decided.* R2.

**WM-13 — Phase-boundary commands are pose-carrying and idempotent (OR-3).** *Rule.* Every SYSTEM command at a phase boundary (`pod_arrive`, `pod_settle`, `berth_step`) applies the **absolute end pose** of the phase it closes (`BM7`) and the next velocity (`BM6`) in the same apply; the pose is derived from persisted data (the active leg's parameters, WM-3; the payload carries epochs only, C8). Consequence: the world lands on the authored path regardless of command latency or loss. The arithmetic, from locked KRN and Body text: a SYSTEM command queued at tick `S` dispatches at `S+1`; a restore sends `Dropped(RELOAD)` to every pending command and the first tick after `RUNNING` sees an empty queue. A snapshot taken after tick `S = t0 + n - 1` (the hook has already submitted `pod_arrive`) therefore restores into a world where the command is gone; `body.integrate` at `S+1` moves the pod once more (it has now integrated `n + 1` times), the hook resubmits at `S+1` and the command applies at `S+2`. Without pose-carrying the pod would stop at `p1 + v`; with it the apply sets the pose to `p1`. **The expected slip is exactly `v x (T_apply - (t0 + n))` for any apply delay** (checkpoint 2026-10-26, C8): a restore drop costs one step as above, and a kernel queue delay of a SYSTEM command (up to +8 ticks, KRN D.4) costs up to eight; both are legitimate and are logged at DEBUG as `EXPECTED_RESTORE_SLIP` (hidden by the pod-local camera and by the black hold that follows). `LEG_POSE_MISMATCH` is raised **iff the observed pod offset from `p1` differs from that value**, and the pose is forced to `p1` by BM7 either way. The same rule governs `berth_step` (the expected slip is `v_i x (T_apply - (t_phase + n_i))` for the phase it closes). **The pose is always derived from persisted parameters** (the active leg's `{n, vx, vy, p0x, p0y}` or the active berth's phases, WM-3, OQ-WM-7); the payload carries `leg_epoch` / `berth_epoch` and `phase` only, never a pose. If BM7 is refused none of the exact leg and berth rules hold (WM-6 fallback). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Boundary values are keyed to nominal ticks, so a late apply persists nothing.** `arrive_T := t0 + n`, the settle tick `arrive_T + ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS`, `first_boarded_T` and each `t_phase := t_phase + n_i` are the NOMINAL ticks of the schedule, never the tick at which the (possibly restore-dropped, possibly queue-delayed) SYSTEM command happened to apply. A late apply (lag `k = T_apply - nominal`: 1 after a restore on an empty queue; `a - 1` <= 7 extra ticks per submit under queue delay, and `SLIP_MAX_TICKS` 15 with one restore, D.2; each further restore at the same boundary adds up to `QUEUE_AGE_MAX` ticks) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* therefore changes only the wall tick of the work, not any persisted Web Map & Transit or Body value, and the pose it sets is the **nominal trajectory at `T_apply`** (the closing phase's end pose plus the next phase's velocity times `k`; for `pod_arrive` and the last `berth_step` the next velocity is 0, so the pose is the end pose), which makes the restored world rejoin the uninterrupted one exactly. A late apply can still show in blocks that clock on where the avatar is during the `k` lag ticks (in the restored run the avatar is still `IN_POD`; in the uninterrupted run it is already aboard the target). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* They arise **only when the `pod_settle` apply is late** (Case B: a restore that drops the pending `pod_settle`, a snapshot after tick `arrive_T + ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS - 1`, or a kernel queue delay on the settle submit; a snapshot that drops only `pod_arrive` leaves the settle on its nominal tick and has none, States and Transitions, D.2, AC-WM-15a/b).
