Three bounded accruals are the **named exclusions**, stated numerically in AC-WM-54 (b): the Graph's divisor-6 loaded clocks on the target section (at most `ceil(k / 6)` steps, until M19 takes an `as_of_tick`, ASK-G1b, OPEN), Suit's thermal load and hold counters (the `IN_POD` ambient band versus the target section's band, both running after the OQ-WM-41 ruling, at most `ceil(k / 6)` runs) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* and Sanity's level (`aboard` false versus true, at most `ceil(k / 12)` accruals of `ABOARD_EXPOSURE`). Transient differences (the Controller's `IN_POD` exit one step late, Emitter re-arming) are gone by T* + 12, `T*` being the first tick at which both runs are IDLE. *Cites.* KRN edge cases (`Dropped(RELOAD)`, the empty first queue); BDY-9, BDY-26. *Pillar.* P1. *Decided.* OR-3 (adopts the systems-designer's SD-3 over the game-designer's "defect" reading).

**WM-14 — Arrival: `pod_arrive` then `pod_settle` (OR-1).** *Rule.* **`pod_arrive{leg_epoch}`** applies at `t0 + n`: BM7 sets the pod pose to `p1` and BM6 sets velocity 0; `transit_state := ARRIVING`; `arrive_T := t0 + n` (the NOMINAL tick, not the apply tick, WM-13); the target's `status` goes UNSEEN to SEEN if it is currently UNSEEN (it **never regresses**: a BOARDED record stays BOARDED on a return visit; "unconditionally" is against the lamp condition, not against the current status; the hull has resolved out of the dark) and, separately, `unknown_seen := true` iff the residual lamp is lit by WM-18's predicate at that tick (monotone: it is only ever set, never cleared; the target is dormant, so its stock and node state are identical at the nominal tick and at a late apply) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* (never from what the camera framed, which varies with the zoom ladder; the simulation never reads presentation). The target is **still dormant** and the avatar is **still in the pod**. During `ARRIVING` the `PROJECTOR` builds the target's scene content from static Layout data plus the Graph's persisted state, under the Pipeline's `TRANSIT_HOLD` black (it may pre-build during the leg, which gives it `HOP_LEG_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] ticks; -> ADR candidate). Exactly `ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] later `transit.step` submits **`pod_settle{leg_epoch}`** (condition `transit_state == ARRIVING` **and** `T >= arrive_T + ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS - 1` [PLACEHOLDER], the same idempotent resubmit rule; the ARRIVING conjunct makes the deferral explicit: the hook defers, never drops, until the `pod_arrive` that opens ARRIVING has applied, and lint L3b below guarantees the order) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, which applies: re-validate the arrival cell with `can_transfer` (WM-15); clear the target's dormancy through M19 with `as_of_tick := arrive_T + ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS` (ASK-G1 accepted; the `as_of_tick` parameter is ASK-G1b, OPEN) and re-sync its Interaction subjects (ASK-I1, accepted) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; BM5 transfers the avatar from the pod to the target body at the authored `arrive_*` cell; BM7 parks the pod beside the target dock (not drawn, WM-33); `transit_state := IDLE`; `pod_at := target`; Emitter E10 `set_dock_status(the target dock section, on)` in the same apply (WM-8b) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; on the first `pod_settle` at a record `status` goes SEEN to BOARDED and `first_boarded_T := arrive_T + ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS` (the nominal tick); notify `POD_ARRIVED` (and `DERELICT_LOADED`). Total time in the pod is `n + ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] on the nominal schedule (after a restore the avatar leaves the pod body `lag` ticks later, WM-13). **"Destination ready" is state, never a call:** `web.view.destination_ready := (transit_state == IDLE)`, a pure function of the persisted machine state alone (checkpoint 2026-10-26, C2): it is false through `ARRIVING` and becomes true from the `pod_settle` apply tick, before P5 Notify, which is the tick the avatar has actually left the pod body. The `- 1` appears **only** in the hook's submit condition (`T >= arrive_T + ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS - 1` [PLACEHOLDER], which queues `pod_settle` for T+1) and never in `destination_ready`, so the Pipeline can never cut to the avatar while the avatar is still on the pod body. The Pipeline ends `TRANSIT_HOLD` when that holds **and** its own projector-local readiness latch holds. If the projector is late, the simulation has already settled and the avatar stands on the destination for the late frames under black; this is acceptable because the target was frozen until `pod_settle` and the fairness window `W_MIN_WARNING_S` (10 s = 600 ticks) dwarfs the worst build lag (at most a few frames: the largest single build is B's 149 tiles, one frame at `BUILD_TILES_PER_FRAME` 256 (LY Tuning Knobs, LY:1041; not a registry entry), and building home and B together would be ceil(331 / 256) = 2), and a projector-side `ARRIVE_SCENE_LATE` diagnostic is logged. *(orchestrator ruling OR-1: this departs from the literal wording of R12 and R26 and from the game-designer's own flag "not ruling-compliant"; it amends R12 and R26 (ruled, checkpoint 2026-10-26), OQ-WM-1, and is a CONFLICT-clarification against KRN:200 and PIPE C10 (CF-KRN, CF-PIPE).)* *Cites.* KRN:200, KRN conformance ("`PRESENTATION` and `PROJECTOR` have no submit path"), KRN Open Question 1; PIPE C10, CR-10; REG `W_MIN_WARNING_S`; `BUILD_TILES_PER_FRAME` 256 (LY Tuning Knobs, LY:1041; not a registry entry); lint `ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] >= ceil(largest single-target tiles / `BUILD_TILES_PER_FRAME` 256 (LY Tuning Knobs, LY:1041; not a registry entry)) frames + margin (checkpoint OQ-WM-1). *Pillar.* P2. *Decided.* R2 (ARRIVING), R28; OR-1.

**WM-15 — The arrival cell and the recovery branch.** *Rule.* The arrival cell is the authored `arrive_*` cell of the target's `entry_room_key` room: `arrive_a_dock` (168, 336), `arrive_b_dock` (168, 312), `arrive_home_airlock` (552, 696), all section-local px (DA table); each is one tile clear of the docked-podcraft prop (lint `SPAWN_BLOCKED`). It is **never searched** (BDY:703-706): it is validated clear by `can_transfer` at departure (`ARRIVAL_BLOCKED`) and re-validated in `pod_settle`. Because the target is frozen during the leg, a re-check failure is a **defect**, and the deterministic recovery is: the `pod_settle` apply takes a recovery branch (it is total; a SYSTEM command that rejected would leave the machine stuck) that starts **one reversal** to the departure body at no fuel cost, with `leg_epoch += 1`, `leg_dir` reversed and `ARRIVAL_RECHECK_FAILED` logged; a second failure on the reversal places the avatar at the authored cell of the body it is arriving at with no re-check and logs `ARRIVAL_FORCED`. This bounds the machine to at most one reversal per hop (the reversed leg is marked by the persisted `leg_reversed`, so the second failure is recognisable after a restore). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **The T5 apply list (the recovery branch, in order; nothing in it can reject).** (1) **Dormancy: no M19 call.** The target stays dormant (it was dormant through the leg and ARRIVING) and the departure body's dormancy is untouched; the dormancy clear of the normal `pod_settle` (WM-14) is never reached on this branch, which is what stops the target going live with the avatar not aboard. (2) **Body:** no BM5 (the avatar stays on the pod body); BM7 sets the pod pose to the nominal trajectory of the reversed leg at the apply tick (`p1` plus the reversed velocity times the lag `k`, WM-13); BM7 re-poses the departure body to the reversed leg's authored approach pose (D.3, the exact time reverse) and every other body to its slot-relative pose (WM-6); BM6 sets the pod velocity to `-v` (whole pixels). (3) **Record:** `t0 := arrive_T + ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS` (the NOMINAL settle tick, never the apply tick, WM-13); `leg_epoch += 1`; `leg_dir` reversed; `target_id` and `origin_id` swapped; the active leg's parameters replaced by the time reverse `{n, -vx, -vy, p1x, p1y}` (so `p1' == p0' + v' x n` still holds for `post_restore`, WM-30); `leg_reversed := 1`; `transit_state := LEG`; `pod_at` stays IN_FLIGHT; the target's `status` is **not** advanced to BOARDED and `first_boarded_T` is **not** written (the first successful `pod_settle` at a record writes them). (4) **Emitter:** no E10 call; every dock glow stays OFF through the reversed leg and the destination dock's glow turns ON at the reversed leg's own `pod_settle` (WM-8b). (5) **Notify:** `ARRIVAL_RECHECK_FAILED` logged; no `POD_ARRIVED`, no `DERELICT_LOADED`; `POD_DEPARTED` is not re-emitted. (6) **Fuel:** none, and the hop's fuel is not refunded. `destination_ready` stays false (the machine is LEG), the reversed leg restores like any leg, and a re-check failure inside the reversed leg's own `pod_settle` (`leg_reversed` 1) takes the forced placement `ARRIVAL_FORCED` instead of a second reversal. AC-WM-135 checks the list and the `web.state` validity after the reversal. **Arrival safety is geometry-only.** `ARRIVAL_BLOCKED` and the re-check test Body's `can_transfer` (the authored cell is free and inside its section) and nothing else: they say nothing about the cell's air, temperature, fire, hull breach or any other Graph hazard. A BACK leg can therefore land the crew in a depressurised or breached home dock room; that is legal (the hop removes the player from a derelict's danger, not from the home ship's, which keeps running, WM-26b), `ARRIVAL_BLOCKED` is a geometric reason and is never raised for a hazard, and the hazard the arrival meets is the one the Graph's and Attention's own tells already gave. AC-WM-120 covers a return into a depressurised dock. Two crewmates on one cell is legal (BDY-17), so a forced placement is never a crash. *Cites.* BDY-17, BDY-25, BDY:703-706; DA table; DA-3. *Pillar.* P2 (never an unwarned lethal arrival). *Decided.* R26; recovery rule from the systems-designer (SD-18).

**WM-16 — `IN_POD` and the Controller.** *Rule.* The Controller enters `IN_POD` on `pod_hop` `Applied` at the console (PCI:191) and leaves it in the first step in which the avatar's `body_of_actor` is no longer the pod body, a pure Body read, **not** by a call from a Web Map & Transit hook. This is a clarification of PCI:192 ("arrival body transfer complete (Transit hook)"), because the Controller's block is not in `restore_after` (WM-2) and an event-driven exit could desync from the world block on restore. `transit.step` reconciles by read only: if the machine is LEG or ARRIVING the traveller's `body_of_actor` must be the pod body (`WEB_RESTORE_STATE` if not, at restore). **ASK-C1 (accepted, PCI States table)**: derive `IN_POD` from body occupancy, so that derived state cannot desync. `controller.step` runs before `transit.step` in the tick, so it sees the new occupancy one tick late, which is harmless. *Cites.* PCI:173, :191-192, :694. *Pillar.* P1. *Decided.* R28 correction; R1. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**WM-17 — Air in the pod (Suit's `POD_RUN`, formerly `HOLD_ROOM`; ASK-S1).** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R6)* *Rule.* The pod's Graph section is dormant and is never breathed. While the avatar is `IN_POD`, Suit makes **no `draw_air` call** (M15 on a dormant section is a no-op that returns 0 and would otherwise raise `breath_starved`, LSG:806) and evaluates the seat against the **pod cabin rule** (Suit SE-3 (i), SE-35, the `POD_RUN` row): a small sealed cabin with no breathable air of its own (`band_o2` ANOXIC, so the suit's reserve is the only air), a bearable and unchanging temperature (`band_t` COMFORT with no gain: the thermal load only relaxes) and no light but the player's own lamp. So the **reserve drains** (`d` per Suit run, `R_BREATH` 300 mGU on an intact suit), `breath_src` is RESERVE, the **cell drains while the beam is lit**, `breath_starved` stays 0 and no GASP cue fires (an AIR_NONE pair is never HYPOXIC, so GASP is unreachable). *Resolved 2026-10-27 — delegated ruling R6 (re-review; user delegated to best judgment): Suit's reading of user ruling D60 ('statuses run in the pod') stands; this GDD's earlier HOLD reading of the reserve and the cell is deleted and ASK-S2 is closed on this side.* The valve click plays **only on the source transition edge** (once when `breath_src` becomes RESERVE on entry, once at disengage on arrival), never per run (Suit SE-35, AC-SE-36a, AC-SE-87), so a leg is not a stream of clicks (Pillar 2). The mechanism is **changed from the brief**: the brief's fix of making `room_at` answer NONE on the pod kind is rejected because it contradicts BDY-29 (CI asserts NONE never occurs on a Layout body in a clean build). Instead **ASK-S1 (accepted, SE:341, now the `POD_RUN` row at SE:354)** keys the pod case on `loco_state == IN_POD`, whatever `room_at` answers. **Time in the pod and the reserve cost** are Suit's to derive and are quoted here, not re-derived (Suit D.1 pod derivation, registry values `HOP_LEG_TICKS` 900, `ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS` 45, `IN_POD_SLACK_TICKS` 30): the occupant's time in the pod is **945 ticks nominally**, at most **975** with the slack, **2,265** at `HOP_LEG_TICKS_MAX` with the default settle, and the registry sum-lint ceiling is 2,400; `suit.vitals` runs every 6th tick, so a 945-tick window is 157 or 158 runs (162 or 163 for 975; 400 exactly for 2,400), costing **47,100 mGU = 15.7 s = 17.4 %** of a full reserve at the nominal leg (up to 47,400 at 158 runs). A nominal or slack leg cannot reach FAILED (fewer than 250 runs); the lethality table by boarding reserve is Suit's AC-SE-94. Suit's build assertion A15 (the reserve covers two legs) is what keeps a boarding at full reserve from being lethal; AC-WM-23 below is a consequence of it, not of a hold. **Required acceptance criterion:** across a full leg `breath_starved` stays 0, no GASP cue fires and the reserve falls by Suit's figure (AC-WM-23). The lint `HOP_LEG_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] + `ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] + `IN_POD_SLACK_TICKS` <= 2400 stays as the registry's sum-lint ceiling: it bounds the reserve cost of one leg at 400 runs x 300 = 120,000 mGU (44.4 % of `RESERVE_MAX_MGU`, Suit D.1) and keeps `HOP_LEG_TICKS` at or under `HOP_LEG_TICKS_MAX` 2190. ASK-S1 is a wording alignment of Suit's pod row with Suit's SE:344 (which already keys the pod case on `IN_POD`) and is **not optional**, like ASK-G1 and ASK-B1. The pod section is dormant always (WM-5); a live cabin is not a fallback. The remaining Layout question (`kind` and the `fixtures_min` exemption for the `pod` template, OQ-DA-17) is ASK-L1 and no longer touches air. *Cites.* SE-3, SE-35 (SE:259), SE:344, SE:354, SE OQ-32, OQ-48; BDY-29; LSG:806; REG `R_BREATH` [PLACEHOLDER], `HOP_LEG_TICKS_MAX`. *Pillar.* P1, P2. *Decided.* R4; delegated ruling R6. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**WM-17b — What runs and what holds in the pod (hold/run table; resolved 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions): statuses run in the pod, and a death in the pod is the Controller's DEAD; OQ-WM-41).** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* The pod is a leg's worth of transport, not a safe room. The earlier claim that "nothing can hurt a pod occupant" was backed only for air and is withdrawn. The table states, per ticking status, its owner and what happens while the seat is `IN_POD`. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R6)* **No `draw_air` is ever issued** (WM-17: the pod section is dormant and never breathed, and a `draw_air` on it would raise a false `breath_starved`, a lie by commission), **but the reserve and the cell still run against Suit's pod cabin rule**; **every status runs**, so a seat that boards the pod already hurt, cold or breached can still go down in it. *Resolved 2026-10-27 — delegated ruling R6 (re-review; user delegated to best judgment): the HOLD reading of the reserve and cell is deleted from this table and ASK-S2 is closed; Suit's `POD_RUN` stands.* This reverses WM-10, WM-32, E-33 and AC-WM-93 as first written; Suit already conforms (SE-35, the `POD_RUN` row) and the Controller side is ASK-PC-1.

| Status | Owner | In the pod (ruled) | Basis | Criterion |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| O2 reserve and suit cell | Suit | RUN: no `draw_air` (the pod section is dormant), the reserve drains `d` per Suit run with `breath_src` RESERVE (47,100 mGU = 17.4 % over a nominal 945-tick leg, 157 or 158 runs), the cell drains while the beam is lit; the valve click on the entry and disengage edges only. Pod fiction, once: a sealed cabin with no breathable air (ANOXIC), a bearable unchanging temperature (COMFORT, no gain) and no light but the player's lamp | WM-17, SE-35 (`POD_RUN`), Suit D.1 pod derivation; delegated ruling R6 | AC-WM-23 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R6)* |
| Thermal load and the hold counters | Suit | RUN against Suit's pod cabin rule (`band_t` COMFORT with no gain, so the thermal load only relaxes; the holds advance; the value is Suit's, this system supplies none); a band that reaches harm is the Suit's own harm channel | OQ-WM-41 ruling; SE-35 (ASK-S2 closed, delegated ruling R6) | AC-WM-121 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R6)* |
| Suit integrity (exogenous damage, patches) | Suit / Interaction | no spatial hazard or harm source exists in the pod (the section is dormant, there is one seat, nothing else is aboard); integrity changes only through the running statuses above | WM-17b, E-33 | AC-WM-93a *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| Sanity level | Sanity | RUNS at Attention's `held_ring`, no `ABOARD_EXPOSURE`, recovery `REC_IDLE` | SN-5 E1, EC-SN-58 | AC-WM-121 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| Attention TELL and BREACH clocks | Attention | RUN: the hop does not interrupt a tell; the due run voids it when a non-home locus is vacated (AT-22) | AT-22, WM-26 | AC-WM-105 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| Controller STAGGERED, DOWNED and the DOWNED-to-DEAD timer | Controller | ACTIVE: the Controller's own rules apply with no pod exemption; a seat can become STAGGERED or DOWNED, and DEAD, while `IN_POD`, from the running statuses. A STAGGERED, DOWNED or DEAD seat still cannot *press* the console (`BUSY`), but it can already be in the pod | WM-9, PCI states table, ASK-PC-1 | AC-WM-93b, AC-WM-21 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| Carried module | Controller / Boarding | travels unchanged | IA-40 | AC-WM-116 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |

**Consequences, stated (*(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*).** (1) **DEAD in the pod** is the Controller's DEAD and resolves as every other death: "restart from the last autosave" (SL-7, WM-32); the world block rolls back with the autosave, so the machine, `pod_at` and the fuel the hop spent come back together, and this system adds no special case. (2) **STAGGERED or DOWNED in the pod:** the leg and the settle are unchanged and total (BM5 is geometry-only, WM-15); the avatar is transferred to the arrival cell in the Controller's state, the DOWNED-to-DEAD timer keeps counting through the leg, and a DOWNED seat cannot hop home (`BUSY`, E-15). The player's recourse is the Controller's solo "give up and reload" action (ruling D7), not a Web Map & Transit rule. (3) **Fairness:** the pod adds no hazard of its own, so a death or fall in the pod needs a status the player carried aboard; the leg plus settle (945 ticks at the defaults) is longer than `W_MIN_WARNING_S` x `TICK_RATE` = 600 ticks (D.13), so the warning for a running status is the owner's own cue (Suit's and the Controller's), which plays during the leg like anywhere else. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R6)* At the defaults the leg itself is not lethal to a seat boarding at a full reserve: it costs 17.4 % of the reserve and FAILED needs at least 250 runs (Suit AC-SE-94 owns the table by boarding reserve; CRITICAL inside a nominal leg needs a boarding reserve at or below 107,400 mGU at `d` 300). (4) The autosave soft gate treats `IN_POD` as a soft state already (E-31); a seat in the pod at CRITICAL vitals is also a soft-gate input under the Save/Load package (ruling X7), which is Save/Load's text, not restated here.

**WM-18 — The exterior leg and the residual lamp (truth reads).** *Rule.* During the leg the view is an **exterior chase**: the pod sprite with its running light, a camera that follows the pod pod-local at constant velocity (no cinematic cuts within the leg; PIPE C10), the avatar not drawn, and the `pod_cabin` interior never rendered. The target wreck resolves out of the dark as unlit silhouette art with **one warm point**: the residual lamp, drawn at the target's pose plus `visible_unknown.exterior_anchor_px`, Tungsten, steady, **iff `read_view.node(fx_res).state in {OK, DEGRADED}` and `read_view.node(battery).stock > 0`** (registry: `residual_lamp_predicate`) for that derelict's residual lamp node and residual battery (A: node `lamp_a_life`, emitter `fx_a_res`, battery `b_a1`; B: node `lamp_b_breach`, emitter `fx_b_res`, battery `b_b1`; REG `visible_unknown`; `fx_res` in the predicate denotes that lamp node), read from the Graph's persisted state (a truth read, never from a Web Map & Transit copy; `read_view` works on a dormant section because dormant state is persisted and hashed unchanged, LS-17). Exactly one such point per derelict, zero per HOME; it is never modulated by Attention. It is **presentation-only unlit art**: no `Light2D`, no emitter record, no stimulus (cross-body light is off at MVP, EM-30; the running light is cosmetic and is not an eligible emitter, ASK-A1). A lamp found dark on return is the honest reading (DA:452, REG `visible_unknown`). The lamp's clock is *loaded* time, so berth time and visit time both burn it (D.10). The Perception Filter may not fabricate a second point or erase this one (DA:1188). `unknown_seen` and `SEEN` are pure functions of the leg and of the Graph's persisted stock (WM-14), never of camera framing. *(orchestrator ruling OR-4, ruled, checkpoint 2026-10-26: the chain predicate amends R6. In default MVP play it equals `stock > 0`; a salvaged or destroyed lamp with a charged battery shows no point, so no phantom light outlives the lamp's removal.)* On a BACK leg the departure derelict is drawn astern by the same predicate (unlit silhouette plus its residual-lamp point iff the chain predicate holds), receding (CF-AD-3). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Exterior distance cull.** The wreck silhouette, the lamp point and the departure hull are drawn only within `EXTERIOR_CULL_RADIUS_PX` [PLACEHOLDER] 192 base px of the pod (the narrowest ladder field's half-diagonal is 184), at every zoom step. Zoom stays free (the user decision): a wider field shows more void and the same two lit things, never the whole leg and the target at once, so the wreck still resolves out of the dark (Smallness). The cull is presentation only (ASK-PIPE, OQ-WM-47) and is a derived input of D.6; a zoom lock is not used because it would contradict the zoom in/out decision. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **The cull is specified per pixel, with a fade band, so nothing pops.** It is a mask on the exterior layer (the wreck silhouette, the residual-lamp point with its halo, and the departure hull), evaluated per pixel on the distance `r` from the pod's centre in base px, not per object: a pixel is fully drawn for `r <= EXTERIOR_CULL_RADIUS_PX - EXTERIOR_CULL_FADE_PX`, fully culled for `r >= EXTERIOR_CULL_RADIUS_PX`, and in between it is drawn at one of four **ordered-dither coverage steps** (a 4x4 Bayer mask at 25, 50, 75 and 100 %, so the pixel grid and the palette are kept; there is no alpha blend and no scale change). The lamp's colour never changes across steps (Tungsten stays Tungsten); only the number of its pixels does. At the default speed of 1 px per tick the 24 px band is crossed in 24 ticks, one step per 6 ticks, so at the widest zoom (4K at 1x, where the field is far larger than the circle) the lamp or the hull enters the picture over about 0.4 s as a thickening of dither, never as a one-frame appearance mid-screen. The mask is presentation only: the lamp's predicate (WM-18, `residual_lamp_predicate`), `unknown_seen` and `SEEN` never read it, and the Perception Filter may not wrap or move it (VA-R7). AC-WM-137 captures the widest zoom. *Cites.* DA-27, DA:445-452, DA:1188; REG `visible_unknown`; EM-30, EM:660; ART 2.5; PIPE C10. *Pillar.* P1, P4. *Decided.* R5, R6; OR-4.

**WM-19 — Interior content of a non-avatar body is drawn only when the body is docked or welded to the avatar's body.** *Rule (proposed — the Pipeline has not accepted it on disk, ASK-PIPE draw rule OPEN, OQ-WM-47; with the WM-6 departure re-pose it is defence in depth rather than the only guard) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.* The rule governs **interior scene content only**: the `PROJECTOR` draws the interior content of a non-avatar body only if that body is `docked` to the avatar's body or welded to it. **Exempt (checkpoint 2026-10-26, C7):** the unlit exterior silhouette and the residual-lamp point of the leg target (WM-18), which are drawn during LEG while the target is docked to nothing. Independently, the content lint on `world_slot` spacing keeps the initial poses of all bodies apart. Both are needed: the lint covers the `init_world` poses, the WM-6 departure re-pose keeps every dormant body at its slot-relative pose, and the draw rule is the defence in depth for a leg target and for any pose a content pass gets wrong; without them a live body within the field would draw as a ghost interior in the void (a P1 lie). *Cites.* BDY-27, BDY-33; PIPE C2. *Pillar.* P1. *Decided.* systems-designer SD-7; recorded as a presentation rule, no simulation change.

#### C. Fuel, the berth and topology changes

**WM-20 — Fuel is the Graph's: Web Map & Transit spends, never stores.** *Rule.* A hop and a berth *spend* fuel through M16 `withdraw_store(node_id, amount) -> granted` (Class B, clamps to stock, so `apply` is total). **Who pays:** a hop pays `HOP_FUEL_ML` [PLACEHOLDER] on departure from HOME only (the round trip is prepaid; the return hop costs `RETURN_HOP_FUEL_ML` [PLACEHOLDER], a knob kept at 0), and `berth` pays its own constant `BERTH_FUEL_ML` [PLACEHOLDER]. **Where from:** the FUEL_TANK node(s) in sections whose `kind` is HOME, found by kind through the Graph's pure `section(section_id) -> {kind, dormant}` read (ASK-G1, accepted), **never by the string `ft_main`** (lint); if there is more than one payer tank, withdrawal runs in ascending node id until the cost is met (the Graph's own convention, LSG:111); a DERELICT-kind tank (for example B's `ft_b` after a weld) is never drawn on, so it is not an MVP refuel source *(OR-6, refining R10)*. **Validate:** the pool is the sum of the payer tanks' persisted stock read from `read_view.node(tank).stock`; `pod_hop` and `berth` are rejected `FUEL_LOW` when `pool - cost < HOP_FUEL_FLOOR_ML` [PLACEHOLDER] (accepted when equal). **Apply:** `granted := withdraw_store(...)`; a `granted` below the cost cannot occur on a validated command in the same tick (one command per apply, no interleaving) and, if it ever did, is logged `transit_fuel_short` and the hop **still proceeds** (never strand). The floor must not stay a hidden threshold (P1 design test: a hidden stat versus a visibly leaking pipe -> the pipe); the first draft's refusal-only witness was a P1 risk (stage 12). Its witnesses, in order: (1) **physical, uncorruptible world marks of the refusal thresholds on the home FUEL_TANK** *(ASK-G3 accepted by the Graph as static prop art carrying no state, LSG L280; the art itself is ASK-AD-9, OPEN with the art-director)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: two painted reserve marks on the tank body at the **refusal thresholds**, not at the floor: the **pod mark** at `HOP_FUEL_FLOOR_ML` [PLACEHOLDER] + `HOP_FUEL_ML` [PLACEHOLDER] = 1,900 mL = 9.5 % of the tank's 20,000 mL height ("below this line the pod will not leave") and the **berth mark** at `HOP_FUEL_FLOOR_ML` [PLACEHOLDER] + `BERTH_FUEL_ML` [PLACEHOLDER] = 2,300 mL = 11.5 % ("below this line the home will not come alongside") (LSG:111); the floor itself (1,500 mL, 7.5 %) is the reserve the refusal protects and is **not painted**, because a line at the floor would let a tank visibly above it still refuse the hop, the hidden `FUEL_LOW` this witness exists to prevent; drawn as static consequence-channel art (a fact painted on the tank, not a gauge reading), so a player can read that a reserve exists and where a press will be refused before pressing the console; (2) the console's `FUEL_LOW` refusal, truth-only (ASK-F1); (3) the pool itself, witnessed only by the Graph's sight-glass (a corruptible module-body reading) and consequence channel (drip, `PUDDLE` descriptor id 25, SCRAM). **The marks make the refusal thresholds world facts; the level against them is still the Graph's.** The marks do not make the current fuel level truthful: a low-sanity player's sight-glass can still disagree with the tank, so the refusal stays the final truthful arbiter. The marks join the truth-only list (ASK-F1, ASK-AD-8): never wrapped, moved, erased, relocated or animated. Lint `FLOOR_MARK_MATCHES_KNOB` (Config/Data, AC-WM-119): each mark's authored height fraction equals (`HOP_FUEL_FLOOR_ML` [PLACEHOLDER] + the verb's cost) over the tank capacity to within one art pixel (pod mark: `HOP_FUEL_ML` [PLACEHOLDER]; berth mark: `BERTH_FUEL_ML` [PLACEHOLDER]), so a retuned floor or cost cannot leave a stale painted line (a lie by commission). The marks are for the single payer tank at MVP; with more than one payer tank (Vertical Slice) they are re-ruled for the pooled floor (a welded B's tank is not a payer under OR-6, so the single-tank marks hold in S3 and S4; the OQ-WM-23 lean would reopen them). **Degraded behaviour if ASK-G3 or ASK-AD-9 is refused:** the floor is witnessed by the `FUEL_LOW` refusal alone (a refusal-only witness, a P1 risk the designer accepts at MVP), and the playtest tripwire AC-WM-81h records any tester who reports `FUEL_LOW` as arbitrary or hidden; the recorded response is to bring the marks back, never to move or hide the thresholds. There is **no Web Map & Transit fuel store, meter, gauge, ETA or spill**. The amount's unit is millilitres, matching the FUEL_TANK field `stock_ml` (LSG:111); the Graph states M16's `amount` unit (mL for a FUEL_TANK) explicitly (ASK-G2, accepted). **There is no MVP refuel source** (FUEL_PORT waits for Vertical Slice A), so fuel is a **finite campaign clock shared with the reactor**: every hop and berth shortens the campaign, and the constants are tuned at the kill/continue playtest so that a handful of sorties stay a *timer* and not the managed loop the Graph forbids for MVP (the Graph's `FUEL_UML_PER_W` Tuning Knobs row, LSG:958, not a registry entry). **Campaign-scale intent** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: fuel is a campaign-scale timer. The tension of a sortie comes from time away and the reactor's burn, not from the hop toll; the target and its lint are in D.4 and AC-WM-37a. **ASK-G2 (ACCEPTED, LS-18 M16 row and the Graph's Web Map row):** this system is a declared M16 caller, the unit of `amount` is mL for a FUEL_TANK, and the amount leaves the world into the section's `withdrawn_*` counter so the conservation sum closes (`sum fuel + sum fuel_puddle + burned + sum withdrawn` is constant); a hop is neither a refuel nor a burn and this system keeps no counter of its own. *Cites.* LSG M16 (LSG:195), LSG:111, :957-958, :1025, :1152; LS-17. *Pillar.* P5 (never strands, still debits), P1 (witnessed by the pipe, not a stat). *Decided.* R10, R11; OR-6. *Constants.* `HOP_FUEL_ML` [PLACEHOLDER] 400, `BERTH_FUEL_ML` [PLACEHOLDER] 800, `HOP_FUEL_FLOOR_ML` [PLACEHOLDER] 1500, all [PLACEHOLDER], registered by this GDD; derivations in Formulas. The reserve marks' heights are derived from the floor and the verb costs, never authored separately (lint `FLOOR_MARK_MATCHES_KNOB`).

**WM-21 — `berth{derelict_id}`: the home-ship comes alongside (docking for the weld).** *Rule.* `berth` is a PLAYER command at the home pod console while `pod_at` is HOME and the machine is `IDLE`. It is the answer to the second meaning of "docking" (Overview, "Index wording, reconciled"): it establishes `docked(home, B, frame_shift_px)` so that Assembly may offer the weld (MSA-12: "Web Map & Transit establishes `docked` (provisional contract); Body tests it; Assembly gates the *offer*"). A PLAYER-origin weld is therefore **offered only once `docked(a, b, frame_shift_px)` holds**, i.e. once this system has established `world_b - world_a == frame_shift_px` (BDY-27; repeated for Body and for Assembly's T1). **Fiction and exterior: the home-ship moves**; the dead hulk does not (a derelict has no drive; P1). Validate (in order, after the shared reasons of WM-9): the target's `status` is BOARDED (`NOT_BOARDED`), its kind is DERELICT, it carries a socket that mates a FREE home socket by Layout's pure `can_attach` preview (`NO_MATE_SOCKET`, `SOCKET_NOT_FREE`), no other derelict is berthed (`ALREADY_BERTHED`), and `pool - BERTH_FUEL_ML >= HOP_FUEL_FLOOR_ML` [PLACEHOLDER] (WM-20). Apply, in kernel order: Graph M16 withdraw; **clear B's dormancy and re-sync its subjects** (`berth` loads B; LS-17: a dormant section cannot be welded, `SECTION_NOT_LOADED`, so loading is a precondition of the weld) (ASK-G1, ASK-I1, accepted; the clear is gated by the WM-24 rules); Body BM7 poses (home to the origin, B to its start pose derived from the path) and BM6 `set_body_velocity(home, v_1)` (ASK-B1, accepted); the record (`transit_state := BERTHING`, `berth_target_id`, `berth_epoch += 1`, `berth_phase := 1`, `t_phase := T`); and **no** `push_stimulus` call (the berth's one LOUD push is made by Interaction, the channel owner, from the `berth` declaration, IA-33, IA-40; WM-26) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling G1)*. The berth path is **authored per derelict as a piecewise-constant-velocity path** of at most three phases with an exact-sum lint (the start offset is *derived* from the path so exactness is structural), each speed at most `VEL_MAX_PX_PER_TICK` [PLACEHOLDER], the last phase a slow contact crawl so the `BERTHED` contact beat is soft, and a start far enough outside the visible field that no pose is seen to jump (values and lints in D.7). Phase changes are SYSTEM `berth_step{berth_epoch, phase}` commands submitted by `transit.step` at `T >= t_phase + n_i - 1` and applied at T+1 (BM6 is Class A; pose-carrying, WM-13); `t_phase` advances by the nominal `n_i` and the pose set is the nominal trajectory at the apply tick *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. The last step sets both velocities to 0, sets the home pose so that `world(B) - world(home) == frame_shift_px`, sets `transit_state := IDLE` (the machine is `IDLE`, the derelict is *berthed*), and notifies `BERTHED`. `docked` is quoted verbatim from the registry: `docked(a, b, frame_shift_px) = (vel(a) == vel(b)) and (world(b) - world(a) == frame_shift_px); delta_abs = frame_shift_px - (world(b) - world(a))`; at the last step both velocities are 0 and `delta_abs = (0, 0)`. `frame_shift_px` for the authored pair is **(696, 384)** (line_px 720 minus 24, start-axis difference 384); the (576, 96) in the registry's `weld_offset` and `body_transfer` examples is Layout's illustrative fixture and is **never** the authored pair. **The pod does not move with the home body during a berth**: its section is dormant so its parked world pose is never read (WM-6), `pod_at` is a record id and not a pose, the docked-podcraft prop is presentation drawn from `pod_at` (WM-33), and the next `pod_hop` apply re-poses the pod (WM-10); the pod is not "left behind in space". The player is *not* in the pod during a berth: they stand inside the home hull (the camera is avatar-local on the home body), and the interior never shows the ship's own motion, so there is no progress element and no picture; the witnesses are a sustained drive-hum and hull-strain bed across the whole of BERTHING (its audible length is the closing's length), the `BERTHED` contact cue with its Settle, and the weld offer appearing (Visual/Audio §2 and §8, Game Feel). The drive burn the player hears is the drive burn reported to Attention as one LOUD push at the apply, pushed by Interaction (WM-26) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling G1)*. *Cites.* MSA-12 (MSA:206), MSA-25; BDY-26, BDY-27, REG `docking_invariant`; LSG LS-17; REG `mvp_template_roster` sockets. *Pillar.* P1 (the weld starts with a physical meeting, not a menu), P5 (growth costs fuel). *Decided.* R9. *Why this over the alternatives.* A derelict pre-berthed from minute 0 would make the closing a lie about the cost and the time of coming alongside (the hop to B is separately shown by its exterior leg); hiding `delta_abs` behind a camera cut shows a jump, which BDY-27 permits only where this system cannot guarantee `docked`.

**WM-22 — Hold rule while berthed, and `unberth` (ruled, checkpoint 2026-10-26).** *Rule.* Between the last `berth_step` and the weld, this system issues **no BM6 or BM7 on either body**, in particular while an ASSEMBLY weld channel is open (MSA-12 consequence 3, mitigation (i): "do not break `docked` for a pair while an ASSEMBLY weld channel is open on it", readable from `channel_view`; MSA T1). At most one derelict is berthed (one home socket, MSA-25 sockets are terminal; a build assertion). `pod_hop` while berthed is `TARGET_BERTHED` (to that derelict) or `BERTH_PENDING` (to any other), and `pod_hop`, `berth` and `unberth` while an ASSEMBLY channel is open on the home body are `BUSY`. A hop pressed while a weld *offer* exists but no channel is open is allowed; the offer simply vanishes. When Assembly welds B, the reconcile (WM-23) sets B `ABSORBED`, clears the berth fields, and the dock room stays the home `airlock`. **`unberth` *(ruled, checkpoint 2026-10-26, OQ-WM-2; R9 does not list it)*.** Without it a berthed-but-never-welded derelict (the player lacks the welder, or the socket pair fails) grounds the pod permanently, because every hop is `BERTH_PENDING`: a soft lock that is a *rule*, not a consequence the player earned. `unberth{}` is a PLAYER command at the home pod console, valid iff a derelict is berthed (the machine `IDLE`), no ASSEMBLY channel is open, and the home socket is FREE (not WELDED); it plays the berth path in reverse (the same exact-sum lint, no extra authored data), costs `UNBERTH_FUEL_ML` [PLACEHOLDER] 0 with **no `FUEL_LOW` path and no floor check** (never strand), returns B to dormancy at the end and the machine to `IDLE`, leaves B's `status` BOARDED (never regresses) and emits `UNBERTHED` unconditionally. Its reason set is `BUSY`, `IN_TRANSIT`, `OUT_OF_RANGE`, `NOT_LIT`, `NOT_DOCKED_HERE`, `NOT_BERTHED`, `SOCKET_NOT_FREE`. Exploit check: a berth then unberth cycle costs `BERTH_FUEL_ML` [PLACEHOLDER] each time and gains no resource (repositioning is not a resource), so cycling is self-limiting. *Cites.* MSA-12, MSA-25, MSA T1 (MSA:1049). *Pillar.* P1, P5. *Decided.* R9 (hold); OR-5 (`unberth`, ruled checkpoint 2026-10-26).

**WM-23 — Topology reconcile: weld, cut, orphan.** *Rule.* Assembly never calls this system (MSA-17, MSA-24), so a weld or a cut is detected by a **read**: each `transit.step` run compares, per record, Layout's current body of `section_id` with the stored `body_id` (the cheap early-out is Assembly's published `last_shape_change_T()`; -> ADR candidate). A difference submits SYSTEM `web_reconcile{record_ids}` (idempotent), which applies: **weld** gives `status := ABSORBED`, clears the berth fields (`berth_target_id := NONE`) and the record's `body_id` follows the surviving body id of the weld (MSA-14; Layout `body_of(section_id)` after LM2); **cut** leaves the detached piece as a dormant **orphan** body: M19 sets it dormant (ASK-G1, accepted; gated by the WM-24 rules), `DETACH_DRIFT_PX_PER_TICK` is 0 (MVP: the cut-off piece does not drift, REG `DETACH_DRIFT_PX_PER_TICK`), nothing is discarded (`M4`, `LM4` and `E2` are not called in MVP) and the record's `status := ORPHAN` *(ruled, checkpoint 2026-10-26; extends R19's four statuses so a cut piece is named and kept out of the destinations)*. The orphan counts toward `MAX_BODIES`, is never discarded and never re-loaded by Web Map & Transit, and is never a `pod_hop` target (`TARGET_ABSORBED`). **Stranded-crew recovery is out of MVP scope**: the solo seat is on the keep side by construction (MSA-18: the cut is offered only from the component that keeps its body), so MSA E-22 and ASK T2 are answered "Vertical Slice A / B" (no recovery path at MVP, so `CUT_STRANDS` applies at VS-B per AC-MSA-124; records AC-MSA-124 (i) none at MVP, (ii) never a destination); MSA E-17 is answered by the WM-22 hold (ASK T1). A welded former-DERELICT section keeps its section `kind` (set by M1, never changed), so whether compression and the lamp clock continue on a welded B is the Graph's and Assembly's call (OQ-WM-5). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **HOME follows the piece that contains the airlock/dock room.** The HOME record's `body_id` is the body of the HOME section (`home_ship`, which holds the `airlock` dock room where `pod_at` HOME is parked); a record is made `ORPHAN` only if its section is not in that body. A cut that would leave the airlock room, or every payer FUEL_TANK, on a detached dormant piece would leave `pod_at` HOME pointing at a dormant piece, or the pool at 0 with no hop possible: an unruled soft lock. Modular Ship Assembly must forbid such cuts (`CUT_ORPHANS_DOCK`, `CUT_ORPHANS_PAYER_TANKS`; ask ASK-MSA-1, OQ-WM-44, cross-GDD note). The defensive handling here is total and named: the HOME-kind and avatar-present guards of WM-24 refuse to set the HOME section or an avatar-hosting section dormant, `web_reconcile` logs `WEB_RECONCILE_GUARD` and applies the rest (a SYSTEM command may not reject), and the broken state is a defect, not a reachable game state (E-75, E-76; AC-WM-133, AC-WM-134). *Cites.* MSA-17, MSA-18, MSA-24 (MSA:266), MSA E-17, E-22; BDY-4; REG `DETACH_DRIFT_PX_PER_TICK`. *Pillar.* P5 (loss is not total: a cut piece persists, nothing is discarded). *Decided.* R24; OR-5.

#### D. The loaded set, depth, knowledge and persistence

**WM-24 — The loaded set and dormancy policy.** *Rule.* The loaded set is the HOME body (with any welded pieces) plus **at most one unwelded derelict**; the pod's section is always dormant. A departed derelict goes dormant **in the departure apply** (WM-10); the arrived derelict goes live **in `pod_settle`** (WM-14, OR-1); `berth` loads B (WM-21); a reconcile may set an orphan dormant (WM-23). **Nothing is ever built, discarded or re-instantiated on a hop**: every section is built once at `init_world`, and a hop only flips dormancy, unregisters or re-syncs Interaction subjects, and (in the `PROJECTOR`) frees or rebuilds scene content. This is a CONFLICT-clarification against Layout's offer that Web Map & Transit uses `build_section` (LM1) "for the pod hop's atomic data change" (LY:444): it is not used on a hop (CF-LY). The reachable sets are exactly the five of DA-32 (confirmed here, answering OQ-DA-15's first question): **S0** {home}; **S1** {home, A}; **S2** {home, B}; **S3** {home + B welded}; **S4** {home + B, A} (the post-weld sortie to A). Set transitions: S0 to S1 (hop to A, live at `pod_settle`); S1 to S0 (hop home, A dormant at departure); S0 to S2 (hop to B, or `berth` B); S2 to S0 (B dormant on the hop home or on `unberth`); S2 to S3 (weld); S3 to S4 (hop to A); S4 to S3 (hop home); S3 to a S0-shaped set (cut: the orphan is dormant). **During a leg the loaded set is {home}** (both derelicts dormant). `BERTH_PENDING` and the star topology (WM-4) make every set outside S0 to S4 unreachable. DA-32 budgets, quoted: subjects `S_int` 70 / 102 / 113 / 113 / 145 as INFO upper bounds (D.9) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* against `INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET` [PLACEHOLDER] 120 (an Interaction lint value, not a registry entry; it applies to registered neighbourhoods, each at or under 120, so S4 is no longer an overrun, DA-47, D37, E-44, OQ-WM-27) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; world tiles 182 / 300 / 331 / 331 / 449 (453 with the pod body), against `TILES_WORLD_MVP` 600 (LY D.13; a Layout Tuning Knob, not a registry entry) with a WARN at 540. Dormancy-at-once is necessary but **not sufficient** for S4's subject count (all four S4 members are legitimately live, so this system has no lever on it); the arithmetic is in D.9. **ASK-G1 is ACCEPTED** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the Graph publishes M19 `set_section_dormant(section_id, dormant)` (Class A, LS-17, LS-18; pure check `check_set_section_dormant`) and the pure read `section(section_id) -> {kind, dormant}` (LS-19). M19's reasons are `SECTION_UNKNOWN`, `NO_CHANGE` (already in that state) and `SEAM_ACTIVE` (an ACTIVE seam joins it to a loaded section); the earlier proposed names `SECTION_HOME`, `SECTION_JOINED` and `SECTION_TOMBSTONED` are withdrawn. In M19 `NO_CHANGE` and `SEAM_ACTIVE` are **validate rejections**, and a rejected Class A call inside a no-rollback apply is a hazard (KRN CR-9), so every dormancy flip is gated by this system in the owning command's `validate`, in this fixed order: (1) **skip, never call, when the section's `dormant` already equals the target** (the plan drops the flip; it is neither an error nor a `NO_CHANGE` rejection, and a re-run of a restored apply stays idempotent); (2) the **HOME-kind guard** (this system's; the Graph has no such guard): a flip that would set a `kind` HOME section dormant is a defect, reason `WEB_DORMANCY_HOME`; (3) the **avatar-present guard** (this system's; the Graph assigns it to the caller, LSG L200): a flip that would set dormant a section whose body hosts the avatar (Body `body_of_actor`) is a defect, reason `WEB_DORMANCY_AVATAR`. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **For `pod_hop` the guard evaluates the origin body's occupancy EXCLUDING the issuing actor that the same apply moves out with BM5** (the traveller): BM5 runs in the Body phase of the apply, after validate and after the Graph phase, so at validate time the traveller is still on the origin derelict by construction, and without the exclusion every return `pod_hop` would be refused `WEB_DORMANCY_AVATAR` (a soft lock: the player could never come home). The exclusion names the issuer only; any OTHER living actor still on the origin body at validate time (a second seat, Vertical Slice B) keeps the guard firing, so a hop that would freeze a body someone is standing on is refused `WEB_DORMANCY_AVATAR` and changes nothing. Every other flip (`pod_settle`, `berth`, `unberth`, `web_reconcile`) evaluates the guard against the full occupancy, with no exclusion, because none of them moves an actor with the same apply except `pod_settle`, which only CLEARS dormancy. (4) `check_set_section_dormant` for `SECTION_UNKNOWN` and `SEAM_ACTIVE`. A guard failure in a PLAYER command (`pod_hop`, `berth`, `unberth`) rejects the command with its named defect reason and changes nothing; in the total SYSTEM commands (`pod_settle`, `web_reconcile`), which may not reject (WM-15), it is logged (`WEB_DORMANCY_GUARD`) and the rest of the apply proceeds. **ASK-G1b (OPEN, new):** M19 takes an optional `as_of_tick` so that a late `pod_settle` clear is accounted by the Graph as of the nominal tick (WM-13); Suit and Sanity need the equivalent accounting for their lag-window accruals (OQ-WM-43); until the owners answer, the three named bounded accruals of AC-WM-54 (b) apply. **ASK-I1 is ACCEPTED**: `sync_section` unregisters subjects on dormancy (IA-37, IA-40). The earlier degraded behaviour (a departed derelict stays live and unobserved) is retired because the mutator exists; E-49 keeps it as a CI-detected defect state. AC-WM-45c and AC-WM-94 cover the guards *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. *Cites.* DA-32 (DA:469), REG `legal_loaded_sets`; LS-17 (LSG:174); IA-37; LY:444, LY:381; AT Moment C (AT:75). *Pillar.* P2 (a departed derelict freezes; HOME keeps running, WM-26b), P5. *Decided.* R12; OR-1 for the arrival moment.

**WM-25 — Re-entry is frozen: no fast-forward.** *Rule.* A derelict resumes from its last state. Two owners concur and are quoted here as the contract: the Graph, LS-17: "Loading it clears `dormant` and it resumes from its last state with no fast-forward"; Horror Entities, HE-21 (HOR:191): "A record whose body is unloaded is frozen: no step, no mark ageing, no disturbance, **no fast-forward** (LS-17, LSG:174) ... A record in TRANSIT whose destination body unloads waits." HE-21 also answers ASK-H1 yes: a record may enter Horror's `TRANSIT` behaviour state toward an unloaded body and waits there (Horror's `TRANSIT` is neither this system's transit machine nor the Pipeline's `TRANSIT_HOLD`). Derelict Authoring states the player-facing consequence (DA:285-291): "a derelict's authored hp values are the values the player meets on every return." This confirms OQ-DA-15's second question and the Graph's Open Question 10 ("fast-forward on re-entry vs frozen-at-last-state is Web Map's call"). Fast-forward is the Vertical Slice A lever (serialised into this system's deltas on unload, ADR-LSG-04) and is not performed or reserved here. Consequences stated for the sibling clocks: `DERELICT_FAULT_COMPRESSION` (cited by name, value 500 permille, range 250-1000; owned by the Graph) applies to endogenous damage on a *loaded* DERELICT section only, so this system's loaded-set policy sets how long compression runs per visit, and a departed derelict stops wearing at once; the residual lamp's clock counts loaded time only. *Cites.* LS-17; HE-21; DA:285-291; REG `DERELICT_FAULT_COMPRESSION` (the registry's unit text carries two stale clauses which this GDD does not quote, CONFLICT-registry-text CF-REG). *Pillar.* P5 (nothing regrows, nothing heals), P2 (a left derelict's world state is as it was; its tell is Attention's and was resolved at its due run, AT-22). *Decided.* R13. *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*

**WM-26 — Hops and Attention.** *Rule.* The hop is **always allowed** during a TELL or BREACH (it is the escape verb, AT:73); this system pushes **no stimulus** (it makes 0 `push_stimulus` calls; the berth's single declared LOUD report is Interaction's, see "The berth is not silent to the Web" below) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling G1)*; the pod's running light is cosmetic presentation, not an eligible emitter (ASK-A1, accepted: AT-22 and AT V13 hold `held_ring` 1 for a hop between two ring-1 loci and count the pod's lights only if authored as an eligible FIXTURE, which they are not). Departure does not abort a TELL (AT-22): the clock, the bed and the beats run through the hop, and `tell_epoch`, `tell_len` and `breach_due_T` are unchanged. **The departure void (Resolved 2026-10-27 — user ruling, design-review decisions, Attention OQ-AT-12; Attention AT-22 and E-23 state it):** if the locus body is not the home-ship body and every seat recorded in it at TELL entry has left it by the due run (Attention's `locus_vacated`, derivable from Body's `body_of_actor` / `actor_section` and the IN_POD state of the Controller), the due run is an ABORT: TELL to CALM, `tell_outcome` ABORTED, no STRESS on any body, no beacon, `rearm_until_T` set, release cue. This system's departure dormancy (WM-10 step 1, WM-24) never re-points the locus: Attention suppresses its dormancy fallback from the first run at which `locus_vacated` holds (AT-14, AT-22, E-27; AC-AT-46 clause 1), so a hop never moves a breach onto the home ship the player escaped to, and this system makes no Attention call for it. The fallback to the home ship's lowest `section_id` remains only for a cut, a berth release or a dormancy while a living seat is still in the body (AT E-27). A living seat that stays in the locus body keeps the breach on that body. **A home-ship locus is not voidable** (user ruling OQ-AT-12 (2): 'the lights you left on' are the price): a hop from a tell centred on the home ship does not end it, the due run breaches on the home ship, including with nobody aboard (Attention E-73). BREACH ends when the last recorded occupant leaves a locus body that is not the home body, derivable now from Body's `body_of_actor`, so no notification from this system is needed for correctness. ASK-A4 (hold the TELL-to-STRESS transfer while no seat is aboard home) was DECLINED by Attention (AT-22, E-73) and is closed here. What is frozen across a hop is the derelict's own Graph and Horror state (WM-25), not the tell, which is Attention's. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix, conformed to Attention AT-22 / E-23 / E-73)* The option "LOUD on departure" (the hop itself becomes a stimulus) is recorded as a **default-off** knob routed to Attention (`HOP_LOUD_ON_DEPARTURE` false), not designed here. **The berth is not silent to the Web (CD-GDD-ALIGN, stage 12; amended by the design review of 2026-10-27; OQ-WM-40: Resolved 2026-10-27 — user ruling D59, route 1: the `berth` verb declared LOUD with one `U_LOUD` push at the berth apply and `unberth` QUIET; the pusher is Interaction, re-review).** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling G1)* `berth` is the home-ship's own drive burning fuel (`BERTH_FUEL_ML` [PLACEHOLDER]) for `BERTH_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] to come alongside, the largest power-and-noise act in the MVP; P3 names power use and noise as what moves Attention, so a berth that cost Attention nothing would make growth a free action and weaken restraint as a strategy. **Attention declined the first route** (OQ-AT-23): a self-generated source keyed on `transit_state == BERTHING` would be a third intake path against AT-3, AT-4 and AT-25, so "this system pushes nothing and Attention models the burn" is withdrawn and ASK-A3 as worded is DECLINED. Attention's conforming routes were (1) the `berth` verb's declaration carries `noise_class` LOUD, pushed once at the berth apply, or (2) the Graph reports the drive's draw in `power_use`. **Route 1 is ruled** (Interaction IA-40: "Resolved 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions): D59, route 1"). **Who pushes (resolved by delegated ruling G1: the channel owner and the registry's declared callers win): Interaction.** As channel owner, Interaction calls Attention's intake `push_stimulus(NOISE, source_ref = the home console section, LOUD)` once for the `berth` apply (IA-33 lists the berth apply among its LOUD one-shots, with cut completion, seam-cut DONE, vent apply and throw landing; IA-40 declares `berth` LOUD and `unberth` and `pod_hop` QUIET), and the registry's `push_stimulus` lists the Controller and Interaction as the only callers, with this system a zero-call negative reference. **This system makes 0 `push_stimulus` calls, always.** The earlier text in which this system pushed once, last in the berth apply, is withdrawn: with Interaction's declaration also pushing it would double the P3 event, and if each deferred to the other it would never be pushed. There is no `BERTH_LOUD` toggle here (the class is Interaction's `noise_class` declaration; the magnitude `U_LOUD` is Attention's) and no `NO_PUSH_OUTSIDE_BERTH` exception: the lint is now `NO_PUSH_STIMULUS` (zero `push_stimulus` calls in this system's scripts). The slot of the push (the berth apply tick or the next, relative to `attention.step`) is Interaction's and Attention's to state; this system's apply order is unchanged by it. **Counts, from any caller:** exactly **1** `push_stimulus(NOISE, ..., LOUD)` per `berth` apply that reaches `Applied`, **0** for a refused `berth`, and **0** per hop, per `unberth`, per `berth_step` and at `BERTHED`, so the hop stays the silent escape verb and the berth is the one loud act; this is the cross-system Integration criterion AC-WM-26b. A BREACH or TELL on the home body during BERTHING is not deferred or suppressed by this system. Needed from neighbours (cross-GDD notes, mirrored in OQ-WM-40): Attention confirms the intake slot; Adaptive Audio revisits the berth bed level (a LOUD act heard only as a -44 dBr tier-4 bed reads wrong, OQ-WM-46). *If the user later rules the berth silent or takes route 2:* Interaction's declaration changes, this system changes nothing, and AC-WM-81g records the gap. Re-entering a derelict: its Graph and Horror state resumes frozen (WM-25); the tell is Attention's and its clock ran during the absence. At the defaults a round trip is 2 x (`HOP_LEG_TICKS` 900 + `ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS` 45) = 1890 ticks, which exceeds `TELL_MAX_TICKS` 1800, so a tell running at departure has reached its due run, and been voided (AT-22), before any return; Attention owns the tell's fate (ASK-A2 answered by AT-22; AT E-27 on re-entry) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. *Cites.* AT-6, AT-22, AT E-23, E-27, E-73, V13, Moment B and C; AT `U_LOUD` and the `push_stimulus(NOISE, source_ref, class)` / `power_use` intake. *Pillar.* P2, P3. *Decided.* R16; stage 12 (ASK-A3).

**WM-26b — Derelicts freeze; HOME keeps running (the price of leaving).** *Rule.* A departed derelict freezes (dormant, WM-24). HOME and a berthed derelict keep running, because the Graph's clocks run whether or not the player acts (the earlier rationale 'nothing live runs unobserved' stays withdrawn) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. One consequence is stated, not hidden: a leg plus settle is 945 ticks at the defaults (900 + 45), longer than the fairness window `W_MIN_WARNING_S` x `TICK_RATE` = 10 x 60 = 600 ticks (D.13), so a home fault that begins its warning as the seat departs can warn and fire entirely while the seat is in the pod. (The earlier consequence (1), 'a TELL-then-hop moves the due-run breach STRESS to the unwitnessed home ship', is DELETED: it described behaviour the user ruling OQ-AT-12 removed; a hop from a non-home locus ends the tell as ABORTED with no STRESS and a home-ship-locus tell is not escapable, WM-26, AT-22.) **Resolved 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R21 (user delegated all remaining calls; applied under G6): the companion rule is WITHDRAWN and the exposure is accepted as the price of leaving.** The companion rule had two halves. Attention's half (hold the TELL-to-STRESS transfer while the seat is in the pod) was declined by Attention (AT-22) on the user ruling that a home-ship-locus breach proceeds as ruled and is not deferrable by hopping (E-73). The Graph's half (ASK-G5: defer arming new endogenous home failures while `transit_state` is LEG or ARRIVING) never received an answer (`life-support-graph.md` has no ASK-G5 and no OQ-WM-42), so it was unanswered, not accepted; adopting it alone would need the Graph (a Foundation system) to read Web Map's `transit_state` and would still let a fault already inside its 600-tick warning fire unwitnessed, so it is not a complete guarantee. The Graph's fairness gate (LS-13, `W_MIN_WARNING_S` 10 s of two-channel physical warning before any endogenous loss) still holds in the simulation; the pod window only means the player may not be present to read it. This is consistent with D60 (statuses run in the pod) and with the time-away costs E-62 and D.4 rely on. This GDD therefore asks nothing of the Graph or Attention for it. **Guards that remain:** the boolean lint `LEG_EXCEEDS_WINDOW` (true iff `n` + `ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS` + `IN_POD_SLACK_TICKS` > 600; 900 + 45 + 30 = 975 > 600, true at every default) reports the build state, and the playtest tripwire counts any home fault that fires with the seat IN_POD (telemetry `home_fault_fired_in_pod`, an observation, not a gate); if playtests make it frequent the levers are the leg and settle knobs of the Tuning Knobs section, not a new rule. The Tell bed stays audible during LEG (dread persists; the relief arrives at the arrival cut), a rule for Adaptive Audio (OQ-WM-46). *Cites.* AT-22, AT E-23, E-27, E-73, OQ-AT-12; LS-13; LS-17; D.13. *Pillar.* P2 (the fairness guarantee lives in the sim; the price of leaving is stated), P3. *Decided.* user ruling OQ-AT-12 (void confirmed; home-locus tell not voidable) plus delegated ruling R21, 2026-10-28. *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R21)*

**WM-27 — Rings and the depth reads.** *Rule.* Each record carries `ring` (an integer at least 1; **1-based**, outer ring = 1, matching AT-6, LO-12 and `AMBIENT_RING_PM` [PLACEHOLDER] rings 1 to 3). MVP: ring 1 for HOME, A and B (the pod's section resolves through `pod_at`, rule 3). This system publishes, as SIM reads of integers: `ring_of(section_id)` (a record's section, or a section welded into the home body, resolves to the record's ring, HOME and its welded pieces to 1; the pod's section to the ring of the record at `pod_at` when parked, and mid-leg resolves to the ring of the departure record (the record the leg left, which equals Attention's `held_ring` by AT-22); Attention and Sanity use `held_ring` by their own rules), `ring(PlayerId)` (`ring_of` of the avatar's `actor_section`), `body_is_home(PlayerId)` (true iff the avatar's body is the home body, including welded sections; **false in the pod**, OQ-WM-6 for Sanity) and `locus_kind(PlayerId)` (HOME, POD or DERELICT, so Sanity need not re-derive). `depth_label` for `SaveInfo` is the string `RING <n>` for the avatar's ring at capture, with no progress semantics. An absent or sub-1 ring resolves to 1 and is logged once (AT-6 order, AT E-61); the block rejects `ring < 1` at `post_restore` (never clamped). Ring values beyond ring 1, `B_table` [PLACEHOLDER] and the `AMBIENT_RING_PM` [PLACEHOLDER] rows are Depth Rings & Scarcity's (Vertical Slice). **Sanity ring-indexing CONFLICT, status:** the Sanity draft first indexed rings from 0 (the rulings cite SN:194, :219, :341, :343); the **current** draft already reads 1-based ("three depth rings 1 to 3, ring 1 the shallowest"; `RING_EXPOSURE[1..3]` 0 / 4 / 8, `AMBIENT_FLOOR[1..3]`), so the conflict is closed in the draft *(provisional — Sanity is in design; re-grep at the confirming sweep)*. *Cites.* AT-6 (AT:178); LO-12; SN:194, :341; REG `AMBIENT_RING_PM` [PLACEHOLDER]. *Pillar.* P3 (depth sets the baseline), P5. *Decided.* R17.

**WM-28 — Seen and first-entry knowledge (world facts, not profile knowledge).** *Rule.* Per record, WORLD-scope: `status` UNSEEN to SEEN (set in `pod_arrive`, WM-14) to BOARDED (set in `pod_settle`) to ABSORBED (WM-23) or ORPHAN *(ruled, checkpoint 2026-10-26)*; `first_boarded_T`; `unknown_seen`. At MVP SEEN and BOARDED are adjacent (at most `ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] + 1 ticks apart); SEEN is kept because a Vertical Slice leg may abort. At `init_world` both derelicts are UNSEEN but **listed** in the view (the pod's nav chart carries two bearings from the first minute; the Chart Table renders UNSEEN as a bare node, OQ-WM-4). A DIAGNOSTIC event `FIRST_BOARDED{derelict_id, T}` gives the derelict-level first boarding only (the first compartment of each derelict, its dock room). Save/Load's `T_enter` (SL:825, metric M2 at SL:830) is compartment-level and is not supplied by this system (CF-SL). Compartment-level "first entry" is **not** this system's: its owner is the Vertical Slice Knowledge Codex & Logs (PROFILE scope), as Boarding & Salvage records (Boarding Cross-References, row 11, formerly BS:429); Layout OQ-12 is answered 'derelict level here, compartment level Codex (VS)'. These flags are WORLD-scope and **roll back on death with the world** (MVP ships zero PROFILE blocks; Pillar 5's "never knowledge" is the Vertical Slice Codex's PROFILE data, and the chart shows what the crew lit and never reveals, ART 7.4). The `unknown_seen` tick on the chart appears only once seen from the pod, and **`unknown_seen` is monotone** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: once set to 1 at a `pod_arrive` it is never cleared by a later dark lamp, a later visit, a restore or a weld (what the crew saw, they saw; AC-WM-123). *(Alternative considered and rejected at the checkpoint, OQ-WM-8: a fixed lead `seen_T = t0 + n - SEEN_LEAD_TICKS`. SEEN stays at `pod_arrive`; revisit only if D.6 moves the visual reveal well ahead of arrival.)* *Cites.* ART 7.4; SL:825; LY OQ-12 (LY:1483). *Pillar.* P1, P5. *Decided.* R19.

**WM-29 — Seeds and deltas: reservations only.** *Rule.* Each record reserves `seed` (u64 under S-1, stored as eight bytes or a string, never a GDScript `int`; 0 for handmade) and `template_id`. It does **not** reserve `deltas`: their shape is ADR-LSG-04's, pre-release saves are disposable (SL-14), and baking an unreviewed shape into a golden save is the recorded failure pattern. Both reservations are inert at MVP; there is no generator and no `rng_derive` stream (D.10). *Cites.* SL-14, S-1; LSG LS-17 (VS-A); KRN D.10. *Pillar.* P5. *Decided.* R20.

**WM-30 — Save and restore, per state.** *Rule.* **Persisted:** the block of WM-3, including (ruled, checkpoint 2026-10-26) the active leg's parameters `{n, vx, vy, p0x, p0y}`: a content edit between a save and a restore (a tuning pass) would otherwise change a running leg and break `p1 = p0 + v x n`; `post_restore` validates `p1 == p0 + v*n`, `n` within the leg lint and every coordinate within `WORLD_COORD_LIMIT` [PLACEHOLDER], else `WEB_RESTORE_LEG_INEXACT` (never repaired). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **`origin_id` is persisted** so that a restore in a BACK leg can rebuild the astern departure derelict and its lamp (Visual/Audio Moment 2, the exterior composition, WM-18): `post_restore` validates that, while the machine is LEG or ARRIVING, `origin_id` and `target_id` are known records that differ, that an OUT leg has `origin_id` = HOME and a derelict target, and that a BACK leg has `target_id` = HOME and a derelict origin, else `WEB_RESTORE_STATE` (never repaired); it is NONE otherwise. `leg_reversed` must be 0 or 1 and 1 only while the machine is LEG or ARRIVING, else `WEB_RESTORE_STATE`. The golden save is regenerated with the field (the block is schema 1 and pre-release, so no migration is written). **The active berth is persisted the same way** (C13): `m`, each `{n_i, vx_i, vy_i}` and the start offset `r_start` (formula `berth_path`, D.7), because the path is content and a tuning pass between a save and a restore would otherwise change a running berth and break the exact sum. `post_restore` validates `r_start - sum(v_i x n_i) == frame_shift_px` (696, 384) (sign convention of D.7: `r = world(B) - world(home)` and the home moves at `v_i`; this is the checkpoint's "sum plus start equals `frame_shift_px`" with the start offset taken as the home-relative displacement) and, per axis, `|v_i| <= VEL_MAX_PX_PER_TICK` [PLACEHOLDER], else `WEB_RESTORE_BERTH_INEXACT` (never repaired). **Not persisted:** leg progress (derived from `T - t0`), scene content, the pick, and *(Amended 2026-10-29 — sync knock-on fix)* the Save/Load trigger counters `pod_arrived_seq` and `berthed_seq` (WM-31: host-local, reset to 0 by `post_restore`, which increments nothing; Save/Load re-reads its last-seen values after a load, ADR-0006 1.12, so a restore fires no autosave). **Restore rules:** mid-LEG resumes (the pod's pose is in `body.state`; the hook's idempotent rule resubmits `pod_arrive`); mid-ARRIVING rebuilds the projector from data and completes at `arrive_T + ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER]; mid-BERTHING resumes the path at `berth_phase` (the phase is derived from `t_phase` and the phase lengths; a lost boundary command costs nothing persisted: the late apply keys everything to the nominal tick and sets the nominal trajectory (WM-13) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*); IDLE with a berthed derelict restores the hold. `post_restore` submits nothing (SL-15 item 11) and the kernel's `snap_baseline` on entry to `RUNNING` baselines `previous` *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0008 sync)*; the first `transit.step` run reconciles. **Never clamp:** any inconsistency (a LEG with the avatar not on the pod body, a record id Layout does not know, a stale epoch, a live derelict where the persisted machine allows none: `WEB_RESTORE_LOADED_SET`, E-29) is a named `WEB_RESTORE_*` reason that rejects the restore. **Autosave:** `IN_POD` is a *soft* gate state (`GATE_SOFT_STATES`, SL:532: "a mid-transit state is Web Map's to restore"); the longest leg plus settle is far below `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` 7200, so the cap trips mid-leg only if chained with another soft window (a RISKY header, still restorable). `POD_ARRIVED` is the event that sets Save/Load's event trigger (coalesced by `AUTOSAVE_MIN_GAP_TICKS`; SL-4: "every name provisional"); a world restored from an event autosave at `POD_ARRIVED` is `IDLE` at the dock. Every machine state is restorable to a docked-at-home state (the Vertical Slice re-wake reuses the world block). *Cites.* SL-4 (SL:180), SL-15 (SL:221-236), SL-18; SL GATE_SOFT_STATES; KRN `Dropped(RELOAD)`; REG `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS`, `AUTOSAVE_MIN_GAP_TICKS`. *Pillar.* P5. *Decided.* R18; OR-5 (persisted leg parameters).

**WM-31 — Events (registry: `web_events`) (names provisional until the registry merge).** *Rule.* Notifications are emitted in the P5 Notify batch (KRN phase table: P4 is Commit, P5 is Notify) after the apply that caused them. *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0004 / ADR-0006 sync)* Events reach presentation subscribers (Adaptive Audio) and are **not a sim input**: Attention and Horror Entities do not subscribe (a block or hook owner never subscribes, CR-10) and read **existing `web.view` members** (WM-36) in their hooks *(Amended 2026-10-29 — sync knock-on fix: the first sync named an unspecified "`web.state` view"; no member is added, so the exact field list of WM-36 and AC-WM-60 stands)*: **departure** is `pod_at == IN_FLIGHT` (with `transit_state` LEG or ARRIVING, and `leg_dir`, `leg_origin_id`, `leg_target_id` for the endpoints); **arrival** is `pod_at` equal to a record id with `transit_state == IDLE` (`destination_ready`); **berthing** is `transit_state == BERTHING`; **dormancy** is `nodes[].loaded`, which is `not section(section_id).dormant` from the Graph's pure read (WM-3, ASK-G1), so the Graph stays the single writer of the flag and a consumer that prefers may read the Graph's `section()` directly, the same fact. A level read cannot miss a departure, an arrival or a flip: each state holds for at least a leg (360 ticks or more, WM-34) or a berth (`BERTH_TICKS`), and the edge is the consumer's own comparison with its last-seen value, which is not state of this system. Save/Load does not subscribe either: it polls the two monotonic counters `pod_arrived_seq` and `berthed_seq` through the separate read `web.save_seq()` (ADR-0006 1.12; the counters are what ADR-0006 and ADR-0004 require of the Web Map ADR), defined below *(Amended 2026-10-29 — sync knock-on fix)*. This system owns **no sound**.

**The Save/Load counters** *(Amended 2026-10-29 — sync knock-on fix; chosen from the two options "block fields" or "non-persisted": non-persisted, because Save/Load re-reads its last-seen values after every load (ADR-0006 1.12, so no value needs to survive a restore) and because a block field would change the hash, the byte budget and the golden save for a host-local trigger that no simulation rule reads).* `pod_arrived_seq` and `berthed_seq` are `u32`, start at 0 and only ever increase by exactly 1, and are returned by **one separate pure read** `web.save_seq() -> {pod_arrived_seq, berthed_seq}` granted to Save/Load's `save_load` read facet only. Like `web.leg_timing()` (WM-36) it is **not part of `web.view`**, so the Chart Table cannot reach it and the WM-36 field list is unchanged. They are **not block fields**: not persisted, not in the `state_hash` row, not in the golden save, not counted in D.11, no dirty key, no simulation reader. They live in this system's host-local runtime object beside the DIAGNOSTIC counters (`transit_stale_cmds` and the like, WM-3). **When they advance:** `pod_arrived_seq` increments by 1 inside the `pod_settle` apply that sets `POD_ARRIVED` (not in the failed settle of the T5 reversal, which emits no `POD_ARRIVED`, but in the reversed leg's own `pod_settle`), and `berthed_seq` increments by 1 inside the last `berth_step` apply that sets `BERTHED` (never for `UNBERTHED`, `web_reconcile` or `post_restore`). Each increments once per event in the same apply, so a late apply keyed to the nominal tick (WM-13) still counts exactly one and the new value is visible to the next poll after that tick's P4 Commit. **Restore:** `post_restore` resets both to 0 and increments nothing, and Save/Load re-reads its last-seen values after the load, so a restore fires no autosave and a world restored from an event autosave is `IDLE` at the dock. Several events in one catch-up frame are not lost: the counter has advanced by that many.

| Event | Payload | Emitted by | Consumers |
|---|---|---|---|
| `POD_DEPARTED` | `tick, leg_epoch, from, to, t0, n` (`t0` and `n` serve the Audio bed only; C12) | the departure apply | Audio (presentation subscriber); Attention and Horror read `web.view` (`pod_at == IN_FLIGHT`, `transit_state`, `leg_dir`, `leg_origin_id`, `leg_target_id`, WM-36) in their hooks, not this event *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0004 sync; named in the sync knock-on fix)* |
| `POD_ARRIVED` | `tick, leg_epoch, at` | `pod_settle` | Save/Load (the SL-4 **pod-hop arrival** event trigger, coalesced by `AUTOSAVE_MIN_GAP_TICKS`; Save/Load's provisional `POD_HOP` name is this event; answers SL OQ-24) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0006 sync: Save/Load detects it by polling the monotonic counter `pod_arrived_seq` of the separate read `web.save_seq()` (WM-31), not by subscribing; the counter is non-persisted and increments in this `pod_settle` apply — sync knock-on fix)*, Audio (presentation subscriber) |
| `DERELICT_LOADED` / `DERELICT_UNLOADED` | `tick, derelict_id` | the dormancy flips (departure, `pod_settle`, `berth`, `unberth`, reconcile) | Horror (HOR:369: "load and unload events"; reads the dormancy flag as `web.view` `nodes[].loaded` (derived from the Graph's `section().dormant`, WM-36) or the Graph's `section()` directly in its hook, it does not subscribe, CR-10 *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0004 sync; named in the sync knock-on fix)*), Audio (presentation subscriber) |
| `BERTHED` (and `UNBERTHED` *(ruled, checkpoint 2026-10-26)*) | `tick, derelict_id` | the last `berth_step` / `unberth` | Audio (the berth contact cue; Body's `BODY_DOCK_CONTACT` is the separate weld event, BDY-28), Save/Load (`BERTHED` only: the SL-4 **dock** completion event trigger, coalesced by `AUTOSAVE_MIN_GAP_TICKS`; answers SL OQ-24; `UNBERTHED` is not a trigger) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0006 sync: detected by polling the monotonic counter `berthed_seq` of the separate read `web.save_seq()` (WM-31), not by subscribing; non-persisted, incremented in the last `berth_step` apply, never for `UNBERTHED` — sync knock-on fix)* |
| `FIRST_BOARDED` | `derelict_id, T` | the first `pod_settle` at a record | Save/Load (derelict-level only; `T_enter` granularity is CF-SL), DIAGNOSTIC |

*Cites.* SL-4; HOR:369; BDY `BODY_DOCK_CONTACT`. *Pillar.* P2 (the audio and attention consumers can tell). *Decided.* R18.

**WM-32 — Death and rollback.** *Rule.* Death **can** occur in the pod: the statuses of the hold/run table run there (WM-17b) and a death in the pod is the Controller's DEAD (user ruling, OQ-WM-41) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. It is handled exactly as death elsewhere, which is "restart from the last autosave" (SL-7): the world block comes back with the autosave (WORLD scope), so `pod_at`, `transit_state`, statuses and the loaded flags roll back with the world, and so does the fuel the hop spent. This system never discards a record (there is no destroy path in MVP), so the roster is never lost. The user decision stands: death is lethal everywhere it can occur (including friendly fire), and the pod is a leg's worth of transport, not a safe room; danger lives at both ends and rides in the pod with the player *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. *Cites.* SL-7 (SL:41, :197); user decision. *Pillar.* P5. *Decided.* R18.

**WM-33 — The docked-podcraft prop is honest.** *Rule.* The docked-podcraft prop in a dock room renders **iff `pod_at` equals that dock's record**. When the pod is away or in flight the dock room shows the empty berth, and the arrival cell stays one tile clear of the prop. The mismatch (a prop drawn while `pod_at` says elsewhere) is a presentation defect, not simulation state. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* The dock's DOCK_STATUS glow follows the same condition as simulation state, not presentation: it is switched ON iff `pod_at` equals that dock's record, by the E10 calls of WM-8b, so the prop and the glow cannot disagree. *Cites.* DA-6, DA-38, kit gap K-3. *Pillar.* P1 (the pod is where it is). *Decided.* R22.

**WM-34 — Pacing.** *Rule.* The leg is **not skippable** at MVP. `HOP_LEG_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] 900 (15 s at 60 ticks per second; range 360-2190; `HOP_LEG_TICKS_MAX` 2190 (36.5 s) is a **pacing ceiling** (beyond it a leg invites skip pressure and chains soft autosave windows) that numerically equals 2400 - 180 - `IN_POD_SLACK_TICKS` 30, the registry's sum-lint ceiling of WM-17, which now bounds one leg's reserve cost at 400 runs x 300 mGU = 120,000 mGU, 44.4 % of the reserve (Suit D.1, delegated ruling R6); the former breath-fallback derivation is no longer its justification) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R6)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* is the pacing knob and is revisited at the kill/continue playtest (CON:483-485); the typical session meets about a handful of hops, so the exterior time must not dominate a 30-minute solo session. Reduced motion stops filament drift only (the pod's motion is information and keeps constant velocity); no flashing element appears anywhere in a leg (ART 7.3 HR-8). *Cites.* ART 2.5, ART 7.3; CON:483-485. *Pillar.* P1. *Decided.* R23.

**WM-35 — Truth channels and what this system never does.** *Rule.* The world block, `web.view`, `pod_at`, the leg exterior, `visible_unknown` and the residual-lamp point are **never wrapped by the Perception Filter or by Sanity**; no hallucination class may fabricate, erase or move them (DA:1188; ART 7.4). The pod console's refusal reasons are truth-only (ASK-F1). This system has no combat, no pod weapon, no pod steering, no derelict-to-derelict edge, no win or centre flag, no upgrade or progression track for the pod, no meter, gauge, ETA, spinner, percentage or progress ring for the hop, the berth or the map (ART 7.3, ART 7.4, P3), no authored story text, no stimulus (0 `push_stimulus` calls; the berth's one declared LOUD report is Interaction's, WM-26) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling G1)*, no random event and no loot counting. *Cites.* Player Fantasy guard rails 1 to 8. *Pillar.* P1, P4. *Decided.* Guard rail 4.

**WM-36 — `web.view`, the read view (provisional name).** *Rule.* One pure, side-effect-free read view with **true values only**; the Chart Table (designed, `chart-table-web-map-and-menus.md`) displays it and writes nothing.

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `nodes[]` | per record: `derelict_id`, `template_id`, `kind`, `ring`, `world_slot`, `status`, `unknown_seen`, `loaded` (derived from the Graph's pure `section(section_id) -> {kind, dormant}` read, ASK-G1 accepted), `berthed` (derived) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `pod_at` | a record id, or IN_FLIGHT |
| `transit_state` | IDLE, LEG, ARRIVING, BERTHING |
