| `destination_ready` | `transit_state == IDLE`, true from the `pod_settle` apply tick (WM-14) |
| `leg_target_id`, `leg_origin_id`, `leg_dir` | the persisted `target_id`, `origin_id` and `leg_dir` (OUT, BACK) while `transit_state` is LEG or ARRIVING, else NONE: the in-flight leg's two endpoint identities only, never a position, time or progress datum (ASK-CT-3, Chart Table C12). On an OUT leg the route is `leg_origin_id` (HOME) to `leg_target_id`; on a BACK leg it is `leg_origin_id` (the departure derelict) to HOME *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: `leg_origin_id` added; it is persisted since the re-review, so a restore in a BACK leg can rebuild the astern hull)* |

There is **no fuel field** (fuel is the Graph's witness), no Attention field, no sanity field and **no progress datum of any kind** (no elapsed ticks, no remaining ticks, no `t0`, no `n`; `leg_target_id`, `leg_origin_id` and `leg_dir` are endpoint identity, not progress): guard rail 1 is therefore a structural guarantee of this read surface, not a promise (checkpoint 2026-10-26, C12). A test double reading `web.view` must find none of them. **The Audio-only read.** The Adaptive Audio bed (designed, AA-33) needs the leg's timing to fade its bed in and out. It gets it from the `POD_DEPARTED` payload (`t0` and `n`, WM-31) and, for a mid-leg restore, from a separate read `web.leg_timing()` (name confirmed by Adaptive Audio, CX-A19; designed, `adaptive-audio-and-tells.md`) that returns `{t0, n}` only while `transit_state == LEG` and is **not** part of `web.view`; *(Amended 2026-10-29 — sync knock-on fix)* the same holds for `web.save_seq()` (Save/Load only, two event counters, WM-31), and Attention and Horror use **only the members above** for departure, arrival, berthing and dormancy (`pod_at`, `transit_state`, `leg_dir`, `leg_origin_id`, `leg_target_id`, `nodes[].loaded`; WM-31), so no member is added to this list; the Chart Table's contract names `web.view` alone, so the Chart Table can never reach a progress datum, and presenting either as an ETA or progress element is a defect. *Cites.* ART 7.4. *Pillar.* P1, P3. *Decided.* contract for the Chart Table (designed; its Web Map row reads `web.view` only). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*

**WM-37 — Determinism, integer arithmetic and engine notes.** *Rule.* All simulation rules here are integer-only plain data: no wall-clock or frame dependence (the only presentation-to-simulation path is the INPUT_SURFACE class, which nothing here needs); leg arrival is a pure function of the persisted `leg_epoch`, `t0` and the leg parameters; GDScript integer division truncates toward zero, so every division takes non-negative operands; distance lints use squared distances rather than `hypot`; `u64` seeds are bytes (S-1). H10: systems stay scene-tree free; the exterior view, the pod sprite and the filament layer are `PRESENTATION` / `PROJECTOR` concerns. **Engine reference:** `docs/engine-reference/godot/modules/` has **no file for this domain** (no world-model, transit, scripting.md or core.md); the relevant slice is `modules/rendering.md`, `breaking-changes.md` 4.6 to 4.7 and `current-best-practices.md`. Flagged items: the 4.7 packed-array element-write change (explicit dirty keys in every mutator), the 4.7 typed-return override change (any presenter or projector override class needs an explicit `return`), `[BIN]` `Parallax2D` behaviour under the integer-scale SubViewport (PIPE), and `[UNV]` float32 canvas-offset exactness (spike S2). *-> ADR candidates:* (a) pose-carrying boundary commands and the restore-slip rule (WM-13); (b) projector pre-build during the leg and the `destination_ready` latch (WM-14); (c) derived `IN_POD` (WM-16); (d) the exterior-body draw rule (WM-19); (e) the world-block record schema and the leg content file; (f) Graph M19 and Body BM7 as additive mutators. *Cites.* KRN H10, D.10, D.12; VERSION.md; breaking-changes.md. *Pillar.* P1. *Decided.* guard rail 5; brief engine feasibility.

**Question ids reserved for Open Questions.** OQ-WM-1 arrival as two commands against the wording of R12 and R26 (OR-1); OQ-WM-2 `unberth`; OQ-WM-3 the lamp predicate (OR-4); OQ-WM-4 selectable UNSEEN nodes on the chart; OQ-WM-5 compression and lamp clock on a welded former-DERELICT section; OQ-WM-6 `body_is_home` in the pod; OQ-WM-7 the OR-5 bundle (`ORPHAN`, `TARGET_ABSORBED`, persisted leg parameters); OQ-WM-8 SEEN timing (at `pod_arrive` versus a lead before it); OQ-WM-9 the leg reveal window and standoff numbers (the primary proposal's 300 px standoff and fast leg velocity put the lamp off-screen at the narrowest zoom; the supporting proposal's lamp-in-view lint caps them; Formulas decides); OQ-WM-10 pin PIPE C10's "pod-local" camera reading; OQ-WM-11 the pod-console declaration on the docked-podcraft prop (ASK-L3). **Checkpoint status (2026-10-26):** OQ-WM-1, -2, -3, -4, -7, -8, -9, -10 ruled (see the OR table and the rules above); OQ-WM-5 forwarded to the Graph and Assembly; OQ-WM-6 routed to Sanity; OQ-WM-11 routed as ASK-L3 and ASK-I1. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The design review added OQ-WM-39 to OQ-WM-47 (OQ-WM-39 and OQ-WM-41 are resolved as user rulings on 2026-10-27 and OQ-WM-40 is resolved as route 1, user ruling D59, with Interaction as the pusher; the three sit first in Open Questions) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*.



### States and Transitions

The transit machine has **four states**; `berthed` is a *condition of IDLE* (a derelict is berthed while the machine rests), not a fifth state. Every state is derivable from the persisted integers of WM-3 (no state lives only in memory). Only these causes move the machine: the PLAYER commands `pod_hop`, `berth` and `unberth` *(ruled, checkpoint 2026-10-26)*, and the SYSTEM commands `pod_arrive`, `pod_settle`, `berth_step` and `web_reconcile`. **No timer outside the block, and no write from Attention, Sanity, Horror Entities, Assembly or the Chart Table, can move it.** Within one tick the order is: P1 intake, P2 apply (commands, including the T+1 SYSTEM commands the hook submitted last tick), P3 step hooks (`body.integrate`, then `transit.step`), P4 Commit, P5 Notify batch (KRN phase table, KRN:61-67).

#### The machine

| State | Entry condition | Exit condition | Behaviour | Who may cause entry / exit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **IDLE** | `init_world`; `pod_settle` completes; the last `berth_step` / `unberth` step completes | a validated `pod_hop`, `berth` or `unberth` applies | The pod rests at `pod_at` (a dock record). The avatar is on a body (home or a derelict) and acts normally. Sub-condition **berthed**: `berth_target_id != NONE`; then every hop is `TARGET_BERTHED` or `BERTH_PENDING` (WM-22). `transit.step` only watches for topology changes (WM-23). Dormancy of the departed derelict is already applied. | PLAYER commands (entry by SYSTEM completion) |
| **LEG** | `pod_hop` applies (WM-10) | `pod_arrive` applies at `t0 + n` (WM-14) | The avatar is `IN_POD` on the pod body; the pod's pose integrates the leg velocity once per tick; both derelicts are dormant; the loaded set is {home}; the exterior chase is presented; no block field changes per tick; `transit.step` resubmits `pod_arrive` until it applies. | PLAYER `pod_hop` (entry); SYSTEM `pod_arrive` (exit) |
| **ARRIVING** | `pod_arrive` applies | `pod_settle` applies at `arrive_T + ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] | The pod is stopped at `p1`; the avatar is still in the pod; the target is still dormant; the `PROJECTOR` builds the target's scene under `TRANSIT_HOLD`; `destination_ready` is false throughout and becomes true only when `pod_settle` applies (the machine is `IDLE`). Fixed simulation length, independent of frame rate (OR-1). | SYSTEM `pod_arrive` (entry); SYSTEM `pod_settle` (exit) |
| **BERTHING** | `berth` (or `unberth`) applies | the last `berth_step` applies | The home body moves along the authored piecewise path (B is loaded, S2); the avatar stays aboard the home body and acts normally (the interior never shows the motion); `transit.step` submits `berth_step` at each phase boundary; the machine rejects `pod_hop` as `IN_TRANSIT`. | PLAYER `berth` / `unberth` (entry); SYSTEM `berth_step` (exit) |

#### Transitions

| # | From | To | Guard (checked in) | Cause | Effects (atomic, one apply) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| T1 | IDLE | LEG | `pod_hop` validate passes (WM-9) | PLAYER `pod_hop` | WM-10: fuel (HOME origin), origin dormant, BM5 / BM7 / BM6, `leg_epoch + 1`, `POD_DEPARTED` |
| T2 | LEG | LEG | `T < t0 + n - 1` | none | nothing written; progress is derived from `T - t0` |
| T3 | LEG | ARRIVING | the hook sees `T >= t0 + n - 1` and submits; applies at T+1 | SYSTEM `pod_arrive` | BM7 pod := `p1`, BM6 0, `arrive_T := t0 + n` (nominal), `status` SEEN, `unknown_seen` per WM-18 (monotone) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| T4 | ARRIVING | IDLE | the hook sees `T >= arrive_T + ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS - 1` [PLACEHOLDER] and submits; applies at T+1; arrival cell clear | SYSTEM `pod_settle` | target live, subjects synced, BM5 avatar to the `arrive_*` cell, BM7 pod parked, `pod_at := target`, BOARDED / `first_boarded_T` (the nominal settle tick) on first visit, `POD_ARRIVED`, `DERELICT_LOADED` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| T5 | ARRIVING | LEG (reversed) | the arrival-cell re-check fails (a defect: the target was frozen) | SYSTEM `pod_settle` (its recovery branch, total) | at most one reversal per hop: `leg_epoch + 1`, `leg_dir` reversed, `target_id` and `origin_id` swapped, no fuel, `ARRIVAL_RECHECK_FAILED` logged (the full apply list is WM-15) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; a second failure forces placement, `ARRIVAL_FORCED` (WM-15) |
| T6 | IDLE | BERTHING | `berth` validate passes (WM-21) | PLAYER `berth` | M16 `BERTH_FUEL_ML` [PLACEHOLDER], B live, BM7 poses, BM6 `v_1`, `berth_epoch + 1`, `berth_phase 1`, no `push_stimulus` call here (Interaction pushes the berth's one LOUD, IA-33, WM-26) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling G1)* |
| T7 | BERTHING | BERTHING | phase `i < m` and `T >= t_phase + n_i - 1` | SYSTEM `berth_step` | BM7 nominal-trajectory pose, BM6 next velocity, `berth_phase + 1`, `t_phase += n_i` (nominal) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| T8 | BERTHING | IDLE (berthed) | last phase done | SYSTEM `berth_step` | both velocities 0, `world(B) - world(home) == frame_shift_px`, `docked` holds, `BERTHED` |
| T9 | IDLE (berthed) | BERTHING (reversed) then IDLE | `unberth` validate passes *(ruled, checkpoint 2026-10-26)* | PLAYER `unberth`, then SYSTEM `berth_step` | reverse path, B dormant at the end, `berth_target_id := NONE`, `UNBERTHED` |
| T10 | any | same | a record's Layout body differs from the stored `body_id` | SYSTEM `web_reconcile` | WM-23 (record status, berth fields, orphan dormancy); **no machine change** |

**Illegal pairs** (a SYSTEM command with a stale epoch, a state mismatch or a duplicate for one epoch) are rejected at P2 with no state change and one DEBUG count (`transit_stale_cmds`); they are **never** `FAULTED` (a resubmitted command whose twin already applied is expected, and an arrival signal with no hop pending is an invalid pair by the Pipeline's own rule, PIPE:1029). A PLAYER attempt on a SYSTEM-only command is `ORIGIN_NOT_ALLOWED`.

#### A worked tick table (one hop; `HOP_LEG_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] 900 and `ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] 45, applied at tick 10000 as an illustration)

| Tick | Phase | Event | State after | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 10000 | P2 | `pod_hop` applies (WM-10) | LEG | `t0 := 10000` |
| 10000 | P3 | `body.integrate` moves the pod (integration 1); `transit.step` writes nothing | LEG | order is `body.integrate` then `transit.step` |
| 10001 to 10898 | P3 | integrations 2 to 899; zero dirty keys from this block | LEG | |
| 10899 | P3 | integration 900; `transit.step`: `T == t0 + n - 1` submits `pod_arrive{leg_epoch}` | LEG | queued |
| 10900 | P2 | `pod_arrive` applies: pod := `p1`, velocity 0 | ARRIVING | `arrive_T := 10900` |
| 10900 to 10944 | P3 | pod static; settle window; `destination_ready` false through 10944 | ARRIVING | |
| 10944 | P3 | `transit.step`: `T == arrive_T + S - 1` submits `pod_settle` | ARRIVING | `destination_ready` still false (the avatar is still on the pod body) |
| 10945 | P2 | `pod_settle` applies: target live, avatar to the dock cell, pod parked | IDLE | `destination_ready` true from this apply, before P5; total time in the pod 945 ticks = 15.75 s |
| 10945 | P5 | `POD_ARRIVED` and `DERELICT_LOADED` notified | IDLE | the Pipeline may cut to the avatar once its own projector latch also holds |

Two restore cases are covered by WM-13 and D.2; in both `arrive_T` stays the nominal 10900, the nominal settle tick is 10945 and `first_boarded_T` is the nominal 10945 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. **Case A, a snapshot after tick 10899** (or at any tick through 10943): the pending `pod_arrive` is dropped, the hook resubmits at 10900 and the arrive applies at 10901 with the absolute pose (the pod slipped `v x 1`, `EXPECTED_RESTORE_SLIP`); no `pod_settle` is pending at the snapshot, so it is submitted at 10944 exactly as in the uninterrupted run and **applies at 10945**; the avatar leaves the pod on the same tick as the uninterrupted run, so Case A produces **no accruals**, and the restored run equals the uninterrupted one at tick 10945 for the three exact blocks and at 10957 (`T* + 12`) for every other block. **Case B, a snapshot after tick 10944** (the pending `pod_settle` is dropped): the hook resubmits at 10945 and the settle **applies at 10946** (lag 1); `first_boarded_T` is still the nominal 10945; the avatar leaves the pod one tick later than in the uninterrupted run, and Case B is the only case that produces the bounded lag-1 accruals of WM-13 and AC-WM-54 (b).

#### The roster record's `status`

| Status | Entry | Exit | Meaning | Who may cause |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| UNSEEN | `init_world` (derelicts) | `pod_arrive` at this record | listed, never approached | `init_world` |
| SEEN | `pod_arrive` | `pod_settle` | the hull resolved out of the dark | SYSTEM `pod_arrive` |
| BOARDED | `pod_settle` (first time); HOME at `init_world` | a weld (`web_reconcile`) | the crew has stood aboard | SYSTEM `pod_settle`, `init_world` |
| ABSORBED | `web_reconcile` after a weld | a cut (`web_reconcile`, to ORPHAN; at most one in MVP, MSA-41) | welded into the home body; not a destination | SYSTEM `web_reconcile` |
| ORPHAN *(ruled, checkpoint 2026-10-26)* | `web_reconcile` after a cut of an ABSORBED record | none in MVP | a dormant detached piece; not a destination | SYSTEM `web_reconcile` |

Returning to a BOARDED record does not change its status. A record never returns to UNSEEN (nothing is un-seen).

#### Companion states owned elsewhere (this system causes or reads them; it does not own them)

| State | Owner | Entry / exit relative to the transit machine |
|---|---|---|
| Controller `IN_POD` | Player Controller & Input | entered on `pod_hop` `Applied`; exits when the avatar's `body_of_actor` leaves the pod body (WM-16, ASK-C1) |
| Pipeline `TRANSIT_HOLD` (layer 1 black) | Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline & Camera | entered on `pod_hop` `Applied`; cut to the pod body for the leg; black again for `ARRIVING`; ends when `destination_ready` and the projector latch both hold (WM-14). This system never calls the Pipeline (CR-10). |
| Suit `POD_RUN` (was `HOLD_ROOM`) | Suit & Equipment | while the Controller is `IN_POD` Suit issues no `draw_air` and runs the reserve, cell, thermal load and holds against the pod cabin rule (WM-17, WM-17b, ASK-S1; ASK-S2 closed) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R6)* |
| Graph section `dormant` | Life-Support Graph | set by M19 (ASK-G1, accepted) at the applies named in WM-24; this system holds no copy (OR-2) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| Attention TELL / BREACH | Attention | untouched by a hop (WM-26) |
| Assembly weld channel | Modular Ship Assembly | an open channel makes `pod_hop`, `berth` and `unberth` `BUSY` and forbids any BM6 / BM7 on the pair (WM-22) |

### Interactions with Other Systems

*Disk status at authoring (Glob of `design/gdd/`, 2026-10-26).* **Designed:** Command & Simulation Layer, Life-Support Graph, Player Controller & Input, Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline & Camera, Save/Load & Versioned Format (designed in this run), Emitter State & Light-Exposure Query, 2D Lighting & Occlusion, Interaction System, Suit & Equipment, Item & Module Database, Failure & Trail System, Ship Section & Layout Data Model, Ship Body & Interior Physics, Derelict Authoring (Handmade Kits) (designed in this run), Attention System, Modular Ship Assembly. **In design at authoring (draft on disk):** Sanity & Hallucination Rules, Horror Entities, Boarding & Salvage, Perception Filter (skeleton). **No file at authoring:** Chart Table (Web Map & Menus), Adaptive Audio & Tells, HUD & Suit Layer, In-World Instruments & Inspect View, Debug Overlay, Onboarding (Cryo Bay), Procedural Derelict Generation, Depth Rings & Scarcity, Knowledge Codex & Logs, Failure Contract & Re-wake, Co-op Session & Replication. *Disk status at the consistency pass (2026-10-27):* Sanity & Hallucination Rules, Horror Entities, Boarding & Salvage, Perception Filter (ASK-F1 adopted as PF-5 row 13), Chart Table (Web Map & Menus) (`chart-table-web-map-and-menus.md`), Adaptive Audio & Tells, HUD & Suit Layer, In-World Instruments & Inspect View and Debug Overlay are now **designed**; the Sanity, Horror and Boarding rows moved to §2. Onboarding and the Vertical Slice systems still have no file. Inline *(provisional — X undesigned / in design)* tags elsewhere in this GDD date from authoring; each is re-confirmed against the designed neighbour at the sweep. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The design review of 2026-10-27 re-confirmed them: the tags on ASK-G1, G2, G3, B1, I1, S1, C1, F1, K, A1 and LT1 are cleared (accepted or answered, Interactions section 6); the tags that remain name a still-open decision or ask. This GDD never edits another system's GDD or the index: a neighbour's text that needs a change is a **CONFLICT row** below, with the owner and the proposed fix.

#### 1. The three declared upstream dependencies (their own rows, quoted, then confirmed or corrected)

The index lists exactly three dependencies for this system (IDX:179): Command & Simulation Layer, Derelict Authoring (Handmade Kits), Save/Load & Versioned Format. Each wrote an *offer* before this GDD existed. Per the project's reflexion rule, an offer is confirmed or corrected, never copied.

| Dependency (status) | Its own row, quoted | Confirmed or corrected here |
|---|---|---|
| **Command & Simulation Layer** (designed; hard) | KRN:200, Interactions row 8: "World-model block (derelict list, seeds + deltas, loaded set) and the `pod_hop` command; Transit advances via a hook \| Atomic apply of the hop's data change \| Web Map & Transit \| Content load for a target may exceed a tick: the hop applies *data* atomically and sets Transit to `ARRIVING`; a `PROJECTOR` builds scene content and reports ready; the Transit hook completes arrival. No multi-tick transaction in the kernel" (KRN:686: Hard, "Atomic data apply, hook", "World block, `pod_hop`") | **Confirmed:** one world block, the `pod_hop` command, advancement by a hook, atomic data apply, no multi-tick transaction. **Corrected in two places (CF-KRN):** (1) the hop sets the machine to `LEG` (the exterior crossing, which the row omits), not `ARRIVING`; `ARRIVING` begins at the SYSTEM `pod_arrive`; (2) arrival is completed by the SYSTEM command `pod_settle` that the hook *submits* and the kernel applies at T+1, not "inside the hook" (a hook may not call Class A mutators), and it does not wait for the `PROJECTOR` to "report ready" because a `PROJECTOR` has no submit path (KRN conformance) and a frame-rate-dependent exit would break determinism. "Destination ready" is a pure function of the persisted machine state (`transit_state == IDLE`) in `web.view` (WM-14). |
| **Derelict Authoring (Handmade Kits)** (designed; hard) | DA:681: "reads \| a stable `template_id` roster, `entry_room_key`, the `visible_unknown` records, `mvp_world.json`, the spawn table \| Transit owns `ARRIVING`, the loaded set, the pod leg and the exterior view; it calls `init_world`, `build_section` x3, Graph M1, Emitter E1 and Interaction `sync_section`, then the PROJECTOR (provisional — Web Map & Transit undesigned) \| the reachable loaded sets of DA-32 (S4 in particular); who unloads a derelict when the pod leaves; the exterior state rule (lit iff persisted stock above 0); the pod is a Layout body". DA:1239 must-repeat: "Web Map & Transit repeats the reachable loaded sets (DA-32, including the post-weld sortie to A), that the pod is a Layout body built from the `pod` template, who unloads a derelict when the pod leaves, and the exterior-view rule for the residual light." | **Confirmed, with all four must-repeats answered:** the reachable sets S0 to S4 including the post-weld sortie to A (WM-24); the pod is a Layout body built once from the `pod` template (WM-5); **the derelict is unloaded by this system, in the departure apply, through ASK-G1** (WM-10, WM-24); the exterior rule is the chain predicate (lamp node OK or DEGRADED **and** residual battery `stock > 0`), a truth read (WM-18); this **narrows** DA:452 / DA-27's `stock > 0` by the lamp-intact term (CF-DA clause below). Also confirmed: DA-6 ("the arrival is a presentation transition owned by Web Map & Transit"): the arrival is more than presentation (it is a body transfer and a dormancy flip) and its presentation is the Pipeline's cut through black. **Corrected (CF-DA):** the exterior rule of DA-27 / DA:452 ("lit iff persisted stock above 0") is **narrowed** by the lamp-intact term: lit iff `read_view.node(fx_res).state in {OK, DEGRADED}` and `read_view.node(battery).stock > 0` (checkpoint 2026-10-26; the two coincide in default MVP play and differ only when the lamp is salvaged or destroyed with a charged battery, where the unchanged rule would draw a phantom light, a P1 lie by commission); `build_section` is called **four** times at `init_world`, not three, because `mvp_world.json` lists the `pod` template too (DA-4); Authoring's "who unloads" answer is "this system, by dormancy, never by discard" (nothing is built or discarded on a hop). Answers OQ-DA-15 in full (sets confirmed, no fast-forward), OQ-DA-29 (all four templates resolve to ring 1; this system publishes the ring, WM-27) and narrows OQ-DA-17 to the pod's `kind` / `fixtures_min` exemption (ASK-L1; the air question is closed by ASK-S1). |
| **Save/Load & Versioned Format** (designed; hard) | SL:464: "A world block (derelict list, seeds and deltas, loaded set; name provisional) is `WORLD`; unloaded derelicts persist as data (Graph `dormant` sections); a `pod_hop` arrival is an event (name provisional) \| A depth label (provisional) for `SaveInfo`; SL-15 \| Block + notification (provisional)". SL:283: "World block: derelict list, seeds and deltas, loaded set (name provisional) \| Web Map & Transit \| WORLD × SHARED \| ... seeds plus deltas keep it small". SL:532: `GATE_SOFT_STATES` "a mid-transit state is Web Map's to restore". SL-18 (SL:255): `init_world(world_seed)` is "K-2 (provisional — kernel amendment)". | **Confirmed in full:** the block is WORLD x SHARED, named `web.state` (provisional), SL-15-conformant (WM-2, WM-30); the arrival event is `POD_ARRIVED` (WM-31, the autosave event trigger); `SaveInfo.depth_label` is `RING <n>` (WM-27); `IN_POD` stays a soft gate state and every mid-transit state is restored (WM-30); this system owns `init_world` (WM-7). One refinement: the "loaded set" is **not stored** in the block (OR-2): it is derived from the Graph's `dormant` flags, which already persist. |

a derelict is dormant and never discarded, so a room is as the crew left it (WM-24, WM-28). **BS row 11** (Dependencies: "`pod_hop`, the CONSOLE subject, the arrival envelope, fuel constants, dormancy"): **confirmed.** **C-BS-9 / BS-19** (a true soft-lock: on a dark derelict dock a player with a spent cell and no glowstick gets `NOT_LIT` at the console and cannot go home; BS-19: "No sortie state exists in which the hop is unavailable for want of light alone"): **position revised by the design review (2026-10-27) and closed by a user ruling (2026-10-27, OQ-WM-39, Option B)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: this GDD's earlier position (adopt ASK-BS-I3 as ASK-I2, a `pod_hop` lit exemption) was **REFUSED by Interaction** (IA-6: 'No fourth exception for the pod console'; Interaction Open Question 31), and that refusal stands; the loose-glowstick fallback stays withdrawn as unsound (a loose stick is unlit and `pickup` is lit-only; calling the strand an 'earned consequence' contradicts BS-19 and P5's never-strand rule). **Status: the BS-19 invariant is CLOSED by construction**: the docked pod's dock-room prop carries a small self-powered status glow that counts as an ordinary lit fixture (not a fourth `act_gate` exception), so the console is always lit where it can be pressed (WM-8b, D.15, AC-WM-127a to c, AC-WM-128). The invariant is owned jointly: this system and Emitter State guarantee the glow, Derelict Authoring lints its placement in every dock room. The ruling reverses this GDD's own answer to ASK-BS-W2 (no console self-light) and accepts ASK-BS-W3 (OQ-WM-38 (a)); it needs Emitter State's EM-3/EM-11 amendment, Authoring's placement rule and lint, and the art-director's luminance separation from the visible-unknown lamp. **ASK-BS-W1** answered: `HOP_FUEL_ML` [PLACEHOLDER] 400, `HOP_FUEL_FLOOR_ML` [PLACEHOLDER] 1500, home tank `ft_main` 20,000 mL at wake (WM-20, D.4, D.5). **C-BS-12** (`T_SORTIE_SAFE_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] 28,800, 8 min, does not nest with Boarding's 5-minute sortie target or Sanity's roughly 5.3-minute stage-1 point): carried as OQ-WM-35. The wrong citations in the Boarding draft are CF-BS-1 and CF-BS-2. (Boarding designed, `boarding-and-salvage.md`; compartment-level first entry is declined by Boarding and owned by the Vertical Slice Knowledge Codex & Logs, Boarding row 11 / OQ-BS-24) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |

## Formulas

*Conventions.* Every quantity that is hashed, replicated or persisted is an **integer** (KRN determinism rule): poses and velocities are whole base pixels, durations are ticks (`TICK_RATE` 60), fuel is whole millilitres (the FUEL_TANK field `stock_ml`, LSG:111), distances in lints are compared as **squared** integers (never `hypot`), and every division takes non-negative operands (GDScript truncates toward zero). Locked names appear verbatim with their registry values; Web Map & Transit constants are `[PLACEHOLDER]` at every mention and are registered by this GDD at the merge stage from the **final** text (the canonical list, used verbatim in Cross-References and AC-WM-70: `HOP_FUEL_ML` [PLACEHOLDER], `BERTH_FUEL_ML` [PLACEHOLDER], `HOP_FUEL_FLOOR_ML` [PLACEHOLDER], `RETURN_HOP_FUEL_ML` [PLACEHOLDER], `UNBERTH_FUEL_ML` [PLACEHOLDER], `HOP_LEG_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER], `ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER], `LAMP_VIEW_MIN_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER], `LEG_STANDOFF_PX` [PLACEHOLDER], `LEG_LATERAL_PX` [PLACEHOLDER], `EXTERIOR_VISIBLE_RADIUS_PX` [PLACEHOLDER], `SLOT_PITCH_PX` [PLACEHOLDER], `WEB_COORD_BUDGET_PX` [PLACEHOLDER], `BERTH_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER], `LAMP_GLOW_RADIUS_PX` [PLACEHOLDER], `T_SORTIE_SAFE_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER], `MAX_WORLD_RECORDS` [PLACEHOLDER], `ARRIVE_MARGIN_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER], `MIN_FRAME_RATE` [PLACEHOLDER], `LEG_SPEED_SQ_MAX` [PLACEHOLDER], `BERTH_SETTLE_PX` [PLACEHOLDER], `EXTERIOR_CULL_RADIUS_PX` [PLACEHOLDER]) together with the **formulas** `pod_leg`, `leg_timeline`, `return_leg`, `hop_fuel`, `fuel_floor`, `reveal_window`, `berth_path`, `loaded_exposure`, `settle_lint`, `coordinate_budget` (the reflexion rule: register the formula, not only its constants). Numbers marked "illustrative" are lint-passing worked values for the authored content file `assets/data/world/legs.json` and `mvp_world.json`, tuned at the kill/continue playtest. The Body, Lighting and Attention placeholders this system reuses (`WORLD_COORD_LIMIT` [PLACEHOLDER] 4194304, `VEL_MAX_PX_PER_TICK` [PLACEHOLDER] 16, `BODIES_LIVE_MAX` [PLACEHOLDER] 4) belong to their owners and are cited, never restated as settled. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

### D.1 `pod_leg` (the exact constant-velocity leg)

The `pod_leg` formula is defined as:

`p1 = p0 + v × n` per axis, with the **resolved** `{p0, v, n}` persisted for the active leg (WM-3, WM-30); `target_pose = p1 + approach_offset`, `approach_offset = s_lamp − exterior_anchor_px`; lints L1 to L6 below.

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `p0` | (i32, i32) base px | `abs(c) <= WEB_COORD_BUDGET_PX` | content, `legs.json` (`pod_exit_px` of the departure dock, with the departure body at the origin after the re-origin of WM-6) | pod pose at the departure apply |
| `v` | (i32, i32) base px per tick | each axis -16..16 (`VEL_MAX_PX_PER_TICK` [PLACEHOLDER] 16); not (0, 0) | content | whole-pixel velocity (BDY-26, schema v1) |
| `n` | i32 ticks | `HOP_LEG_TICKS_MIN` 360 .. `HOP_LEG_TICKS_MAX` 2190 (= 2400 - 180 - 30); default `HOP_LEG_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] 900 | content | integrations between the departure apply and the `pod_arrive` apply |
| `p1` | (i32, i32) base px | `abs(c) <= WEB_COORD_BUDGET_PX` | **derived** | the pod's pose at arrival |
| `s_lamp` | (i32, i32) base px | along-track `LEG_STANDOFF_PX` [PLACEHOLDER] 48; lateral `LEG_LATERAL_PX` [PLACEHOLDER] 48 | content | the residual lamp's offset from the pod at the end of the leg |
| `exterior_anchor_px` | (i32, i32) base px | A (576, 84), B (948, 672) | REG `visible_unknown` | the lamp's section-local anchor |
| `approach_offset` | (i32, i32) base px | within `WEB_COORD_BUDGET_PX` [PLACEHOLDER] | **derived** | target body pose minus `p1` |

**Lints (content load, never at runtime):** **L1** per axis `|vx|, |vy| <= VEL_MAX_PX_PER_TICK` [PLACEHOLDER]; **L2** `vx² + vy² <= LEG_SPEED_SQ_MAX` [PLACEHOLDER] 14 (camera insurance only: PIPE C2(iv), PIPE:153, pins the pod-local camera, so the bound is non-binding unless that reading changes; re-derive if it does); **L3** `HOP_LEG_TICKS_MIN <= n <= HOP_LEG_TICKS_MAX` (a pacing ceiling, WM-34), and the sum lint `n + ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] + `IN_POD_SLACK_TICKS` <= 2400 (`IN_POD_SLACK_TICKS` = 2 x `SLIP_MAX_TICKS` = 30 today, derived in D.2 from the kernel's `queue_drain_ticks`, so the lint re-runs when the kernel changes, AC-WM-124; the D.2 worst case; `HOP_LEG_TICKS_MAX` is 2190 when settle is its ceiling 180; the sum lint is the registry's ceiling on one leg's reserve cost, 400 runs, WM-17, delegated ruling R6) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; **L4** every pose coordinate of the departure body, pod, target and every intermediate pose satisfies `|c| <= WEB_COORD_BUDGET_PX` [PLACEHOLDER] 131072, itself `<= WORLD_COORD_LIMIT` [PLACEHOLDER] / 32; **L5** `v != (0, 0)`; **L6** exactly one of distance, speed and `n` is derived: the file authors `v` and `n` and **derives the distance** (`p1 - p0`), so `p1 - p0 == v × n` survives any tuning pass by construction (a tuning edit changes `n` or `v` and `p1` follows; the persisted resolved values protect a running leg, WM-30); **L7** the reveal-window lint `reveal_window` (D.6); **L8** `LEG_FOUL`: the swept pod path and the target's footprint keep a clearance from every other body's footprint (the content tool evaluates footprints; this GDD asserts the rule, not the geometry) **against every legal home shape** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: S0 (home alone), S3 and S4 (home with B welded: the approach pose of the leg to A must not fall inside the welded B) and the post-cut home piece, not only S0; the lint runs once per legal shape and the leg is rejected if any fails. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*

**Output Range:** per axis `|p1 - p0| <= 16 × 2190 = 35,040` px (0.84 % of `WORLD_COORD_LIMIT` [PLACEHOLDER] 4,194,304 = 2^22) under L1 and L3 alone; under the default speed `(1, 0)` the leg is at most `1 × 2190 = 2,190` px. The 2^17 budget is 1/32 of the limit and 1/64 of 2^23, the float32 exactness bound that is `[UNV]` pending spike S2, so a failure there is cosmetic (exterior only) and never a simulation fault.

**Example:** (illustrative [PLACEHOLDER]) Derelict A: `p0 = (768, 384)`, `v = (1, 0)`, `n = 900` -> `p1 = (768 + 900, 384) = (1668, 384)`; `s_lamp = (48, 48)` (the lamp is 48 px ahead and 48 px south of the pod's final position: the pod passes 48 px north of the north hull run); `exterior_anchor_px = (576, 84)` -> `approach_offset = (48 - 576, 48 - 84) = (-528, -36)` -> `target_pose = (1668 - 528, 384 - 36) = (1140, 348)`; check `target_pose + exterior_anchor_px = (1716, 432) = p1 + s_lamp`. L2: `1 <= 14`. Speed is 1 px per tick = 60 px/s = 1.25 tiles/s, below `V_WALK` (2 px per tick): a drift ("Smallness", WM-34). Travel 900 px = 18.75 tiles. Derelict B is built the same way from its own `s_lamp` and anchor (948, 672) (numbers live in `legs.json`).

### D.2 `leg_timeline` (arrival tick, integration count and the expected slip)

The `leg_timeline` formula is defined as:

`submit_T = t0 + n - 1`; `apply_T = submit_T + a` with `a` in [1, 8] (KRN D.4: age at dispatch); `integrations = apply_T - t0`; `slip = v × (apply_T - (t0 + n))`; after a restore at snapshot tick `S >= submit_T` with the command still pending: `submit_T' = S + 1`, `apply_T' = S + 1 + a'` with `a'` in [1, 8]; settle: `submit_Ts = arrive_T + ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS - 1`, `settle_T = submit_Ts + a`; `in_pod_ticks = settle_T - t0`. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Nominal keys (restore-proof, WM-13):** `arrive_T = t0 + n`, `settle_nominal = arrive_T + ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS`, `lag = apply_T - nominal >= 0`; the persisted values use the nominal ticks. **Derived names:** `QUEUE_AGE_MAX = queue_drain_ticks` (KRN D.4; 8 today), `SLIP_MAX_TICKS = 2 x QUEUE_AGE_MAX - 1` (15), `IN_POD_SLACK_TICKS = 2 x SLIP_MAX_TICKS` (30); no bare 15 or 30 is authored in a lint. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **Repeated restores.** The slip bound `SLIP_MAX_TICKS` holds for ONE restore at a boundary. With `r` consecutive restores inside one boundary window (each drops the pending command and the hook resubmits on the first tick after `RUNNING`) the lag at that boundary is at most `(QUEUE_AGE_MAX - 1) x (r + 1) + r` ticks (7 x 2 + 1 = 15 for `r` = 1, 23 for `r` = 2); the nominal keys (WM-13) make the pose and every persisted Web Map & Transit and Body value independent of `r`, and the three named accruals grow only by `ceil(lag / 6)`, `ceil(lag / 6)` and `ceil(lag / 12)`. Lint L3b: `ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS - 1 > SLIP_MAX_TICKS` (44 > 15 at the defaults, and 23 > 15 at the range floor 24), so the settle can never be submitted before the arrive has applied and the pod cannot stall in ARRIVING (AC-WM-124).

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `t0` | i64 tick | >= 0 | the departure apply tick | persisted |
| `n` | i32 ticks | 360..2190 | D.1 | leg length |
| `a`, `a'` | i32 ticks | 1..`QUEUE_AGE_MAX` (= `queue_drain_ticks`, 8 today) | KRN D.4 (`queue_drain_ticks = ceil(QUEUE_CAPACITY / MAX_CMDS_PER_TICK)`) | dispatch age of a SYSTEM command; 1 when the queue is empty, which is the MVP norm *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `arrive_T` | i64 tick | exactly `t0 + n` | the NOMINAL arrival tick (the apply may land up to `SLIP_MAX_TICKS` later) | persisted *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] | i32 ticks | 24..180, default [PLACEHOLDER] 45 | content constant | fixed simulation length of `ARRIVING` |
| `slip` | (i32, i32) px | `abs(slip) <= SLIP_MAX_TICKS × abs(v)` per axis | derived | **expected** pose offset from `p1` before BM7 re-asserts it (WM-13) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |

**Output Range:** `integrations` is `n` on an uninterrupted run with an empty queue. The worst case combines a full-queue delay (`a` = 8) with a restore just before the apply (`S = apply_T - 1`) and a second full-queue delay (`a'` = 8): `apply_T' = t0 + n + SLIP_MAX_TICKS`, so `integrations` is in [n, n + `SLIP_MAX_TICKS`] and the slip is at most `SLIP_MAX_TICKS` (15) px at the default speed and 240 px per axis at `VEL_MAX_PX_PER_TICK` [PLACEHOLDER]; the exterior is hidden by the pod-local camera and the black hold, and BM7 forces the exact pose either way. `in_pod_ticks` is `n + ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS` normally and at most `n + ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS + IN_POD_SLACK_TICKS` (30) in the worst case; with the maximum `n` 2190 and `ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] 180 that is 2,400 ticks (40 s), far below `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` 7200 (the autosave cap cannot trip inside one leg), and exactly the registry's sum-lint ceiling (400 Suit runs, WM-17): the pod's reserve cost is bounded at 120,000 mGU (44.4 % of `RESERVE_MAX_MGU`, Suit D.1), the figures at the defaults being 945 ticks nominal, 975 with slack and 2,265 at `HOP_LEG_TICKS_MAX` with the default settle. (An `n` of 2400 would have given 2,610 ticks = 43.5 s, past the ceiling, which is why `HOP_LEG_TICKS_MAX` is 2190.) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R6)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Example:** `t0 = 10000`, `n = 900`, `ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] 45: `submit_T = 10899`, `apply_T = 10900` (`a` = 1), `slip = (0, 0)`, `arrive_T = 10900`, `submit_Ts = 10944`, `settle_T = 10945`, `in_pod_ticks = 945` = 15.75 s (the tick table in States and Transitions). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **Case A, a restore after tick 10899** drops the pending `pod_arrive`: `submit_T' = 10900`, `apply_T' = 10901`, `integrations = 901`, `slip = v × 1 = (1, 0)`, logged `EXPECTED_RESTORE_SLIP`, and the apply sets the pose to `p1 = (1668, 384)`; `arrive_T` is still 10900 (nominal). The settle is keyed to the nominal `arrive_T`, not to the apply tick, and nothing is pending for it at the snapshot, so `submit_Ts = 10944` and **`settle_T = 10945` in both runs**, `first_boarded_T = 10945`, and there are no accruals. **Case B, a snapshot after tick 10944** drops the pending `pod_settle` itself: it is resubmitted at 10945 and applies at **10946** (lag 1), `first_boarded_T` is still the nominal 10945, and the lag-1 accruals of WM-13 appear. **Kernel-change lint (AC-WM-124):** `SLIP_MAX_TICKS` and `IN_POD_SLACK_TICKS` are recomputed from the kernel's published `queue_drain_ticks` at every build, and L3 and every figure that quotes 15, 30 or 2,400 re-run and fail if the kernel value changes without this document. A queue delay `a = 4` applies at 10903 with `slip = (3, 0)`, also expected; an observed offset of `(5, 0)` at that apply is `LEG_POSE_MISMATCH`. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

### D.3 `return_leg` (derived, never authored)

The `return_leg` formula is defined as:

`p0_r = -approach_offset`; `v_r = -v`; `n_r = n`; `p1_r = p0_r + v_r × n_r`; `home_pose_r = -target_pose` (the origin of the return is the derelict at the origin, the re-origin of WM-6).

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `approach_offset` | (i32, i32) | D.1 | derived from the outbound leg | the outbound target pose minus `p1` |
| `target_pose` | (i32, i32) | D.1 | derived | the outbound target body pose with HOME at the origin |
| `p0_r`, `p1_r` | (i32, i32) | within the D.1 budget | derived | return start and end poses of the pod, with the derelict body at the origin |
| `home_pose_r` | (i32, i32) | within the D.1 budget | derived | the home body's pose after the departure re-origin |

**Output Range:** the return leg is the exact time reverse of the outbound leg, so the exactness and budget lints **L1 to L6 hold for it whenever they hold outbound** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* (L7, the reveal window of D.6, applies to outbound legs only: a return leg ends at HOME, which carries no residual lamp, and the astern departure lamp is not a reveal; L8 `LEG_FOUL` is evaluated explicitly for each direction because the return meets the home shape and the other bodies' slot-relative poses as they stand at the departure, WM-6), and it needs no persisted extra data beyond `leg_dir` (the active leg's resolved values are persisted, D.1). The pod ends at the home dock exterior: `home_pose_r + pod_exit_px == p1_r`, an identity that the exactness lint checks.

**Example:** From D.1 (derelict A): `p0_r = (528, 36)`, `v_r = (-1, 0)`, `n_r = 900` -> `p1_r = (528 - 900, 36) = (-372, 36)`; `home_pose_r = (-1140, -348)`; check `home_pose_r + pod_exit_px = (-1140 + 768, -348 + 384) = (-372, 36) = p1_r`. The residual lamp is in the pod's wake: `exterior_anchor_px` relative to the derelict at the origin is `(576, 84)`, behind and 48 px south of the pod's start `(528, 36)`.

### D.4 `hop_fuel` (cost, validate and apply)

The `hop_fuel` formula is defined as:

`cost = HOP_FUEL_ML` if the command is `pod_hop` and `pod_at == HOME`; `RETURN_HOP_FUEL_ML` [PLACEHOLDER] for `pod_hop` from a derelict; `BERTH_FUEL_ML` [PLACEHOLDER] for `berth`; `UNBERTH_FUEL_ML` [PLACEHOLDER] for `unberth`. `pool = sum of stock_ml over FUEL_TANK nodes in sections whose kind is HOME`. **Validate:** `FUEL_LOW` iff (`pod_hop` from HOME or `berth`) and `pool - cost < HOP_FUEL_FLOOR_ML`; the floor test is **not applied** to a hop from a derelict or to `unberth`. **Apply:** `granted = withdraw_store(tank_i, remaining)` over the payer tanks in ascending node id until `cost` is met; `granted_total <= cost`.

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `HOP_FUEL_ML` [PLACEHOLDER] | i32 mL | 100..`floor(CAMPAIGN_HOP_SHARE_PM x tank_capacity / (6 x 1000))` = 400 at the defaults (derived from the BLOCKING lint AC-WM-37a: six sorties of hop fuel at most 12 % of the tank; the former box 100..2000 contained values the lint rejects), default [PLACEHOLDER] 400 (the lint's ceiling) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | this GDD | departure from HOME (the round trip is prepaid) |
| `RETURN_HOP_FUEL_ML` [PLACEHOLDER] | i32 mL | 0..400, default [PLACEHOLDER] 0 | this GDD | a hop from a derelict; kept at 0 |
| `BERTH_FUEL_ML` [PLACEHOLDER] | i32 mL | 200..`floor(CAMPAIGN_HOP_SHARE_PM x tank_capacity / 1000) - 3 x HOP_FUEL_ML` = 1,200 at the defaults (derived from the end-to-end script clause of AC-WM-37a: three hops plus one berth at most 12 %; the former box 200..4000 contained values the lint rejects), default [PLACEHOLDER] 800 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | this GDD | `berth` |
| `UNBERTH_FUEL_ML` [PLACEHOLDER] | i32 mL | 0..800, default [PLACEHOLDER] 0 | this GDD | `unberth` (never `FUEL_LOW`) |
| `HOP_FUEL_FLOOR_ML` [PLACEHOLDER] | i32 mL | `floor_min(T_SORTIE_SAFE_TICKS)`..4000 (1,440 at the default 28,800 ticks; 2,700 at 54,000 ticks, D.5), default [PLACEHOLDER] 1500 | this GDD; derived in D.5 | pool that must remain after the debit |
| `CAMPAIGN_HOP_SHARE_PM` [PLACEHOLDER] *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | i32 permille | 120 (range 60..200) | this GDD | the campaign share that six sorties of hop fuel may take of the tank (12 % at the default): the number AC-WM-37a lints, named as a knob so the `HOP_FUEL_ML` and `BERTH_FUEL_ML` ranges are derived from it, not boxed independently |
| `pool` | i32 mL | 0..20,000 | Graph `read_view.node(tank).stock` (FUEL_TANK `stock_ml`, LSG:111) | persisted stock of the HOME-kind tanks (MVP: `ft_main`, 20,000 mL at wake) |
| `granted` | i32 mL | 0..cost | Graph M16 `withdraw_store` (Class B, clamps to stock) | the amount actually withdrawn; equals `cost` whenever validate passed in the same tick |

**Output Range:** a validated apply withdraws exactly `cost`; `granted < cost` cannot occur on a validated command (one command per apply, no interleaving) and, if it ever does, is logged `transit_fuel_short` and the hop **still proceeds** (never strand). The smallest pool that allows a hop from HOME is `HOP_FUEL_ML + HOP_FUEL_FLOOR_ML` = 1,900 mL, and a berth 2,300 mL (accepted when equal). A return hop and an `unberth` are accepted at any pool, including 0.

**Example:** Full tank: `pool = 20,000` -> hop from HOME accepted, `pool' = 19,600`; the return hop from A: `cost = 0`, `pool' = 19,600` (no floor test, so a home that SCRAMmed while the crew was away does not strand them away). Boundaries: `pool = 1,900` accepted (-> 1,500); `pool = 1,899` `FUEL_LOW`, nothing changes and no event. `berth` at 2,300 accepted, at 2,299 `FUEL_LOW`. The MVP end-to-end script home -> A -> home -> B -> home -> berth B -> weld -> A debits 400, 0, 400, 0, 800, 400 mL = 2,000 mL = 10 % of the tank. Six sorties of 400 mL are 2,400 mL = 12 % of the tank (a timer, not the managed loop that the Graph's `FUEL_UML_PER_W` row, LSG:958, forbids for MVP). **Binding order of the constraints on a sortie** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* (creative-director view: this suits the 'committed crossing' fantasy): (1) **the derelict's lamp clock** (D.10) ends a visit's usefulness first, (2) **the reactor's burn** (a function of the home's draw; D.5) bounds the campaign length, and (3) **the flat hop toll binds last**; it is never the rationing constraint (the hop is 12 % of the tank over six sorties) and is a **legibility device**: it exists so that the departure feels paid for, not to ration hops. The guards of D.5 are evaluated at one draw basis, the rated 2,500 W (Graph LSG:111, LSG:958); other draws are reported, not gated. **Campaign-scale target** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* (creative-director position): fuel is a campaign-scale timer. The tension of a sortie comes from time away and the reactor's burn, not from the hop toll. With no faucet *(conditional: after a weld, B's `ft_b`, about 10,000 mL at its start state, may be a hidden source of up to about 55 minutes of burn at 2,500 W or a dead asset, depending on which tank the Graph burns first; the claim and every campaign-length figure are verified only once the Graph publishes its tank draw order or Authoring authors `ft_b` small, 2,000-3,000 mL, OQ-WM-50, AC-WM-37d)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* the 20,000 mL tank (111 minutes at full rated draw) fits the 60-120-minute campaign; six sorties of hop fuel are held to at most 12 % of the tank (BLOCKING Config lint AC-WM-37a); and over the 60-minute low end of the campaign the reactor at 2500 W burns about 10,800 mL, 4.5 times the six-sortie hop total (2,400 mL). `FUEL_LOW`, the reserve marks and a dark lamp on return are therefore **not reachable in a 30-minute playtest by play alone**; they are exercised by scripted start-state fixtures (tank near the floor, lamp battery pre-drained), not by retuning (AC-WM-37b).

### D.5 `fuel_floor` (why `HOP_FUEL_FLOOR_ML` [PLACEHOLDER] is 1500, the sortie share and the conservation term)

The `fuel_floor` formula is defined as:

`floor_min = ceil(P_rated_W × FUEL_UML_PER_W × T_safe_steps / 1,000,000)` mL; `F_sortie = HOP_FUEL_ML + ceil(P_W × FUEL_UML_PER_W × steps_away / 1,000,000)`; `hop_share = HOP_FUEL_ML / F_sortie`; time-equivalent of the debit `t_hop_steps = HOP_FUEL_ML × 1,000,000 / (P_W × FUEL_UML_PER_W)`; conservation (ASK-G2, ACCEPTED): `sum fuel + sum fuel_puddle_ml + burned + sum withdrawn_fuel_ml = constant`, where the Graph's M16 adds `granted` to the section's `withdrawn_*` counter in the same apply (LS-18); no Web Map & Transit counter exists. **Tuning guard (self-consistent form):** `HOP_FUEL_ML <= burn_over_T_safe / 3`, with `burn_over_T_safe = ceil(P_W × FUEL_UML_PER_W × steps_away / 1,000,000)`, which is exactly `hop_share <= 25 %` (`H / (H + B) <= 1/4` iff `3H <= B`); the earlier form `HOP_FUEL_ML <= 0.25 × F_sortie_typical` was circular because `F_sortie` contains the hop. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `P_rated_W` | i32 W | 2500 | Graph (reactor rating used in LSG:111, LSG:958) | the burn the floor must outlast |
| `FUEL_UML_PER_W` | i32 µmL per W per step | 40..400, default 120 | LSG Tuning Knobs, LSG:958 (not a registry entry) | the Graph's burn rate; one Graph step is 6 ticks (divisor 6), so 10 steps per second |
| `T_safe_steps` | i32 steps | derived from `T_SORTIE_SAFE_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] 28,800 ticks / 6 = 4,800 | this GDD | the sortie the floor is meant to survive: 8 minutes at rated draw with the authored drip repaired, not a limit on visit length *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `P_W`, `steps_away` | i32 W, steps | `P_W` 1..2500 (`P_W` = 0 would divide by zero in `t_hop_steps` and is outside the range: a dead home has no time-equivalent), `steps_away` 0..~20,000 | Graph (home draw), session | the home's actual draw and the time away *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `FUEL_STARVE_STEPS` | i32 steps | 100 | LSG Tuning Knobs, LSG:957 (not a registry entry) | the Graph's starve-to-SCRAM timer (10 s) |
| `W_MIN_WARNING_S` | i32 s | 10 | REG | the fairness window (600 ticks) |
| `withdrawn_*` (sum of the sections' fuel withdrawn counters) | i32 mL | monotone, 0.. | Graph LS-18 M16 (ASK-G2, accepted) | the conservation term this debit lands in *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |

**Output Range:** `floor_min = ceil(2500 × 120 × 4800 / 1,000,000) = 1,440` mL; the default 1,500 leaves 60 mL = 1,200 ticks = 20 s of margin at full draw. Below 1,440 mL the safe-sortie guarantee is lost; the upper bound 4,000 mL is the point at which hops are refused with usable fuel. The floor is 1,500 mL = 30,000 ticks = 500 s of full-draw burn, 50 times both the `FUEL_STARVE_STEPS` window (600 ticks) and `W_MIN_WARNING_S` (600 ticks), so a `FUEL_LOW` refusal always arrives long before any fairness-window event. **What the floor guarantees** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: it protects an **8-minute sortie at rated draw with the authored FUEL-DRIP repaired**. It is not a promise to the longer visits of D.10's examples (14 to 18 minutes), and it is void while any fuel-path fault (the drip, a leak) is unrepaired, because the tank then also loses 600 mL per minute to the puddle and the unrepaired drip ends in SCRAM, never in `FUEL_LOW`. `T_SORTIE_SAFE_TICKS` is the intended *safe* sortie and not a cap on visits; the tension of a longer visit is the point (E-62). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **Floor sizing, recorded (OQ-WM-49):** the painted pod mark ('below this line the pod will not leave') will read to players as a safe line, yet it covers an 8-minute sortie while D.10 plans 14 to 18 minute visits, so a normal-length visit late in the campaign can end at a SCRAMmed home with every hop refused. The creative-director's lean is to size the floor from the median planned visit (about 15 minutes = 54,000 ticks, `floor_min` = `ceil(2500 x 120 x 9000 / 1,000,000)` = 2,700 mL, the marks then at 3,100 mL = 15.5 % and 3,500 mL = 17.5 %) and widen the `T_SORTIE_SAFE_TICKS` range accordingly (done below); the alternative is to keep 1,500 mL and record the late overrun as the designed 'last sortie' tension with a diegetic cue that the mark guarantees only a short trip. The default [PLACEHOLDER] 1,500 is **not changed by this revision** (R10: a PLACEHOLDER moves only with its balance pass; the derivation is not internally inconsistent), the sizing is routed to the kill/continue playtest with the metric AC-WM-37c, and the lean is the recommended retune. 'Never strand' is about the **return hop**, which is never refused (E-03); the floor protects the reactor, not the arrival state. The floor must not stay a hidden threshold (P1): WM-20 routes two painted reserve marks on the home FUEL_TANK at the refusal thresholds, floor + hop cost = 1,900 mL = 9.5 % and floor + berth cost = 2,300 mL = 11.5 % of the tank's height, not at the floor (ASK-G3 accepted as prop art, ASK-AD-9 open) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; until they land, its witness is the console's refusal reason, and the pool is witnessed only by the Graph's sight-glass (corruptible module-body channel) and consequence channel (drip, `PUDDLE` descriptor id 25, SCRAM); there is no Web Map & Transit meter.

**Example:** Burn at full draw: `2500 × 120 = 300,000` µmL per step = 0.3 mL per step = 3 mL/s = 180 mL/min (the tank lasts 111 min, LSG:111). One hop debit of 400 mL equals `400 / 180 = 2.2` min of full-power burn; at `P_W` 1200 W the burn is 86.4 mL/min and the same debit is 4.6 min. A sortie of 8 minutes away at 2500 W: `F_sortie = 400 + 1,440 = 1,840` mL, `hop_share = 21.7 %`; at 1200 W: `400 + 692 = 1,092` mL, `hop_share = 36.6 %`. **Tuning guard (flagged; self-consistent form, design review):** the bound is `HOP_FUEL_ML <= burn_over_T_safe / 3`: at 2500 W the 8-minute burn is 1,440 mL so the bound is 480 mL and 400 passes; at 1200 W the burn is 692 mL so the bound is 230 mL and 400 fails (hop_share 36.6 %). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **One basis, and the guard is reported, not gated:** the single draw basis for every guard in this section is the rated 2,500 W (`P_rated_W`, the Graph's reactor rating, LSG:111); the 1,200 W row is a reported datum, and `hop_share` and the first-`FUEL_LOW` sortie index are **reported by AC-WM-37 without gating** (the 25 % share guard fails by construction at 1,200 W and the toll is a legibility device, D.4). The campaign-share check (D.4: six sorties = 12 % of the tank, AC-WM-37a) is the only gate. Conservation: before a departure `20,000 + 0 + 0 + 0 = 20,000`; after it `19,600 + 0 + 0 + 400 = 20,000` (the 400 lands in the Graph's `withdrawn_*` counter, ASK-G2 accepted, WM-20). The time until the last allowed hop with no hops spent and 2500 W is `(20,000 - 1,900) / 180 = 100.6` min. Until the authored FUEL-DRIP on `pf_feed` is repaired (LSG D.15, DA-15), the tank also loses 1 mL per step (600 mL/min) to the puddle, which dwarfs a hop debit; the 12 % campaign-share check is measured with the drip repaired, and the playtest AC-WM-37 reports both. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

### D.6 `reveal_window` (the lamp-in-view lint)

The `reveal_window` formula is defined as:

`view_ticks = min over axes with v_axis != 0 of floor((F_axis - |s_axis|) / |v_axis|)`, valid when the velocity on that axis points toward the lamp, and **0** if any axis with `v_axis = 0` has `|s_axis| > F_axis`; lint `view_ticks >= LAMP_VIEW_MIN_TICKS`, evaluated at the **narrowest** field of the zoom ladder **on OUTBOUND legs only**. Integer comparison without division: `F_axis >= |s_axis|` **and** `(F_axis - |s_axis|)² >= LAMP_VIEW_MIN_TICKS² × v_axis²` **and** `sign(v_axis) == sign(s_axis)` for each axis with `v_axis != 0` (the direction conjunct, *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: without it a leg flying away from the lamp passes the first two conjuncts, because the squared test is blind to sign; `s_axis` is the lamp's signed offset from the pod's final position, so a pod moving toward the lamp has the same sign). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The first conjunct is a separate, explicit test: the squared test alone passes falsely when `F_axis < |s_axis|`, because a negative difference squares to a positive. With the exterior cull (WM-18) `F_axis` is `min(field half-extent, EXTERIOR_CULL_RADIUS_PX)`, a rectangular **upper bound** (it over-estimates) of the circular cull *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: the lamp actually enters the circle when `x_enter² + s_y² <= EXTERIOR_CULL_RADIUS_PX²` (integer squared distances, WM-37), which at the default lateral 48 is `x_enter` = 185 px along the track.

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `F_x`, `F_y` | i32 base px | (160, 90) at the narrowest supported field; (192, 108) at 1080p and k = 5 | Pipeline D.5: `field = (floor(W / k), floor(H / k))`, `k` up to `k_auto + 2` | half-extent of the visible world field about the pod-local camera centre |
| `s_axis` | i32 base px | along-track `LEG_STANDOFF_PX` [PLACEHOLDER] 48 (24..96), lateral `LEG_LATERAL_PX` [PLACEHOLDER] 48 (0..66) | D.1 | the lamp's offset from the pod's final position |
| `v_axis` | i32 px per tick | D.1 | D.1 | leg velocity component |
| `LAMP_VIEW_MIN_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] | i32 ticks | **60 <= `LAMP_VIEW_MIN_TICKS` <= `floor((F_x_narrowest - LEG_STANDOFF_PX) / abs(v))`**, a single inequality tied to the standoff and the speed, not a box (136 at the shallowest standoff 24 and v 1, 112 at the default standoff 48, 64 at the deepest standoff 96, 56 at the default standoff with v 2; a larger value is unreachable and fails the lint), default [PLACEHOLDER] 90 (1.5 s) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | this GDD | the minimum time the warm point is on screen |

**Output Range:** 0 (the lamp is never on screen) to `n`. **The narrowest field is not 1080p k = 5.** The Pipeline's minimum window is 1280 × 720 with `k_auto` 2, so the ladder top `k = 4` gives a 320 × 180 field (half-extent 160 × 90); the Steam Deck's 1280 × 800 gives 320 × 200; 1080p gives 384 × 216 (192 × 108) at `k` = 5. The lint therefore uses (160, 90). The lateral bound `LEG_LATERAL_PX <= F_y - 24 = 66` keeps the lamp inside the field's height with a margin. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Derived constraints replace independent ranges:** a legal authored leg is axis-aligned (one velocity component zero) at `|v|` 1 or 2 with `LEG_STANDOFF_PX <= 160 - LAMP_VIEW_MIN_TICKS × |v|` along the track; a velocity with both components non-zero passes only if both axes pass (the example `v = (1, 1)` fails on y); the Tuning Knobs boxes are bounded by these inequalities and the corner-sweep AC-WM-125 evaluates every corner of the box, listing the failing corners instead of hiding them. **Upper bound at wide zoom:** at 4K 1x the field half-extent is (1920, 1080), so without the cull the whole leg and the target are visible for hundreds of ticks and the wreck stops resolving out of the dark; the cull caps the effective half-extent at 192 px, so the lamp is in view for at most 137 ticks at the default at every zoom step (the circle test gives `x_enter` 185 and 185 - 48 = 137; the rectangular `floor((192 - 48) / 1)` = 144 is an **upper bound** on the circle's 137, not a conservative one: it over-estimates the view time by 7 ticks at the default, so the lint should use the circle test when the cull is on) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, and zoom stays free. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*

**Example:** Derelict A with the D.1 numbers: `s = (48, 48)`, `v = (1, 0)`. x axis: `floor((160 - 48) / 1) = 112`; y axis has `v_y = 0` and `|s_y| = 48 <= 90`, so it does not limit: `view_ticks = 112 >= 90` (squared check `112² = 12,544 >= 90² × 1 = 8,100`). At 1080p and k = 5: `floor((192 - 48) / 1) = 144`; at the default 640 × 360 field: `floor((320 - 48) / 1) = 272` (with the cull of WM-18 the 272 becomes 137 by the circle test, 144 by the rectangular approximation, because the cull caps the view at 192 px). **Failing sets (why the earlier numbers were replaced):** `v = (-8, 3)` with a 300 px standoff has `|s_y| = 300 > 90`, so `view_ticks = 0`; `v = (2, 0)` gives `floor(112 / 2) = 56 < 90`; `v = (1, 1)` is limited on y to `floor((90 - 48) / 1) = 42 < 90`. The default `LAMP_VIEW_MIN_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] is 90 rather than 120 because 112 is the most the narrowest field allows at a 48 px standoff and speed 1. Whether the lamp's art glow radius widens the effective field is the art director's constant; it may relax `LAMP_VIEW_MIN_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] but never a simulation value. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

### D.7 `berth_path` (derived start, exact closing offset)

The `berth_path` formula is defined as:

`r = world(B) - world(home)`; `Δ = sum over i of (v_i × n_i)` (the home's displacement; `v_i` is the **home** velocity in phase `i`); `r_start = frame_shift_px + Δ`; `r(t_end) = r_start - Δ = frame_shift_px`; `BERTH_TICKS = sum of n_i`. `unberth` plays the path in reverse (velocities negated, phases in reverse order), so `r` returns to `r_start`.

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `frame_shift_px` | (i32, i32) base px | (696, 384) for the authored pair | derived: `line_px` 720 minus the 24 px seam, and the 384 px start-axis difference (REG `mvp_template_roster` sockets); never the (576, 96) Layout fixture | the world offset at which the two sockets mate |
| `m` | i32 | 1..3, recommended 2 | content | phase count |
| `v_i` | (i32, i32) base px per tick | each axis within `VEL_MAX_PX_PER_TICK` [PLACEHOLDER] 16 | content | home velocity in phase `i` |
| `n_i` | i32 ticks | >= 1 | content | phase length |
| `BERTH_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] | i32 ticks | 240..1800, default [PLACEHOLDER] 624 | derived | total berth duration |
| `r_start` | (i32, i32) base px | derived | **derived**, persisted for the active berth (WM-30) | the B-minus-home offset set by BM7 at the berth apply |

**Lints (structural, so no equality lint is needed):** `m <= 3`; the last phase has `|v_m| <= BERTH_CONTACT_V` 1 px per tick per axis and `n_m >= BERTH_CONTACT_TICKS` 24 (soft `BERTHED` contact); `r_start` lies outside the visible field: `r_start.x² + r_start.y² >= EXTERIOR_VISIBLE_RADIUS_PX²` ([PLACEHOLDER] 2400; the half-diagonal of the largest ladder field, 3840 × 2160 at 1× on a 4K display, is 2,203 px); no overlap at any `t` (every `v_i` component has the sign that reduces `|r|` on that axis); all coordinates within `WEB_COORD_BUDGET_PX` [PLACEHOLDER]; per-axis `|v_i| <= VEL_MAX_PX_PER_TICK` [PLACEHOLDER].

**Output Range:** `docked(home, B, frame_shift_px)` holds at the last `berth_step` by equality, not tolerance (D.8). The 24-tick crawl at 1 px per tick covers exactly the 24 px of seam engagement (the offset closes from `line_px` 720 to 696). With BM7 present the start offset is computed, which removes the gcd obstacle: `gcd(696, 384) = 24`, so a BM7-free single equal-tick constant-velocity phase from a separated start would need `n` to divide 24 and force `|vx| >= 696 / 24 = 29 > 16`.

**Example:** (illustrative [PLACEHOLDER]) Cruise `(3, 0) × 600`, then contact crawl `(1, 0) × 24`: `Δ = (1800 + 24, 0) = (1824, 0)`; `r_start = (696 + 1824, 384) = (2520, 384)`; `|r_start|² = 6,350,400 + 147,456 = 6,497,856 >= 2400² = 5,760,000`; `BERTH_TICKS = 624` = 10.4 s; home speed 3 px per tick (the berth is not drawn). With the home at the origin the apply sets `world(B) = (2520, 384)`; at the end `world(home) = (1824, 0)` and `world(B) - world(home) = (696, 384)`. The earlier three-phase 510-tick path `(12, -4) × 300`, `(6, -2) × 120`, `(2, 0) × 90` (`Δ = (4500, -1440)`) is exact but fails the D.7 lints: its last phase speed (2) exceeds `BERTH_CONTACT_V` 1, and its y component carries r_y from -1056 through 0 to 384, so |r_y| grows. It serves as the failing fixture of AC-WM-41. The two-phase path was chosen; it also has one fewer boundary command exposed to restore loss (orchestrator ruling, checkpoint OQ-WM-9).

### D.8 `docked` establishment

The `docked` formula is defined as:

`docked(a, b, frame_shift_px) = (vel(a) == vel(b)) and (world(b) - world(a) == frame_shift_px); delta_abs = frame_shift_px - (world(b) - world(a))` (quoted verbatim from the registry `docking_invariant`)

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `vel(a)`, `vel(b)` | (i32, i32) base px per tick | (0, 0) at the last `berth_step` | Body pose rows, set by BM6 | the two bodies' velocities |
| `world(a)`, `world(b)` | (i32, i32) base px | within `WEB_COORD_BUDGET_PX` [PLACEHOLDER] | Body pose rows, set by BM7 | world poses |
| `frame_shift_px` | (i32, i32) | (696, 384) | D.7 | the authored socket pair offset |
| `delta_abs` | (i32, i32) | (0, 0) when docked | derived | the signed shortfall |

**Output Range:** Web Map & Transit **establishes** the predicate at the last `berth_step` (pose-carrying BM7 plus BM6 zero on both bodies), Body **tests** it, and Assembly **gates the offer** on it. It is then **held**: this system issues no BM6 or BM7 on either body until the weld, in particular while an ASSEMBLY weld channel is open (WM-22).

**Example:** At the end of the D.7 path: `world(home) = (1824, 0)`, `world(B) = (2520, 384)`, both velocities (0, 0): `world(B) - world(home) = (696, 384)`, `docked` true, `delta_abs = (0, 0)`. Had the home stopped 24 px short, `world(home) = (1800, 0)`: the offset is (720, 384), `docked` false, `delta_abs = (-24, 0)` (the shape of the registry's own second example, on the authored pair rather than the Layout fixture).

### D.9 `loaded_set_budget` (DA-32 quoted)

The `loaded_set_budget` formula is defined as:

`tiles(S) = sum of V(section) over the sections loaded in S`, `+ V_pod_body` when the pod body is counted; `subj(S) = sum of the Interaction subjects registered in S`; legal sets (REG `legal_loaded_sets`): S0 {home}; S1 {home, A}; S2 {home, B}; S3 {home + B welded}; S4 {home + B, A}.

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `V(home)`, `V(A)`, `V(B)`, `V(pod)` | i32 tiles | 182, 118, 149, 4 | REG `mvp_template_roster` | roster volumes |
| `S_int` | i32 subjects | 70 / 102 / 113 / 113 / 145 | REG `legal_loaded_sets` | Interaction subjects per set, INFO upper bounds as loaded-set sums (home 70 after DA-47's 2 scrubber slots; A adds 32, B adds 43: F 40 + L_seed 3, after the second pipe fitting placement is removed) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* |
| `INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET` [PLACEHOLDER] | i32 subjects | 120 | REG (Interaction System) | the content ceiling for the per-registration-window sum at every authored body position, not for S_int over a loaded set *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)* |
| `TILES_WORLD_MVP` | i32 tiles | 600, WARN at 540 | LY D.13 (a Layout Tuning Knob, not a registry entry) | the world tile budget |
| `BODIES_LIVE_MAX` [PLACEHOLDER] | i32 bodies | 4 [PLACEHOLDER], range 2..8 | REG | live-body cap; Lighting defines *live* as built and with a non-dormant section or hosting the avatar (LO-24), so at most 2 are live here *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |

**Output Range:** tiles 182 / 300 / 331 / 331 / 449 (186 / 304 / 335 / 335 / 453 with the pod's 4 tiles); the peak 453 is 83.9 % of the 540 WARN and 75.5 % of 600 (87 tiles of headroom to the WARN, 147 to the cap). Subjects: S_int is 70 / 102 / 113 / 113 / 145 as INFO upper bounds; the budget applies to registered neighbourhoods ({home} 70, {A} 50, {B} 61, {home+B} 113), each at or under `INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET` [PLACEHOLDER] 120, so S4 is no longer an overrun (DA-47, D37) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. **Dormancy at once is necessary but not sufficient:** all four S4 members are legitimately live, but the subjects register by adjacency or proximity, so this system has no lever on subject counts. Live (non-dormant) bodies: S0 1, S1 2, S2 2, S3 1, S4 2; the pod section is always dormant; built bodies: 4 / 4 / 4 / 3 / 3 (home, A, B, pod before the weld; the weld merges B into the home body), with the cut adding one orphan back (MSA-41). During a leg the loaded set is {home}.

**Example:** `182 + 118 = 300` (S1); `182 + 149 = 331` (S2, and S3 once B is welded); `331 + 118 = 449` (S4); `449 + 4 = 453`. Subjects: `70 + 32 = 102`; `70 + 43 = 113` (B: F 40 + L_seed 3); `113 + 32 = 145` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. The transitions this system causes: S0 to S1 (hop to A, live at `pod_settle`); S1 to S0 (hop home, A dormant at the departure apply); S0 to S2 (hop to B, or `berth` B); S2 to S0 (B dormant on the hop home or on `unberth`); S2 to S3 (weld); S3 to S4 (hop to A); S4 to S3 (hop home); S3 to an S0-shaped set (cut: the orphan is dormant). `BERTH_PENDING`, `TARGET_BERTHED` and the star topology make every set outside S0 to S4 unreachable.

### D.10 `loaded_exposure` (residual-lamp clock and compression per visit)

The `loaded_exposure` formula is defined as:

`L_loaded(d) = sum over visits of (T_depart_apply - T_settle) + berthed_ticks(d)` (legs add 0: both derelicts are dormant); lamp: `lamp_ticks(d) = floor(battery_dJ(d) / LAMP_DJ_PER_STEP) × 6`, `lamp_lit(d) = (L_loaded(d) < lamp_ticks(d))` under the chain predicate of WM-18; compression: `rate_pm = 1,000,000 / DERELICT_FAULT_COMPRESSION`, `Δhp = floor((Δhp_ovl + Δhp_wear) × rate_pm × stress_pm / 1,000,000)` per wear period of `WEAR_PERIOD_STEPS` steps (the Graph's D.9, cited, not restated).

This is a planning estimate under no other draw on the battery. The rendered state is always WM-18's live chain predicate over the persisted stock, so a Suit top-up from `b_a1` / `b_b1` (DA:802) shortens the clock (for example 100,000 dJ withdrawn leaves 22,000 steps for A). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Lamp-minutes cost per top-up:** each `amount_dJ` withdrawn costs `amount_dJ / LAMP_DJ_PER_STEP × 6` ticks of lamp life, so 100,000 dJ costs 5,000 steps = 30,000 ticks = 8.3 minutes of A's 45 (and of B's 36). A lamp found dark on return after the player topped the suit up from its battery is therefore the player's own action and not time away; the DIAGNOSTIC `lamp_topup_dJ` (per derelict, logged at each `top_up` from a residual battery) lets playtest logs attribute it (E-77, AC-WM-126).

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `battery_dJ(d)` | i32 dJ | A 540,000 (`b_a1`), B 432,000 (`b_b1`) | REG `visible_unknown` | the residual battery's stock at wake |
| `LAMP_DJ_PER_STEP` | i32 dJ per step | 20 (200 dJ/s = 20 W) | REG `visible_unknown` (27,000 steps and 21,600 steps) | the lamp's draw |
| `DERELICT_FAULT_COMPRESSION` | i32 permille | 500 (range 250..1000) | REG (cited by name, value and range only) | derelict wear compression, applies to endogenous damage on a **loaded** DERELICT section only |
| `rate_pm` | i32 | 2000 at 500 | LSG D.9 (`10^6 / DERELICT_FAULT_COMPRESSION`) | wear multiplier in permille (1000 for a HOME section) |
| `WEAR_PERIOD_STEPS` | i32 steps | 100 (10 s) | LSG Tuning Knobs | the wear cadence |
| `berthed_ticks(d)` | i32 ticks | 0.. | this system | ticks from the `berth` apply to the weld or the `unberth` end: B is **live with nobody aboard** |

**Output Range:** the transit machine controls only the loaded minutes; the Graph owns every rate. `lamp_ticks(A) = 27,000 × 6 = 162,000` ticks = 45 min and `lamp_ticks(B) = 21,600 × 6 = 129,600` ticks = 36 min of **loaded** time; a dark lamp is reached only by loaded minutes, never by legs or by time at home. `rate_pm` is 2000 (x2) at the default and 4000 (x4) at the range floor 250; the Graph's `BREACH_PROPAGATION_MULT` multiplies on top (LSG:905: product at most x4 at defaults).

**Example:** Visits to A of 14, 18 and 15 minutes: after two visits 32 min are used and 13 remain; the third arrival leg shows the lamp lit, it goes dark 13 minutes into that visit, and the fourth approach shows a dark hull, an honest reading. The 30-minute script (A about 8 min, B about 8 min, berth about 10.4 s plus the weld channel about 5 min) keeps both lamps lit (A 8 of 45 min; B about 13 of 36). Compression, using the Graph's own FUEL-DRIP flange wear of 16 bp per period (LSG D.15, illustrative here, the Graph owns all values): a derelict section with 999 bp of margin to `FAILED` loses `floor(16 × 2000 × 1000 / 1,000,000) = 32` bp per period, so `ceil(999 / 32) = 32` periods = 320 s = 5.3 min of loaded time, against 63 periods = 10.5 min at the HOME rate: a visit that stays 5 minutes longer meets a worse derelict, and a hop home stops the clock at once.

### D.11 `block_budget` (record size and dirty keys)

The `block_budget` formula is defined as:

`bytes = sum over records of (record_bytes + 8 × fields) + machine_bytes + 8 × machine_fields`; dirty keys per apply `<= 2 + records_touched`.

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `record_bytes` | i32 | 37 (4+4+1+1+1+8+1+1+8+4+4) | WM-3 | canonical payload of one record (11 fields) |
| `machine_bytes` | i32 | 125 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: +4 for `origin_id`, +1 for `leg_reversed`)* | WM-3 | `transit_state`, `leg_epoch`, `t0`, `arrive_T`, `t_phase`, `target_id`, `origin_id`, `leg_dir`, `leg_reversed`, the leg parameters (20), the berth fields and path (up to 3 phases), `pod_body_id`, `pod_section_id`, `pod_at` (32 fields) |
| `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` | i32 | 256 | REG | the kernel's per-block dirty-key ceiling per tick (above it the kernel reports `ALL`, a degradation, not a fault) |
| `MAX_WORLD_RECORDS` [PLACEHOLDER] | i32 | 8 [PLACEHOLDER], range 3..8 (the `world_slot` field is u8 0..7, so 8 is the ceiling) | this GDD | the schema ceiling for the Vertical Slice roster *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |

**Output Range:** three records `3 × (37 + 88) = 375` B plus the machine `125 + 256 = 381` B is about 756 B canonical (WM-3's "about 700") *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* *(Amended 2026-10-29 — sync knock-on fix: the figure and `machine_bytes` 125 are unchanged; `pod_arrived_seq` and `berthed_seq` are non-persisted host-local counters (WM-31), not machine fields, so they add no bytes and no dirty key)*, under 2 KB with field tags and well inside `repl_hint` SHARED_SUMMARY. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* At the ceiling of 8 records the block is `8 × 125 + 381 = 1,381` B, under 2 KB; the earlier ceiling of 16 would have been 2,360 B and broke the claim, which is why the ceiling is 8. Dirty keys: the departure apply 2 (`WEB/T`, `WEB/POD`); `pod_arrive` 2 (`WEB/T`, `WEB/<target>`); `pod_settle` 3 (`WEB/T`, `WEB/POD`, `WEB/<target>`); `berth` and each `berth_step` 1 (`WEB/T`) plus `WEB/<B>` on the last; `web_reconcile` at most 5; **zero per tick during a leg or a berth** (progress is derived from `T - t0`). Worst case 5 of 256. Neighbours' keys in the same applies: Graph 2 `SEC/` + 1 node + `T`; Body 3 to 4; Interaction up to 44 for B (per-subject keying `[UNV]`), all under 256.

**Example:** `pod_settle` at a first visit to A: `WEB/T` (machine IDLE, `arrive_T`), `WEB/POD` (`pod_at := A`), `WEB/A` (`status` BOARDED, `first_boarded_T`) = 3 keys; the same apply marks 2 Graph section keys, 1 Body pod key and the avatar key, none of which is this block's.

### D.12 `ring_of` (the depth reads)

The `ring_of` formula is defined as:

`ring_of(section_id) = record(owner(section_id)).ring` in this resolution order: (1) a record's own section; (2) a section welded into the home body resolves to HOME's ring; (3) the pod's section resolves to the ring of the record at `pod_at` when parked and mid-leg resolves to the ring of the departure record (the record the leg left, which equals Attention's `held_ring` by AT-22); Attention and Sanity use `held_ring` by their own rules; (4) an absent or sub-1 value resolves to 1 and is logged once (AT-6 order, AT E-61). `ring(PlayerId) = ring_of(actor_section(avatar))`; `body_is_home(PlayerId) = (body_of_actor(avatar) == home body)`; `locus_kind(PlayerId)` is HOME, POD or DERELICT.

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ring` | u8 | >= 1 (1-based; outer ring = 1) | the record (WM-3) | MVP: 1 for HOME, A and B (the pod's section resolves through `pod_at`, rule 3) |
| `actor_section`, `body_of_actor` | i32 | stable ids | Body pure reads | the avatar's section and body |
| `AMBIENT_RING_PM` | i32 permille | ring 1 / 2 / 3 = 30 / 15 / 5 [PLACEHOLDER] | REG (Depth Rings' rows beyond ring 1) | cited only |

**Output Range:** an integer >= 1; `post_restore` rejects `ring < 1` (never clamped). In the pod `body_is_home` is **false** and `locus_kind` is POD (the truthful read; Sanity decides whether to hold its exposure, OQ-WM-6).

**Example:** Avatar in `a_machine` on derelict A: `ring_of(a_machine) = 1`, `body_is_home = false`, `locus_kind = DERELICT`. After B is welded, the avatar in `b_breach` has `ring_of(b_breach) = 1` (rule 2), `body_is_home = true` (the welded section is part of the home body) while the Graph still treats the section as `kind` DERELICT (LS-17).

### D.13 `settle_lint` (the `ARRIVING` length against the largest build)

The `settle_lint` formula is defined as:

`ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS >= ceil(tiles_max / BUILD_TILES_PER_FRAME) × TICKS_PER_FRAME_WORST + ARRIVE_MARGIN_TICKS`, with `TICKS_PER_FRAME_WORST = ceil(TICK_RATE / MIN_FRAME_RATE)`.

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `tiles_max` | i32 tiles | 331 | the largest single destination scene: home + B welded (D.9) | tiles the projector builds at an arrival |
| `BUILD_TILES_PER_FRAME` | i32 tiles | 256, range >= 1 (a value below 1 is a division by zero and is rejected by the lint) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | LY Tuning Knobs, LY:1041 (not a registry entry) | the Layout builder's per-frame budget |
| `MIN_FRAME_RATE` [PLACEHOLDER] | i32 frames per second | 30 [PLACEHOLDER], range 1..`TICK_RATE` (>= 1 guards the division in `TICKS_PER_FRAME_WORST`; the Tuning Knobs range 24-60 is the playable subset) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | this GDD | the slowest frame rate the lint tolerates |
| `ARRIVE_MARGIN_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] | i32 ticks | 20 [PLACEHOLDER], range 10..60 | this GDD | slack for a slow projector |
| `W_MIN_WARNING_S` | i32 s | 10 | REG | the fairness window |

**Output Range:** `ceil(331 / 256) = 2` frames x 2 ticks per frame = 4 ticks, plus 20 = 24 ticks: the lower safe bound of `ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] at the defaults *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the bound is the inequality of the formula and moves with `MIN_FRAME_RATE` and `ARRIVE_MARGIN_TICKS`; at `MIN_FRAME_RATE` 24 it is `2 x 3 + 20 = 26`, so a settle of 24 passes at 30 fps and fails at 24 fps; the L3b floor `ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS - 1 > SLIP_MAX_TICKS` is a second lower bound, 17)*. The default 45 is 1.9x that bound and 7.5 % of `W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE` = 600 ticks, so even a late projector leaves the fairness window intact (the target is frozen until `pod_settle`). **Leg versus window** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the late-projector argument covers only the *settle* lag. The leg itself is longer than the window: `n + ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS` = 945 > `W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE` = 600 at the defaults (and any `n` above 600 exceeds it alone), so the fairness window does **not** dwarf the time the player spends in the pod. Lint `LEG_EXCEEDS_WINDOW` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: a boolean lint, not a sum)*: `LEG_EXCEEDS_WINDOW := (n + ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS + IN_POD_SLACK_TICKS > W_MIN_WARNING_S x TICK_RATE)`, true at every default (975 > 600); when true, the build reports the P2 pod-window exposure as the accepted price of leaving (WM-26b, delegated ruling R21: the companion deferral rule is withdrawn; no neighbour declaration is required and AC-WM-122 is ACTIVE). *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R21)*

**Example:** Return to a home that has B welded: 331 tiles -> 2 frames -> `2 × 2 + 20 = 24 <= 45` passes; first arrival at B alone: `ceil(149 / 256) = 1` frame -> `1 × 2 + 20 = 22 <= 45`.

### D.14 `coordinate_budget` (world slots and the exterior pose budget)

The `coordinate_budget` formula is defined as:

`init_pose(slot) = (slot × SLOT_PITCH_PX, 0)`; lint `slot_max × SLOT_PITCH_PX <= WEB_COORD_BUDGET_PX`; separation `SLOT_PITCH_PX² >= (2 × EXTERIOR_VISIBLE_RADIUS_PX + MAX_SECTION_EXTENT_PX)²`; `WEB_COORD_BUDGET_PX <= WORLD_COORD_LIMIT / 32`.

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
