| `world_slot` | u8 | 0..7, distinct per record | `mvp_world.json` (DA-26) | the record's initial pose slot |
| `SLOT_PITCH_PX` [PLACEHOLDER] | i32 base px | derived: from `2 × EXTERIOR_VISIBLE_RADIUS_PX + MAX_SECTION_EXTENT_PX` (6,336 at the defaults) up to `floor(WEB_COORD_BUDGET_PX / (MAX_WORLD_RECORDS - 1))` (18,724 at the defaults); default [PLACEHOLDER] 16,384 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | this GDD | slot spacing so no two bodies overlay in any view |
| `WEB_COORD_BUDGET_PX` [PLACEHOLDER] | i32 base px | derived: from `(MAX_WORLD_RECORDS - 1) × SLOT_PITCH_PX` (114,688 at the defaults) up to `WORLD_COORD_LIMIT / 32` (131,072 at the registry value, so 2^17 is the ceiling and not 2^19); default [PLACEHOLDER] 131,072 (2^17) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | this GDD | the exterior pose budget |
| `EXTERIOR_VISIBLE_RADIUS_PX` [PLACEHOLDER] | i32 base px | derived: from the half-diagonal of the largest ladder field (2,203 at 4K 1x) up to `(SLOT_PITCH_PX - MAX_SECTION_EXTENT_PX) / 2`; stated box 2203..4800, default [PLACEHOLDER] 2400 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | this GDD | D.7's off-screen radius |
| `MAX_SECTION_EXTENT_PX` | i32 base px | content | Layout (the largest section bounding box) | illustrative [PLACEHOLDER] 1,536 |
| `WORLD_COORD_LIMIT` [PLACEHOLDER] | i32 base px | 4,194,304 (2^22), range 2^20..2^22 | REG | Body's hard limit |

**Output Range:** the highest slot pose is `7 × 16,384 = 114,688 <= 131,072`; separation `(2 × 2,400 + 1,536)² = 6,336² <= 16,384²` (margin 2.6x). Because the departure apply re-origins by a pure translation (WM-6, lint `REORIGIN_IS_TRANSLATION`), poses never accumulate drift; every leg starts near the origin. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Slot separation holds at every departure, not only at `init_world`:** each departure re-poses every body outside the leg to `init_pose(slot) - init_pose(slot_departure)` (WM-6), so a dormant derelict is never left at an old approach pose (without it A overlaps home after the second departure) and `|coordinate| <= (MAX_WORLD_RECORDS - 1) × SLOT_PITCH_PX`. The three knob ranges above are inequalities, not independent boxes: (a) `SLOT_PITCH_PX >= 2R + E`, (b) `(MAX_WORLD_RECORDS - 1) × SLOT_PITCH_PX <= WEB_COORD_BUDGET_PX`, (c) `WEB_COORD_BUDGET_PX <= WORLD_COORD_LIMIT / 32`; AC-WM-125 sweeps the corners.

**Example:** The four `mvp_world.json` entries (three records plus the pod body) occupy slots 0 to 3 (poses `(0, 0)`, `(16384, 0)`, `(32768, 0)`, `(49152, 0)`); the largest coordinate, 49,152, is 37.5 % of the 131,072 budget.

### D.15 `pod_console_glow` (what the docked pod's status glow must satisfy; user ruling OQ-WM-39) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*

*(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling G1)* The `pod_console_glow` formula is now a pair of **inequality lints over Emitter State's own chain**. Emitter State is the **sole owner of the glow's base output, reach and falloff** (EM-11, D.1b `dock_status_glow`, boot check I-DOCK); this GDD owns no glow number. The three constants this section used to own (`GLOW_PEAK_PM`, `R_GLOW_PX`, `GLOW_LINT_MARGIN_PM`) and the registry value `pod_console_glow` as authored here are **withdrawn** and replaced by citations of `DOCK_STATUS_BASE_PM`, `DOCK_STATUS_REACH_PX` and `DOCK_STATUS_CONSOLE_MAX_PX`. This GDD owns (i) the **switch**: the glow is switched by Emitter's E10 `set_dock_status(section_id, on)` in the apply that writes `pod_at` (WM-8b), and (ii) the two inequalities below, which are lints over authored content, not game rules.

The chain is quoted from Emitter State D.1b: `R = DOCK_STATUS_REACH_PX`; `F(d, R) = floor(1000 x (R - d) / R)` for `d < R` and 0 for `d >= R`; `c(d) = floor(DOCK_STATUS_BASE_PM x F(d, R) / 1000)` (the glow's exposure in permille at distance `d` from the glow origin, a self-powered glow having `armed`, `live` and `served_pm` fixed at full). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*

**The lints.** **(G1, the console is lit)** `c(d_c) >= LIT_ON_PM + margin_E` for the authored distance `d_c` from the glow origin to the console anchor, where `d_c <= DOCK_STATUS_CONSOLE_MAX_PX` and the margin `margin_E` is Emitter's (I-DOCK C2); this is Emitter's own C2 restated as a content test, so it fails in one place. **(G2, the arrival cell stays dark)** `c(d_arr) < LIT_OFF_PM` for the authored distance `d_arr` from the glow origin to the centre of the target's `arrive_*` cell (sufficient: `d_arr >= DOCK_STATUS_REACH_PX`, where `c` is 0). **(c3)** the glow's luminance sits below the visible-unknown lamp's peak by the art-director's separation margin and it is not Tungsten (unchanged; the art-director owns the value). **(c4)** `switch_on(glow(d)) == (pod_at == record(d))` after every apply, and no glow is ON while `pod_at` is IN_FLIGHT (a runtime criterion, AC-WM-127b). The earlier structural guarantee 'R_GLOW_PX <= r_arr' is gone with the WM-owned radius: it held only because this GDD owned the radius, and it does not hold for Emitter's. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `LIT_ON_PM` | permille | 150 (range 100-300) | REG (Emitter State) | the exposure at which an object is lit |
| `LIT_OFF_PM` | permille | 120 (range 60-140, below `LIT_ON_PM`) | REG (Emitter State) | the release threshold (hysteresis) |
| `DOCK_STATUS_BASE_PM` | permille | 500 (300-800) | Emitter State (cited, REG) | the glow's steady output *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| `DOCK_STATUS_REACH_PX` | i32 base px | 48 (35-49 at the other defaults) | Emitter State (cited, REG) | the SOFT fade-out radius |
| `DOCK_STATUS_CONSOLE_MAX_PX` | i32 base px | 24 | Derelict Authoring's placement lint (cited from Emitter D.1b; registered) | the largest allowed glow-origin-to-console-anchor distance |
| `DOCK_STATUS_ARR_MIN_PX` | i32 base px | 37 at the defaults, derived by the lint, never authored | derived (this section) | least legal `d_arr` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* |
| `d_c` | i32 base px | content, 0 to `DOCK_STATUS_CONSOLE_MAX_PX` | Authoring (the prop's glow and console-anchor positions) | glow origin to console anchor |
| `d_arr` | i32 base px | content | Authoring (the glow origin and the `arrive_*` cell, DA table) | glow origin to the centre of the arrival cell; **replaces** the earlier `r_arr` (console anchor to arrival cell), which is a different quantity whenever the glow origin is not on the anchor |
| `r_arr` | i32 base px | content; 24 at the nearest authored layout (WM-15) | Authoring | console anchor to arrival-cell centre (kept as the content fact the placement is built from) |
| `REACH_PX` | i32 base px | 72 | REG (Player Controller) | the player's interaction reach |

**Derivation (what the lints give at Emitter's defaults).** G1: `c(24) = floor(500 x 500 / 1000) = 250 >= 150` (margin 100 over `LIT_ON_PM`), and `c(13) = floor(500 x 729 / 1000) = 364`. G2: `c(d) < 120` needs `F(d) < 240`, and `F(36) = 250` gives `c` 125 (not below 120) while `F(37) = floor(11000 / 48) = 229` gives `c` 114, so the least legal `d_arr` is **37 px** (`DOCK_STATUS_ARR_MIN_PX`, derived from Emitter's values by the lint, never authored; it moves with `DOCK_STATUS_BASE_PM`, `DOCK_STATUS_REACH_PX` and `LIT_OFF_PM`). **If the glow origin sat on the console anchor** (`d_c` 0) and the arrival cell were the nearest authored 24 px away (`r_arr`), `d_arr` would be 24 and `c(24)` would be 250, **above `LIT_ON_PM`: the arrival cell would be LIT and 'a dark derelict stays dark' would be false.** This was the finding of the re-review; it is a content-placement and tuning conflict among three sibling GDDs, not something this GDD can retune (OQ-WM-48). **A placement that satisfies both lints on paper:** put the glow origin on the far side of the prop from the arrival cell, on the line from the arrival cell through the console anchor, `delta` px beyond the anchor: `d_c = delta`, `d_arr = r_arr + delta = 24 + delta`; `delta` in [13, 24] satisfies G1 (`c(13)` 364, `c(24)` 250) and G2 (`d_arr` 37 to 48, `c` 114 down to 0). Whether the prop's footprint allows an origin 13 to 24 px beyond the anchor is Derelict Authoring's call (DA-6, kit gap K-3); the other two routes are Emitter State retuning `DOCK_STATUS_BASE_PM` / `DOCK_STATUS_REACH_PX` / `DOCK_STATUS_CONSOLE_MAX_PX` jointly under I-DOCK, or Authoring moving the arrival cell to at least 37 px from the origin. **Resolved 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G1 (owner wins; applied under G6): route (i) is the rule Authoring applies.** Worked numbers at Emitter's defaults (reach R = 48, base 500, `LIT_ON_PM` 150, `LIT_OFF_PM` 120): `c(d) = floor(500 x floor(1000 x (48 - d) / 48) / 1000)`. With the glow origin `delta` px beyond the console anchor on the line from the arrival-cell centre (`r_arr` = 24 nominal): `d_c = delta`, `d_arr = 24 + delta`. `delta` = 13: `F` = floor(1000 x 35 / 48) = 729, `c(13)` = 364; `d_arr` 37: `F` = floor(11000 / 48) = 229, `c` = 114 < 120. `delta` = 24: `c(24)` = 250, `d_arr` 48: `F` = 0, `c` = 0. Recommended nominal `delta` = 20 (`d_c` 20: `F` 583, `c` 291; `d_arr` 44: `F` 83, `c` 41), which leaves 141 permille of margin on G1 over `LIT_ON_PM` and 79 on G2 under `LIT_OFF_PM`. General feasibility: with `d_c` <= 24 the arrival cell is at least `r_arr` - 24 from the glow, so G2 holds for ANY placement on the prop whenever `r_arr` >= 61 (61 - 24 = 37); for `r_arr` < 61 the far-side rule applies. Each CONSOLE anchor in the room (the POD anchor in every dock room and the DOCK anchor at the home airlock, WM-8) must satisfy G1; G2 is measured to the arrival-cell centre. The lint G2 is a BLOCKING Config/Data criterion (AC-WM-127a) run by Authoring as `DOCK_GLOW_ARRIVAL_DARK`. *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*

**Output Range:** `c` 0 to `DOCK_STATUS_BASE_PM`; `d_arr` at least `DOCK_STATUS_ARR_MIN_PX` (37 at the defaults). The glow never lights the arrival cell, so entering a dark derelict dock stays a dark arrival; it lights the console only. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*

**Example:** HOME at a power-down with the cell spent: the home dock's glow was switched ON at `init_world` (E10) and stays ON while the pod is parked there, `c(d_c)` >= 150, the console is lit, `pod_hop`-to-A is accepted (given pool and floor). At derelict A's dark dock room: the glow was switched ON by the `pod_settle` (E10), the arrival cell is at `d_arr` >= 37 from the glow origin so `c(d_arr)` <= 114 < 120, the player arrives in the dark with the console lit; pressing `pod_hop` home is accepted with zero glowsticks and a spent cell (AC-WM-128). On that departure E10 switches A's glow OFF and with the pod IN_FLIGHT no dock glow is ON; at the home `pod_settle` the home glow is ON again. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*

## Edge Cases

*Format.* Each entry is "If [condition]: [exact outcome]. [rationale]", id `E-nn`, grouped by theme. Reason names are the closed sets of WM-9; every restore reason is a named `WEB_RESTORE_*` and **never** a clamp (SL-15). Entries that depend on an undesigned or in-flight neighbour are marked *(provisional)*.

### A. Fuel, the floor and the payer (zero, max and boundary)

- **If the pool is exactly `HOP_FUEL_ML` [PLACEHOLDER] 400 + `HOP_FUEL_FLOOR_ML` [PLACEHOLDER] 1500 = 1,900 mL when `pod_hop` is pressed at HOME (E-01)**: the hop is accepted (`>=`), 400 mL is withdrawn and the pool is 1,500. At 1,899 mL the command is `FUEL_LOW`: nothing changes, no event, no fuel moves. The floor test is a `>=` on integers so the boundary is exact and testable (D.4).
- **If the pool is 0 (empty tank, SCRAMmed reactor) and the crew is at HOME (E-02)**: `pod_hop` and `berth` are `FUEL_LOW`; `unberth` is still accepted. The crew is stranded at home, not away: failure costs the ship, never knowledge (P5). Nothing in this system ever refuses a return for want of fuel.
- **If the reactor SCRAMs or the pool falls below the floor while the crew is on a derelict (E-03)**: the return hop is accepted at any pool, including 0, because a hop from a derelict costs `RETURN_HOP_FUEL_ML` [PLACEHOLDER] 0 and the floor test is not applied to it. The chest lamp, not the dark home's power, lights the pod console while the cell lasts (hands-free lamp, user decision). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Light does not strand the player either** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: IA-6 still refuses the `pod_hop` lit exemption, but the docked pod's self-powered status glow lights the console at a dock with a spent cell, no stick and a dark room (E-16, WM-8b), so neither fuel nor light can strand the player on the way home (user ruling, OQ-WM-39). The earlier withdrawal of the claim that 'the escape verb is never lost to the dark' is superseded by that glow. The pod returns the player to a dark home: the cost of the fuel decision was already paid (P5: fuel never strands the player; the floor protects the reactor for an 8-minute sortie with the drip repaired, D.5).
- **If the pool falls below the floor during a leg (the reactor kept burning) (E-04)**: the in-flight hop is unaffected (it was prepaid and the return is free); the **next** departure from HOME is refused `FUEL_LOW`. The crew learns the price on the next press, not mid-crossing.
- **If `granted` from `withdraw_store` is below the validated cost in the apply (E-05)**: this cannot happen on a validated command (one command per apply, no interleaving, validate and apply in the same tick); if a defect ever causes it, `transit_fuel_short` is logged and the hop **still proceeds**. A hop that silently stranded the player would be a lie by omission about the price (P1).
- **If a welded B's `ft_b` (section `kind` DERELICT) is connected to the home FUEL loop and the reactor burns it (E-06)**: the reactor can consume `ft_b` while hops can never draw it (the payer is HOME-kind tanks only, OR-6), so a `FUEL_LOW` refusal can coexist with fuel the reactor is still using. This is the recorded OR-6 asymmetry. Outcome: the refusal is truthful about the rule (the pool the hop may draw is below the floor). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The economy-designer finding stands: after the weld the player can see fuel they can never use to leave, and welding a fueled derelict gains them nothing. The lean, recorded as **OR-7 (proposed, not adopted)**, is payer = every FUEL_TANK connected to the home fuel loop. It needs a Graph read of fuel-loop connectivity that the Graph does not publish (and, per the re-review, a Graph publication of which tank it draws first, OQ-WM-50) (LS-2 names the five loops but no connectivity read exists), so OR-6 stands and the lean waits on that read (OQ-WM-23). Adopting it also reopens the single-tank reserve marks of WM-20 (a tank below the pod mark could then still be allowed to leave). The conservative reading keeps the floor a real reserve.
- **If a FUEL pipe or tank is leaking and the pool drops between a refusal and the next press (E-07)**: the next validate reads the current pool; the refusal reason is the only Web Map & Transit witness, and the leak's drip and puddle (`PUDDLE` descriptor id 25) are the Graph's. This system never raises a "low fuel" prompt of its own (guard rail 1).

### B. The leg and arrival (timing and simultaneous rules)

- **If two `pod_arrive` commands for one `leg_epoch` reach P2 (E-08)**: the first applies; the second finds `transit_state != LEG` and is rejected `NOT_IN_LEG`, counted in `transit_stale_cmds`, never `FAULTED`. The hook resubmits every run until one applies (idempotent), so duplicates are expected whenever the queue delays a command.
- **If a `pod_arrive` or `pod_settle` carries an epoch older than the current `leg_epoch` (E-09)**: `STALE_EPOCH`, no state change. A pre-restore leftover cannot exist (the queue is dropped on restore); only a hand-injected command can, and it must not move the machine.
- **If a PLAYER submits `pod_arrive`, `pod_settle`, `berth_step` or `web_reconcile` (E-10)**: `ORIGIN_NOT_ALLOWED` at intake (`allowed_origins {SYSTEM}`). No player can skip or fake a boundary.
- **If the kernel is `PAUSED` (solo pause, CR-16) on the tick the hook submits `pod_arrive` (E-11)**: the tick does not advance and the command waits; on resume it dispatches at the next tick as normal, with no restore and no slip. `TRANSIT_HOLD` works whether or not the simulation is paused (PIPE:296), and the leg does not advance while paused.
- **If `pod_hop` is pressed twice on the same tick, or a second command arrives behind it (E-12)**: the first applies; the second validates against a machine that is no longer `IDLE` and is `IN_TRANSIT`. Exactly one fuel debit and one `leg_epoch` increment. The ordering key (CR-13) decides which of two competing commands is "first"; the loser never partially applies.
- **If `pod_hop` or `berth` and an ASSEMBLY weld channel open on the same tick (E-13)**: whichever applies first wins and the other is rejected with its own reason (`BUSY` for the hop and berth, the channel's own reason for the channel); never both. A hop pressed while a weld *offer* exists but no channel is open is allowed, and the offer simply vanishes.
- **If the traveller is CHANNELLING at the press (E-14)**: Interaction's `ABORT(BODY)` aborts the channel in the same apply and `carry_ref` travels with the pod; the command is not refused for the channel (only an open ASSEMBLY weld channel on the home body is `BUSY`).
- **If the traveller is STAGGERED, DOWNED or DEAD, or already `IN_POD`, at the press (E-15)**: `BUSY` (the Controller's state, not a rule of this system). A downed player cannot hop; this is not a safe-room toggle.
- **If the dock room is unpowered and the player's chest lamp is off or dead, with no glowstick (E-16)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: the console is lit by the docked pod's self-powered status glow (WM-8b, user ruling OQ-WM-39, Option B), so `pod_hop` at the POD console and `berth` and `unberth` at the DOCK console are not refused for light and are accepted subject to their other reasons (the console subject for the verb pressed is lit). *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* `NOT_LIT` stays in the reason set as a backstop and is **unreachable by construction at a pod console** (it is exercised only through a test double that disables the glow, AC-WM-10). The strand cell (spent cell, zero glowsticks in the pack and none thrown, unpowered dock) is AC-WM-128 (BLOCKING). The earlier lint `DOCK_CONSOLE_DARK_NO_STICK` stays withdrawn (a loose stick is unlit and `pickup` is lit-only, BS-19's stage-12 finding) and is replaced by the glow-placement lint AC-WM-127a. **Boundaries:** with the pod away the prop is absent, its glow is OFF (E10) and no console is eligible (`NOT_DOCKED_HERE` from the other dock, WM-33); the glow reaches only the console anchor (D.15), so a dark derelict stays dark and the player still needs the chest lamp or a stick to see the room; a press out of reach is `OUT_OF_RANGE`. **Preconditions (landed in Emitter State, re-review):** EM-3's closed emitter-kind list and EM-11's self-powered rule now admit the new nodeless class (DOCK_STATUS, switched by E10); **a placement inference Emitter State confirms:** EM-13 and EM:660 make a dormant section's lamp not live and the pod section is dormant always (WM-5), so the glow sits on the dock room's docked-podcraft prop (live with that room), not on the pod body (those rules concern `node_ref` nodes and a `node_ref` 0 fixture has none). **Landed 2026-10-28:** Derelict Authoring DA-46 carries the placement rule and both lints (G1, G2; OQ-WM-48 resolved). **Still owed:** the art-director sets the luminance separation from the visible-unknown lamp.
- **If the authored arrival cell is blocked at the press (E-17)**: `ARRIVAL_BLOCKED`, a refusal with no state change and no fuel (validated with Body's `can_transfer` at the departure press). The cell is **never searched** for a free neighbour (BDY:703-706).
- **If the arrival-cell re-check fails inside `pod_settle` (E-18)**: a defect (the target was frozen), handled deterministically: `pod_settle` is total and takes the recovery branch, starting **one reversal** to the departure body (`leg_epoch += 1`, `leg_dir` reversed, `target_id` and `origin_id` swapped, `leg_reversed` set, `t0` the nominal settle tick, no M19 call, no BM5, no E10 call, no fuel, `ARRIVAL_RECHECK_FAILED` logged; the full list is WM-15) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; a second failure places the avatar at the authored cell with no re-check (`ARRIVAL_FORCED`), legal because two crewmates may share a cell (BDY-17). The machine can never be stuck in `ARRIVING`.
- **If the `PROJECTOR` finishes late (a slow frame or a hitch under black) (E-19)**: `ARRIVING` is a fixed simulation length, so the machine has already settled and the avatar stands on the destination under the Pipeline's black plate until its own latch holds; `ARRIVE_SCENE_LATE` is logged (a projector-side DIAGNOSTIC). This is acceptable because the target was frozen until `pod_settle` and `ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] 45 is 7.5 % of the 600-tick `W_MIN_WARNING_S` window (D.13).
- **If the queue delays a boundary command by up to 8 ticks (a congested queue) (E-20)**: the expected slip is `v × (apply_T - (t0 + n))`, the pose is forced to `p1` by BM7, and nothing is logged above DEBUG; `LEG_POSE_MISMATCH` is raised only when the observed offset differs from that value (D.2).
- **If a leg's resolved `p1 != p0 + v × n` is observed at a boundary (E-21)**: `LEG_POSE_MISMATCH` (a defect), the pose is forced to `p1` by BM7 and the machine continues. If BM7 is refused none of the exact-leg and berth rules hold (WM-6 fallback).
- **If the console is pressed while carrying (E-74)**: `pod_hop` is accepted and `carry_ref` travels unchanged (PCI:694, IA-40; ASK-I1 accepted, IA-28 carry-admitted) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
- **If a BACK leg returns the crew to a depressurised or breached home dock room (E-79)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the hop is accepted and the arrival applies; arrival safety is geometry-only (WM-15), so the avatar arrives in the hazard room with the Graph's state unchanged by the hop, the Graph's and Attention's own tells are the warning, and `ARRIVAL_BLOCKED` is never raised for a hazard. AC-WM-120.

### C. Save and restore (per state; never clamp)

- **If a snapshot is taken on tick `t0` itself (the tick `pod_hop` applied) (E-22)**: the restore returns the world after the full atomic departure apply (fuel debited, origin dormant, avatar in the pod, `LEG`); the leg continues from `t0`.
- **If a snapshot is taken at `t0 + n - 1` and restored (E-23)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the pending `pod_arrive` is dropped (`Dropped(RELOAD)`), the hook resubmits on the first run, the pod slips one velocity step, and the pose-carrying apply sets the **nominal trajectory** (`p1`); `EXPECTED_RESTORE_SLIP` is logged at DEBUG. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **This is Case A and it produces no accruals**: the settle is keyed to the nominal `arrive_T` and applies at 10945 in both runs (worked example in States and Transitions and D.2). **Case B** (a snapshot after tick `arrive_T + ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS - 1`, i.e. 10944, drops the pending `pod_settle`): the hook resubmits at 10945 and the settle applies at 10946, lag 1; only Case B has the bounded accruals below. **Restore equivalence (replaces the QA correction of 2026-10-26):** `arrive_T`, the settle tick, `first_boarded_T` and `t_phase` are keyed to **nominal ticks** (`t0 + n`, `arrive_T + ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS`, `t_phase + n_i`), never to the apply tick, so a late apply (restore lag 1 on an empty queue; at most `a - 1` = 7 extra ticks per submit under queue delay, and `SLIP_MAX_TICKS` 15 with one restore) changes only the wall tick of the work, not any persisted Web Map & Transit or Body value. The restored run therefore equals the uninterrupted run **exactly at the first tick `T*` where both are IDLE for `web.state`, `body.state` and `layout.state`** (with no player intent between the nominal boundary and `T*`), and **for every other block at `T* + 12`** (the largest hook cadence, Sanity's divisor 12), except three named bounded accruals: the target section's divisor-6 loaded clocks in `graph.state` (at most `ceil(lag / 6)` Graph steps: for lag 1 the lamp battery stock higher by at most `LAMP_DJ_PER_STEP` 20 dJ), Suit's thermal load and hold counters (at most `ceil(lag / 6)` runs, because in the restored run the avatar is still `IN_POD` and the counters accrue at the `IN_POD` band instead of the target section's band) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*, and Sanity's level (at most `ceil(lag / 12)` accruals of the `aboard` delta). `first_boarded_T` is not affected. The three exclusions close only if the owners adopt as-of accounting (ASK-G1b for the Graph; the Suit and Sanity equivalents are routed with it in OQ-WM-43); otherwise they are the permanent named exclusion. VS-B consequence: replicas that restore at different offsets can differ by those accruals, so VS-B must not ship on the fallback. See AC-WM-15, AC-WM-54 and AC-WM-76. (D.2)
- **If a restore lands in `LEG` with the avatar's `body_of_actor` not the pod body (E-24)**: `WEB_RESTORE_STATE`; the whole restore is rejected (the save is corrupt) and nothing is repaired.
- **If a restore lands in `ARRIVING` (`T` between `arrive_T` and `arrive_T + ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS - 1`) (E-25)**: the hook resumes from the persisted `arrive_T`, the pod is parked at `p1`, the target is still dormant, the `PROJECTOR` rebuilds the scene from data and `pod_settle` lands on schedule. On a restore into ARRIVING the Pipeline must re-enter `TRANSIT_HOLD` after `LOADING -> RUNNING` while `web.view.transit_state == ARRIVING` (black until `destination_ready`). On a restore into LEG it presents the exterior leg (VA §2, Restore mid-LEG) (CF-PIPE).
- **If content `n`, a velocity or a berth phase is edited by a tuning pass between a save and its restore (E-26)**: the persisted resolved parameters win for the running leg or berth, so the path and its exact sum are unchanged; content is consulted at the next departure. `WEB_RESTORE_LEG_INEXACT` or `WEB_RESTORE_BERTH_INEXACT` is raised only when the persisted values themselves fail validation (`p1 != p0 + v × n`, `r_start - sum(v_i × n_i) != frame_shift_px`, or a per-axis `|v|` above `VEL_MAX_PX_PER_TICK` [PLACEHOLDER]), i.e. a corrupt or hand-edited save, and the restore is rejected.
- **If a restore lands in `BERTHING` between phase boundaries (E-27)**: the current phase is derived from `t_phase` and the persisted `n_i`; a lost boundary command costs at most the expected slip and is re-submitted; the final step's pose-carrying BM7 re-asserts `docked` by equality.
- **If a restore lands in `IDLE` with a berthed derelict (E-28)**: the hold is restored (`berth_target_id` persisted, B live because the Graph's `dormant` flag persisted as false); no BM6 or BM7 is issued by this system while berthed.
- **If the Graph says at `post_restore` that the departed derelict is not dormant while `transit_state == LEG` (E-29)**: the restore is **rejected** with `WEB_RESTORE_LOADED_SET` (named, never clamped, never repaired) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. The expected live set is a pure function of the persisted machine (LEG and ARRIVING: no unwelded derelict live; BERTHING and berthed IDLE: exactly the berth target; IDLE: exactly the derelict at `pod_at`, if any, plus the berth target; ORPHAN records dormant; an ABSORBED record is part of the home body). Any other live set is a corrupt save or a writer defect. This system does not re-assert the Graph (`loaded` stays derived, OR-2: two writers of one fact is the recorded failure class), and WM-30's 'reject, never clamp' now holds without exception.
- **If a record id, `body_id` or `section_id` in the block is unknown to Layout at `post_restore` (E-30)**: `WEB_RESTORE_UNKNOWN_RECORD`; rejected, never repaired. A block version outside `supported_versions` is `WEB_RESTORE_VERSION`; a stale or reused `leg_epoch` is `WEB_RESTORE_EPOCH`; a pod body that is not dormant-parked is `WEB_RESTORE_POD_BODY`.
- **If a `RISKY` autosave fires mid-leg because `IN_POD` is a soft gate state and a chained soft window pushed the wait to `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` 7200 (E-31)**: the save is written tagged `RISKY` and is restorable by the rules above. A single leg plus settle (at most 2,400 ticks, D.2) cannot trip the cap by itself.
- **If the player dies on a derelict or at home after a hop (E-32)**: "restart from the last autosave" (SL-7); the world block comes back with the autosave (WORLD scope), so `pod_at`, `transit_state`, record statuses and the fuel the hop spent roll back together. MVP ships zero PROFILE blocks, so nothing about the web is kept across a death; Pillar 5's "never knowledge" is the Vertical Slice Codex's PROFILE data.
- **If death is reached while `IN_POD` (E-33)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: reachable, by user ruling (OQ-WM-41). The statuses of the WM-17b table run in the pod (no `draw_air`, but the reserve, cell, thermal load, hold counters, Sanity, Attention clocks and the Controller's own STAGGERED, DOWNED and DEAD rules all run or apply, delegated ruling R6), so a seat that boarded the pod hurt can go down in it. DEAD in the pod is the Controller's DEAD and resolves as every death: "restart from the last autosave" (SL-7, WM-32), with the world block rolling back. STAGGERED or DOWNED in the pod does not stop the leg or the settle (both are total, WM-15): the avatar arrives in that state and cannot hop home (`BUSY`, E-15). A defect that set a lethal state through an air draw on the dormant pod section is still a violation of the `breath_starved` criterion (AC-WM-23); a death by reserve exhaustion in the pod is Suit's FAILED handed to the Controller (Suit AC-SE-94), and at the defaults it needs a boarding reserve far below the CRITICAL rung's threshold.

### D. The berth, `unberth`, the weld and the cut

- **If `berth` is pressed with a non-FREE home socket or no mating socket (E-34)**: `SOCKET_NOT_FREE` or `NO_MATE_SOCKET` from Layout's pure `can_attach` preview; B is not loaded (validate before apply keeps S2 unreachable without a legal berth).
- **If `berth` is pressed for a derelict whose `status` is not BOARDED, or while another derelict is berthed (E-35)**: `NOT_BOARDED` or `ALREADY_BERTHED`. The crew must have stood aboard before the hull can come alongside.
- **If B is berthed and the weld is never done (no welder, or the player changes their mind) (E-36)**: every hop is `TARGET_BERTHED` (to B) or `BERTH_PENDING` (to anyone else) until `unberth`, which is accepted at any pool, costs `UNBERTH_FUEL_ML` [PLACEHOLDER] 0, returns B to dormancy at the end and leaves B BOARDED. Without `unberth` this would be a permanent soft lock that is a rule, not a consequence the player earned.
- **If `unberth` is pressed while an ASSEMBLY channel is open or the socket is WELDED (E-37)**: `BUSY` or `SOCKET_NOT_FREE`; B stays loaded and docked. `unberth` has **no** `FUEL_LOW` path: a floor on it would bring the soft lock back.
- **If the player cycles `berth` and `unberth` (E-38)**: each cycle costs `BERTH_FUEL_ML` [PLACEHOLDER] 800 and gains nothing (repositioning is not a resource), and every cycle loads B live with nobody aboard, so B's compression and lamp clock tick while the crew idles. Self-limiting and honest; not an exploit.
- **If B is berthed (S2) and nobody is aboard it (E-39)**: B runs live and unobserved: `DERELICT_FAULT_COMPRESSION` (500 permille) wear and the residual-lamp clock tick for the whole berth, and the Graph's fairness gate covers only endogenous warnings the player could perceive. This is R9's accepted cost, stated so a player who berths and then waits at the weld bench loses B's lamp first and B's integrity second.
- **If the weld completes while the berth state is BERTHED (E-40)**: the next `transit.step` run sees Layout's body of B's section differ from the stored `body_id` and submits `web_reconcile`; B becomes `ABSORBED`, the berth fields clear and the dock room stays the home `airlock`. S2 becomes S3.
- **If a cut leaves a detached piece (E-41)**: its record becomes `ORPHAN`, the piece stays a dormant body (`DETACH_DRIFT_PX_PER_TICK` 0, nothing discarded, `M4`, `LM4` and `E2` not called), it counts toward `MAX_BODIES` 8 and is never a destination (`TARGET_ABSORBED`, a diagnostic path the Chart Table never offers). Stranded-crew recovery is out of MVP scope: the solo seat is on the keep side by construction (MSA-18); MSA E-22 and ASK T2 are answered "Vertical Slice A / B" (no recovery path at MVP, so `CUT_STRANDS` applies at VS-B per AC-MSA-124); MSA E-17 is answered by the WM-22 hold (ASK T1).
- **If B is welded and keeps section `kind` DERELICT (E-42)**: by LS-17 the kind never changes, so as written compression and the lamp clock continue on a welded B unless the Graph adds a rule; no Web Map & Transit rule depends on the answer (OQ-WM-5 is forwarded to the Graph and Assembly with the recommendation that both stop once welded).
- **If something other than this system moves a berthed body (an impact, a horror, a future thruster) and `docked` breaks (E-43)**: Body gates nothing on `docked`; this system issues no BM6 or BM7 while berthed and does **not** automatically re-establish it. The weld offer disappears; the player can `unberth` and `berth` again at the cost of `BERTH_FUEL_ML` [PLACEHOLDER].
- **If a hop to A is pressed after the weld (S3) (E-44)**: allowed; A goes live at `pod_settle` (S4, 449 world tiles, 145 subjects as an INFO upper bound; the registered neighbourhoods stay at or under `INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET` [PLACEHOLDER] 120, DA-47 / D37), and the home dock remains the `airlock` room. This system has no lever on subject counts. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)*
- **If a cut would leave the airlock/dock room on a detached piece (E-75)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: forbidden upstream (ASK-MSA-1, `CUT_ORPHANS_DOCK`). If it ever reaches `web_reconcile`, HOME follows the body of the HOME section (WM-23), the HOME-kind and avatar-present guards (WM-24) refuse to set that section dormant, `WEB_RECONCILE_GUARD` is logged, only a piece without the airlock may become ORPHAN, and no record is discarded, so this system's own writes never leave `pod_at` HOME pointing at a dormant piece. AC-WM-133.
- **If a cut would leave every payer FUEL_TANK on a detached piece (E-76)**: forbidden upstream (ASK-MSA-1, `CUT_ORPHANS_PAYER_TANKS`). If it reaches this system the pool reads 0, `pod_hop` and `berth` are `FUEL_LOW`, and `unberth` and every return hop stay accepted (nobody is stranded away from home); the crew at home is left with a ship that cannot send the pod, which is P5's failure cost (ship, not knowledge) but an avoidable soft lock the cut offer must prevent. AC-WM-134. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

### E. Destinations, topology and the loaded set

- **If the destination is not legal from here (E-45)**: `pod_hop` from a derelict to anything other than HOME is `TARGET_UNKNOWN`; to the current location `TARGET_HERE`; to the berthed derelict `TARGET_BERTHED`; to another derelict while one is berthed `BERTH_PENDING`; to an ABSORBED or ORPHAN record `TARGET_ABSORBED`. There is no derelict-to-derelict edge, no free coordinate and no steering.
- **If a console is pressed whose dock is not where the pod is (E-46)**: the console subject for the verb pressed returns `NOT_DOCKED_HERE`; the docked-podcraft prop is not drawn there either (WM-33), so no prompt normally exists. The pod cannot be summoned.
- **If UNSEEN derelicts are listed from minute 0 (E-47)**: `pod_hop` to an UNSEEN record is legal (OQ-WM-4, ruled); `web.view` lists it as a bare node and reveals nothing beyond `status` and `unknown_seen`. The map shows what the crew lit and never reveals.
- **If a derelict is re-entered after any length of time at home (E-48)**: it resumes **frozen at its last state with no fast-forward** (LS-17, HE-21): damaged parts are still damaged, taken salvage is still gone, a lamp that went dark while loaded is still dark, the tell is not part of the frozen state: Attention's clock ran during the absence and a tell left on a vacated non-home locus was voided at its due run (AT-22). Nothing regrows and nothing heals. *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*
- **If a departed derelict's section is found not dormant (E-49)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: this is now a defect state (ASK-G1 is accepted and M19 exists, so only a guard failure or a bug leaves it live): the derelict stays live until the next apply, its hazards run unobserved (a P2 fairness cost), its lamp and compression clocks tick unseen and S0 to S4 cannot be enforced. A restore that finds it is rejected `WEB_RESTORE_LOADED_SET` (E-29).
- **If the loaded set during a leg is examined (E-50)**: it is {home} only: both derelicts dormant, the pod section always dormant, so no derelict hazard can run while the crew is in the dark and `breath_starved` has nothing to count.
- **If the bodies-live cap is read (E-51)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: Lighting defines *live* as built **and** (a non-dormant section or hosting the avatar) (LO-24, LO:277), so live bodies number at most 2 in every reachable MVP state against `BODIES_LIVE_MAX` [PLACEHOLDER] 4: the margin is 2. The earlier zero-margin reading (built bodies = 4) is withdrawn and ASK-LT1 is answered. This system adds no body (lint `BODY_COUNT_MVP`).
- **If the player tops the suit up from a derelict's residual battery (`b_a1` or `b_b1`) (E-77)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the lamp's loaded-time budget shrinks by `amount_dJ / LAMP_DJ_PER_STEP × 6` ticks per top-up (100,000 dJ costs 5,000 steps = 8.3 minutes of A's 45), the rendered lamp state is always the live chain predicate (WM-18), and a lamp found dark on return after a top-up is the player's own action and not time away. The DIAGNOSTIC `lamp_topup_dJ` (per derelict) lets playtest logs attribute it (D.10, AC-WM-126).

### F. Attention, Horror and the escape verb

- **If the console is pressed during a TELL or a BREACH (E-52)**: the hop is always allowed (it is the escape verb); no Attention reason can refuse it; departure does not abort a TELL (AT-22); BREACH ends when the last recorded occupant has left the non-home locus body. A departure that empties a non-home locus is resolved by Attention's void first (AT-22): the due run is the void (ABORTED, no STRESS) and the dormancy fallback is suppressed (WM-26); the locus falls back to the home ship only for a cut, a berth release or a dormancy while a living seat is still in the body (AT E-27); a home-ship-locus tell is not voidable and breaches on the home ship at the due run (AT E-73). *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*
- **If a horror entity is adjacent, or attacking, in the dock room at the press (E-53)**: the hop still applies unless the traveller is STAGGERED, DOWNED or DEAD (`BUSY`); the entity stays on the now-dormant body, frozen, and is still there on return (no fast-forward). The pod is an escape, not a reset: returning meets the same entity in the same place. Covered by AC-WM-130 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. A record in the `TRANSIT` behaviour state toward a body that unloads waits (HE-21; ASK-H1 answered yes in HE-21).
- **If a stimulus-bearing event happens on the departure body during the hop apply (E-54)**: this system pushes no stimulus and the pod's running light is cosmetic (not an eligible emitter, ASK-A1), so a hop is silent to Attention. The `HOP_LOUD_ON_DEPARTURE` option stays default-off and belongs to Attention. A `berth` is a different act: it is the home-ship's own drive burning fuel, and exactly one declared LOUD noise report is pushed at the `berth` apply, by Interaction as channel owner (IA-33, IA-40, WM-26, OQ-WM-40, user ruling D59; Attention OQ-AT-23's route 1), not by this system *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling G1)*; nothing in this system suppresses or defers it, and if the user later rules the berth silent the tripwire AC-WM-81g applies.
- **If a home fault is due while no seat is aboard the home body (E-78)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: no deferral exists (WM-26b, R21): the Graph arms, warns and fires a home endogenous failure exactly as if the seat were at home, including inside LEG and ARRIVING, where a leg of 945 ticks exceeds the 600-tick window (D.13) so it can fire unwitnessed; this is the accepted price of leaving. The lint `LEG_EXCEEDS_WINDOW` reports the state and the telemetry tripwire counts home faults fired with the seat in the pod. AC-WM-122 tests that no deferral occurs. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R21)*

### G. Hallucination interplay (no corruptible channel here)

- **If the traveller is at low sanity and the console refuses (`FUEL_LOW`, `ARRIVAL_BLOCKED`, ...) (E-55)**: the refusal is a rule, not a reading, and its reason is a truth-only surface (ASK-F1, adopted: PF-5 row 13). A refusal the Filter could fake would trap a player inside a breach by a lethal lie with no falsification check (P4). The `FUEL_LOW` floor is also not a hidden number: WM-20 routes two physical reserve marks painted on the home FUEL_TANK at the refusal thresholds (ASK-G3 accepted as prop art, ASK-AD-9 open, truth-only) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; if refused, `FUEL_LOW` is the refusal-only witness of the threshold and AC-WM-81h is the playtest tripwire.
- **If the residual lamp is found dark on return and the player doubts it (low sanity) (E-56)**: the lamp point is never wrapped; the doubt is the player's to resolve by boarding and finding the battery spent. The chain predicate means a salvaged or destroyed lamp with a charged battery also reads dark, never a phantom light.
- **If a phantom entity or a false "pod missing" state appears in the dock room (E-57)**: the Filter may decorate the derelict's space after arrival, but the arrival cell is validated against real Body state and the docked-podcraft prop renders iff `pod_at` equals the dock (WM-33); neither can be faked, so the escape verb is always truthful.
- **If phantom salvage (hallucination class d) is carried through a hop (E-58)**: `carry_ref` travels unchanged through the pod; this system never counts loot, so a phantom item neither blocks nor enables a hop. Boarding & Salvage owns the count (designed, `boarding-and-salvage.md`).
- **If a crewmate appears as a horror (class c, VS-B) and the player flees by pod (E-59)**: the hop is not refused; leaving the crewmate on the derelict is a VS-B co-op question flagged, not designed here *(provisional — Co-op Session undesigned)*. The crewmate glyph on the chart is a human channel and is never faked.

### H. Degenerate strategies and balance

- **If the player hops to a derelict and straight back to reset a tell or heal it (E-60)**: the departure freezes the derelict and re-entry costs `HOP_FUEL_ML` [PLACEHOLDER] 400 from HOME; nothing in the Graph resets or heals; the tell it had reached its due run during the round trip (2 x 945 = 1890 ticks > `TELL_MAX_TICKS` 1800) and was voided by Attention (AT-22, user ruling OQ-AT-12), so the player meets a CALM derelict with the rearm timer set, not a restarted tell. The return from a derelict is free but the next outbound is not. Covered by AC-WM-129 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*.
- **If the player idles on a derelict to farm it (E-61)**: idling burns loaded minutes, so compression wears the derelict toward `FAILED` at x2 (D.10) and the lamp goes dark; the outer ring is kept unsafe by depletion and wear, so there is no safe place to farm forever (P5).
- **If the player minimises hops to save fuel (E-62)**: each sortie yields the module and loot at the derelict; the real constraint is time away: the costs are the home's own fault arming for the whole time away (the pod window included, WM-26b), the reactor's burn, the derelict lamp clock and wear (D.10), not the 400 mL debit *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R21)*; there is no "fewest hops" dominant strategy and no "free hop" (a departure from HOME always costs). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **If the player hoards fuel by never sortieing (E-63)**: the reactor burns 180 mL/min at 2500 W regardless of what the crew does (the Graph's clocks run whether or not the player acts), so the tank empties in about 111 minutes (faster while the authored FUEL-DRIP is unrepaired); waiting is the most expensive plan.
- **If playtest shows the hop is read as "a loading screen" or "free" (E-64)**: the tuning is wrong, not the fantasy: the levers are `HOP_LEG_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER], `HOP_FUEL_ML` [PLACEHOLDER] and `BERTH_FUEL_ML` [PLACEHOLDER]. A skip, a free hop or an instant return is not a lever (guard rail 8).

### I. Content, lint and boot

- **If a record's `world_slot` collides with another, or two `init_world` poses are within `SLOT_PITCH_PX` [PLACEHOLDER] of each other (E-65)**: content-load lint `INIT_WORLD_ORDER` / the slot lint fails the build; it is never handled at runtime.
- **If a leg or berth coordinate would exceed `WEB_COORD_BUDGET_PX` [PLACEHOLDER], or `HOP_LEG_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] + `ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] + `IN_POD_SLACK_TICKS` exceeds 2,400, i.e. `HOP_LEG_TICKS` above `HOP_LEG_TICKS_MAX` 2190 at the settle ceiling (E-66)**: lint failure at content load (`HOP_LEG_TICKS_MAX`, L4). The upper bound is a pacing ceiling and the sum lint is the registry's ceiling on one leg's reserve cost (WM-17, WM-34, delegated ruling R6) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*
- **If `ring` is absent or less than 1 on a record (E-67)**: `ring_of` resolves to 1 and logs once (AT-6 order, AT E-61); the block rejects `ring < 1` at `post_restore` (never clamped).
- **If any `init_world` call rejects (E-68)**: the whole build fails with `SL_NEW_GAME_NO_BASELINE`; no partially built world exists. Nothing is ever built after `init_world`, so the world never grows a fifth body.
- **If a window is narrower than the lint's assumed field (E-69)**: the Pipeline's minimum window (1280 × 720, `k_auto` 2) bounds the ladder at 320 × 180, which is the field `reveal_window` uses; a smaller supported window would require re-running D.6.
- **If a console subject anchor is missing (ASK-L3 refused) (E-70)**: the anchor is one CONSOLE `subject_anchors` entry per console: `console_role` POD on the prop in each dock room, `console_role` DOCK on the home airlock's prop. A missing POD anchor means no console exists and the hop has no in-world trigger; the lints `POD_CONSOLE_MISSING` (a dock room without a POD anchor) and `DOCK_CONSOLE_MISSING` (the home airlock without a DOCK anchor, or a DOCK anchor anywhere else) fail the content build; both run as Authoring rows. *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* The design has no fallback hotkey because the commitment is a physical press (P1).

### J. Co-op hooks (Vertical Slice B; provisional)

- **If a second living player is on the derelict when the single seat departs (E-71)**: MVP is solo (one seat, DA-3); lint `COOP_HOP_UNDEFINED` fails any multi-seat MVP configuration. For VS-B, a body that hosts a living seat must not be set dormant; the semantics are Co-op Session's (OQ-WM-17) *(provisional — Co-op Session undesigned)*.
- **If two seats press `pod_hop` on the same tick (VS-B) (E-72)**: the first applies and the second is `IN_TRANSIT`; the block's `repl_hint` SHARED_SUMMARY already carries the machine to every client. A seat-keyed leg field would be cheaper than a retrofit and is flagged to Co-op.
- **If one client has the Chart Table open (a host-local pick) while another presses the console (E-73)**: the pick is host-local UI state and never affects the simulation; the press is the only write, and the Chart Table never assumes pause (KRN OQ 6).

## Dependencies

*Direction* reads from this system's point of view: "this depends on X" is an **upstream** edge, "X depends on this" is a **downstream** edge. *Type* is Hard (cannot be built or run without it) or Soft (degrades gracefully; the fallback is stated). *Status* is the state of the other system's GDD on disk at authoring (2026-10-26): **designed**, **in design** (draft on disk), or **undesigned-provisional** (no file; the contract is this GDD's and is marked provisional). *Bidirectional* records whether the other GDD already names this system (the project rule that dependencies are bidirectional): "yes (row quoted in Interactions)" or the repeat it owes.

Attention, Sanity and Horror read this system's rings and events but this system reads nothing from them (it reads Attention's `held_ring` only by name in a clarification, not as an input) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: the edges it does have to other systems are the **named write calls** (Graph M16 `withdraw_store` and M19 `set_section_dormant`; Body BM5, BM6 and BM7; Interaction `sync_section`; Emitter E1 at build and **E10 `set_dock_status`**; all inside one atomic apply) and the **named reads** (the Graph's `read_view` and `section()`, Layout's `body_of`, Body's `body_of_actor`, and the Controller's `loco_state`; nothing is read from Suit); it makes **no call into Attention** (the berth's one LOUD push is Interaction's, WM-26) and no `set_switch` call (Emitter rejects it for the dock glow); Suit and the Controller are read-only or constraint edges, not callers; Assembly reads `docked` and this system reads Layout's body of a section (never Assembly's state). **Implementation order:** Command & Simulation Layer, Save/Load, Graph, Layout, Body, Interaction, Derelict Authoring content, then this system; Chart Table after it.

## Tuning Knobs

*Reading the table.* ★ marks a **registry candidate**: a cross-system fact another GDD reads or constrains (the merge stage registers it from this final text). Every Web Map & Transit default is a `[PLACEHOLDER]` unless the row says "fixed"; all are tuned at the kill/continue playtest (at least three outside testers, a 30-plus-minute solo session, CON:483-485). Content-file values (per-derelict legs and berth phases) live in `assets/data/world/legs.json` and `mvp_world.json`, never in code (data-driven rule). **Fixed rules that are deliberately not knobs:** the lamp predicate is the chain predicate (lamp node OK or DEGRADED and battery `stock > 0`, ruled); the leg is not skippable; the divisor of `transit.step` is 1; the star topology; one berthed derelict at a time; the pod has one seat. A knob that would let a player skip, shorten or cheapen the crossing is not offered (guard rail 8). The merge registers the canonical list of the Formulas conventions (23 constants plus the formulas (the 21 of the first merge plus `EXTERIOR_CULL_RADIUS_PX` plus `CAMPAIGN_HOP_SHARE_PM` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; `EXTERIOR_CULL_FADE_PX` is local and not registered; ranges that interact are stated as derived inequalities, D.6 and D.14) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; Cross-References, registry row). ★ marks the subset other GDDs read. `LEG_FILAMENT_COUNT`, `LEG_RUNNING_LIGHT_RADIUS_PX`, `LEG_BED_FADE_TICKS`, `BERTH_CONTACT_V`, `BERTH_CONTACT_TICKS` and `HOP_LEG_TICKS_MIN/MAX` are deliberately local (presentation or fixed lint values).

### Fuel and the price of a crossing

| Knob | Default | Unit | Safe range | Too high | Too low | Interacts with |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

**Knob interaction notes.** (1) `HOP_FUEL_ML` [PLACEHOLDER], `HOP_FUEL_FLOOR_ML` [PLACEHOLDER] and `FUEL_UML_PER_W` move together: raising the burn rate shortens the hop's time-equivalent and raises `floor_min`. (2) `HOP_LEG_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER], the default leg speed and `LEG_STANDOFF_PX` [PLACEHOLDER] are tied by the reveal-window lint: a longer leg at the same speed lengthens the void and never the reveal. (3) `ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] is bounded below by the largest build and above by the player's patience; it never touches fairness. (4) The berth constants, `DERELICT_FAULT_COMPRESSION` and the weld channel length decide how costly an idle berth is. (5) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* Ranges that interact are stated as derived inequalities (D.6, D.14), and the corner sweep of AC-WM-125 evaluates the corners of the box, so a knob can no longer be moved inside its stated range into a failing lint.

## Visual/Audio Requirements

*Authored from the art-director's consult (stage 7; rule ids `VA-n`, asks `ASK-AD-n`, art-bible maintenance notes `AB-n`), reconciled with the rulings (R1 to R28, C1 to C14) and the Detailed Rules. Nothing here changes a simulation value. Where a visual need touches a number this GDD owns (D.6 reveal window), the art side may relax a lint threshold and never a simulation value. Every Web Map & Transit number is a [PLACEHOLDER] at every mention. The audio-director owns every sound, layer, fade and trigger (Adaptive Audio is designed, `adaptive-audio-and-tells.md`): this section gives visual-mood descriptors and the audio **class** of each event, never a sound design. `docs/engine-reference/godot/modules/` has no file for a world-model or transit domain; the rendering questions below are asks to the technical-artist, not designs.*

### 1. Art-director rulings made for this section

| # | Ruling | Why (art-bible rule that decides it) |
|---|---|---|
| VA-R1 | The `TRANSIT_HOLD` plate and the exterior void are the **same true black `#000000`**, not Void Steel. The hold-to-leg cut has no visible edge: the pod's pool simply appears in the black. | ART 2.5 "the void is black, not navy"; 8.11 flag 1 puts unlit *hull* one to two steps above true black, so the void must be the floor below it. A Void Steel plate would read as lit hull. |
| VA-R2 | The pod's running-light pool is **omnidirectional, steady, never modulated**. No cone. | 4.2 and 3.2: the four directional beam shapes are the human-identity channel; a fixture is a static housing, flicker reads as a deployable, a pulse reads as power or alarm. 2.5's "pointing outward" is satisfied by lamps mounted on the outer hull radiating away from it (CF-AD-4). |
| VA-R3 | The pod **coasts**: no thruster plume, exhaust, second emission or trail. | 2.5 "pod running lights are the sole source"; a plume implies thrust and steering (anti-pillar, PCI:217). |
| VA-R4 | The **berth shows no exterior**. The only visual witness of contact is a one-shot camera Settle, never a Tremor. | `interior_invariance` (PIPE C2); `wall_port` glass is Void Steel with one highlight (DA:505). The Tremor is the Tell's signature (2.7) and a dock must never be mistakable for Attention. |
| VA-R5 | **No pod dash at MVP.** The cabin interior is never rendered (WM-18), the pod owns no fuel node (WM-20) and no heading (no steering). ART 7.0's "heading reticle and fuel sight-glass" on the pod dash is withdrawn (AB-2). | P1: a fuel sight-glass needs a Graph node behind it or it is a lie by commission; the Graph's module-body sight-glass lives on the **home tank**. |
| VA-R6 | **CD-2 ruled as bible owner:** ART 2.9's "modifier on any state" is amended to exclude the **world content of 2.5** (exterior, pod sprite and pool, filaments, wreck silhouettes, residual lamp) and **all of 2.11**. The HUD shown during the hold and the leg stays in 2.9's scope and lie-capable (AB-1). | WM-35, DA:1188, ART 7.4. |
| VA-R7 | Leg filaments are a function of **ring only**, laid out from authored per-leg content, and **never** a function of Attention. `LEG_FILAMENT_COUNT` is a presentation knob, not an Attention readout. | P3 allows Attention to show as filaments at a cone edge during boarding; the leg is a quiet truth surface and WM-26 pushes no stimulus. A filament response in the leg would teach a false signal. |
| VA-R8 | The **docked-podcraft prop emits only its self-powered console status glow** (housings dark, no other `Light2D`, no other glow; the glow is the ordinary lit fixture of WM-8b, D.15). The prop renders iff `pod_at` equals that dock, and its glow is switched ON iff the same condition (E10, WM-8b); otherwise the empty cradle shows and nothing glows. The glow's luminance sits clear of the visible-unknown residual lamp's band and is not Tungsten (art-director owns the value; the constraint is D.15 c3). | A lit prop other than this glow would be an unauthored lit FIXTURE in a conserved-dark derelict (DA fixture rules; Principle 3, emission is exposure); the glow is the one authored exception, user ruling OQ-WM-39 (Option B), and it lights the console subject only; WM-33. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| VA-R9 | The residual lamp is drawn **lit** by the chain predicate (WM-18, OR-4) and **dark** otherwise; a dark lamp keeps its **visible housing** (seen where the pod's pool or the hull floor tint reaches it) so absence reads as a fact, not a bug. | P1 "a lamp found dark on return is the honest reading" (DA:452). |
| VA-R10 | **No docking animation.** The leg ends at `pod_arrive` with a fade to the hold; the dock is a camera cut through black to the avatar. | PIPE C10 ("no cinematic cuts within the leg"), anti-pillar (no docking QTE), guard rail 1. |

*(orchestrator ruling: the contact Settle is triggered by this GDD's `BERTHED` event. The art-director named Body's `BODY_DOCK_CONTACT` first with `BERTHED` as the fallback, but `BODY_DOCK_CONTACT` is raised by BM3 at the **weld** (BDY event table: "raised by BM3"), and the berth moves poses with BM6 and BM7 only, so `BERTHED` is the primary trigger. The weld's own contact keeps Assembly's VA-19 and `JOLT_PX` 0 untouched; the berth Settle is a separate, earlier profile requested of the Pipeline through ASK-AD-6, and is not a conflict with MSA VA-19.)*

### 2. Events to visual, sprite state and audio class

Owner column: **WM** = this system's presentation content (PROJECTOR / PRESENTATION, H10); **PIPE** = Pipeline-owned, cited only; **INT** = Interaction; **CT** = Chart Table (designed); **GR** = Graph; **AUD** = Adaptive Audio (designed). Class: **T** = truth channel, never wrapped by the Perception Filter or Sanity; **M** = meta register; **L** = lie-capable and not this system's. Audio class is a descriptor of the cue's role for the audio-director, never a sound.

| Event / state | Visual (sprite state) | Audio class | Owner | Class |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `pod_hop` Applied (departure) | World layer goes to the `TRANSIT_HOLD` plate: **flat `#000000`**, 120 ms fade in (PIPE:264). HUD stays up unchanged. No text, spinner, ring, percentage or tip. The departure room is never seen leaving; the empty cradle is what the dock shows on return (`pod_at` = IN_FLIGHT). | the departure cue (committed, dry) and the start of the leg bed over `LEG_BED_FADE_TICKS` | PIPE (plate), WM (nothing added) | T / HUD = L |
| LEG start (camera cut to the pod body, plate cut out) | First frame: pod sprite centred (`idle_*` facing), one Tungsten pool, nothing else lit. Filaments already crossing the pool (the leg begins mid-corridor). The **departure body falls astern**: on an OUT leg the home hull as a Void Steel mass (no warm point; HOME has zero residual lamps); on a BACK leg the derelict hull and its lamp by the same rules as arrival, receding. Ambient about 0 %. | leg bed (suspended, silent, vast) | WM | T |
| LEG, per tick | World content scrolls past the fixed pod at the leg velocity; **constant velocity, no ease in or out, no camera cuts** (PIPE C10). The pod faces the nearest of 8 compass directions to `v` (five drawn facings s, se, e, ne, n; the rest mirrored) and **never rotates mid-leg**. The pool, the lamp and the hull are the only luminous things; filaments exist only where the pool's mask touches them. | leg bed only; nothing keyed to a progress datum (there is none, WM-36) | WM | T |

It **enters by scroll from the field edge, never fades in, never flares, never pulses, never twinkles**. It is the highest local contrast in frame while in view (hero #4, ART 3.5): lamp core luminance above pool peak. | the one spike of the leg: a wrong warmth, no stinger | WM | T |

Where the pod's pool mask reaches it the hull takes lit Graphite (`#5C6670` class, value at most 40 % on large planes) in Foundry grammar: round ports (glass Void Steel, one highlight), big rivets at wide pitch, worn atlas. The lamp housing sits on the authored `exterior_anchor_px` hull run. | (resolve is the leg's single energy rise) | WM | T |
| Residual lamp dark (predicate false) | No emissive core, no halo. The 8x8 housing remains as hull (Void Steel tint; Graphite mid once inside the pool); the final frame shows it in the pool as a dead fixture. | none | WM | T |
| `pod_arrive` applies (LEG to ARRIVING) | 120 ms fade to the hold plate (`#000000`). Last frame is mid-approach: **no docking sequence, no flare, no braking**. | the leg bed ends (hush) | PIPE, WM | T |
| ARRIVING (the settle window, `ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER]) | Black. Nothing from this system is drawn. **No loading indicator of any kind** (guard rail 1). The HUD stays. | silence | PIPE | T / HUD = L |
| `pod_settle` applies (cut to the avatar) | Hard cut from black to the avatar on the authored `arrive_*` cell, **one tile clear of the docked-podcraft prop**, prop present because `pod_at` now equals this dock (WM-33). First frame follows the room's own lighting: derelict dock = depth-ring ambient floor plus the chest beam only (the 2.6 register begins); home airlock = home ambient, independent of ring (4.3). The prop's housings are dark (VA-R8). | the held breath of boarding (ART 2.6): a hush that arrives with the cut, not before it; no arrival fanfare | PIPE (cut), WM (prop, cradle) | T |
| `POD_ARRIVED`, `DERELICT_LOADED`, `DERELICT_UNLOADED`, `FIRST_BOARDED`, dormancy flips | **No visual.** Dormancy is not drawn; an unloaded body is simply not in the world. | none from this system (consumers may key a bed) | n/a | n/a |
| Departure from a dock (next visit to that dock room) | Empty cradle where the prop was. A prop drawn while `pod_at` says elsewhere is a **presentation defect** (WM-33). | none | WM | T |

Mood only: held, patient. | a sustained drive-hum and hull-strain bed across the whole of BERTHING, non-positional (the duration witness; the same drive burn reported to Attention as one LOUD push by Interaction, WM-26; the bed level and a berth-apply onset cue are Audio's to revisit, OQ-WM-46) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; layers, fades and triggers are the audio-director's | WM | T *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
